# Requirements checklist, part 2: S3 (engine hookup) and P4 (station resolution and scheduler, full)

Built on 5 October 2026 from every requirement line tagged `S3` or `P4` in `slice-blueprint.md`, `slice-plan-cross-cutting.md`, `slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md`, `slice-plan-portal.md` and `slice-plan-recognition.md` (645 lines). Lines tagged with another phase were checked too, and two were brought in (see "Phase placement and conflicts"). Built status comes from `status-built.md` (snapshot of commit `318fa2c`, 5 October 2026, about 05:15). That snapshot is stale: six later Step 2 commits are not in it (see part 1, "Status note", and gap 46). Review round 1 (5 October 2026) applied critic findings; moved or merged items are replaced by a `~~<id>~~ — Retired …` line naming where they went, and a retired id is never reused.

## How to read this file

- Line shape: `- [ ] **<PHASE>-<AREA>-<number>** Requirement. *Source:* ... *Verified by:* ...`
- `- [x]` is used only where `status-built.md` shows the behaviour built **and** names a test that proves it; the test follows *Evidence:*.
- `*(built, not yet tested)*` marks a requirement that already exists in committed code but has no test. *Built in:* names the file.
- `*(partly built)*` marks a requirement where a piece already exists from Step 1 or Step 2 (or from the Phase 0 engine), but the requirement as a whole is not met. *Built so far:* names that piece and its evidence. These stay unticked.
- *Source:* names the slice file and its section, with the underlying place in brackets. "plan" is `C:\Users\Luckson\.claude\plans\c-users-luckson-downloads-system-archit-virtual-steele.md`, "blueprint" is `system_architecture_blueprint.md`, "baseline" is `docs/security-baseline.md`, "conventions" is `docs/conventions.md`, and "global rules" are the "Error handling, logging & tests" rules in the user's CLAUDE.md.
- Names. The readable-names standing requirement (4 October 2026) is newer than the master plan (29 September 2026), so built names are used: schemas `recognition` and `fingerprints` (plan `recog`, `fp`); roles `lookup_api`, `lookup_admin_api`, `lookup_fingerprint_worker`; engine package `lookup_fingerprint` (plan `lookup_fp`); hash table `fingerprints.hashes_version_1` (version 2's `hashes_version_2` since 12 October 2026). Wire names that the plan writes in short form are quoted as "plan name" and must be renamed under S3-STRUCTURE-01.
- Numbering restarts in every area. Area codes: S3 uses ARCHITECTURE, ENGINE, STACK, WORKERS, INGEST, SHAREDAUDIO, QUEUE, MATCHER, INSTALLS, REQUEST, RATELIMIT, ADMISSION, RESOLUTION, SCHEDULE, RECORDING, RESPONSE, ERRORS, LOGGING, PRIVACY, DATABASE, STRUCTURE, SECURITY, TESTS. P4 uses TIMERULE, CLOCK, DEFERRED, IDEMPOTENCY, ADMISSION, RESOLUTION, FALLBACK, CHOOSER, LIMITS, CHOICE, ENGAGEMENTS, DATABASE, TESTS, DELIVERY, and, from the P4 design (11 October 2026), STATIONLIST, UPDATEGATE, INSTALLS, REPORTS and TOOLS.

## Phase placement and conflicts resolved

1. **Request fields in Step 3.** `slice-blueprint.md` §11 tags `idempotency_key`, `captured_at`, `sent_at` and `deferred` as P4; `slice-plan-recognition.md` §2 tags them S3. Chosen: **S3**. The plan says every request, live or deferred, carries them, and the built `recognition_events` table already requires an idempotency key and both times, so Step 3 must accept them. P4 adds what is done with them (claim, clock correction, deferred rules).
2. **Station hint.** `slice-blueprint.md` §11 tags the optional hint P4; `slice-plan-recognition.md` §2 tags it S3. Chosen: accepting and validating it is **S3** (S3-REQUEST-06); storing and using it is **P4** (P4-RESOLUTION-10, P4-RESOLUTION-06).
3. **"On air" judged at the capture time.** `slice-blueprint.md` §8 tags it S3; `slice-plan-recognition.md` §1 tags the period and window parts P4. Chosen: **S3** (S3-RESOLUTION-02), because the Step 3 resolve function already takes the capture time and the receipt time as separate inputs and evaluates the schedule at the capture time. P4 keeps grace, the candidate set, the receipt-time checks and the table-driven split suite.
4. **Several stations owning one audio asset.** `slice-blueprint.md` §13 tags it P4; `slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md` §4 (D7) tags it S3. Chosen: **S3** (S3-SHAREDAUDIO-03), because the Step 3 shared-upload test proves it.
5. **k6 load test at the target rate.** `slice-blueprint.md` §7 tags it P8; `slice-plan-recognition.md` §18 tags it P4 (master Phase 3 "Verify"). Chosen: **P4** (P4-TESTS-06), the earliest phase; the home is flagged as uncertain in the gaps.
6. **Idempotency-key clean-up.** `slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md` §1 tags it P4; `slice-plan-cross-cutting.md` §20 tags the same rule ALL. Chosen: **P4** (P4-IDEMPOTENCY-19), with both sources; it was brought in from the ALL line.
7. **Device-integrity verdict.** `slice-plan-cross-cutting.md` §28 tags reading it at install registration S3; `slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md` §9 tags the verdict column P8. Chosen (review round 1): reading the verdict and the nullable column that stores it are both **S3** (S3-INSTALLS-10, -11, -12), because the plan's data model lists "optional integrity verdict" on the installs table and O10 reads it "at install registration"; P8-INSTALLS-01 is retired.
8. **Grace on both sides.** The plan says "± grace"; the wireframes say "10 minutes after each slot" (`slice-blueprint.md` gap 23). Chosen: **the plan** (both sides), because the plan's decisions win over other sources.
9. **The API may not mark an ad READY.** `slice-plan-recognition.md` gap 27 says the built grants let the API set `ads.status` directly. `status-built.md` (newer, 5 October 05:15) shows the Step 2 status guard that refuses READY and NEEDS_REVIEW from the API. Chosen: **status-built.md**; recorded as partly built under S3-INGEST-15.
10. **The API role cannot write an ad's audio-asset link.** `slice-plan-recognition.md` §14 tags it S3, but it is an S1 requirement in `slice-blueprint.md` §10 and `slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md` §9, and it is already built and tested in Step 1. Chosen: that half stays in the S1 checklist; S3-DATABASE-07 keeps only "only the ingest function sets the link".
11. **Engine requirements.** The engine core was built as a Phase 0 tool (coded S1 in `status-built.md`), but `slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md` codes engine requirements S3 ("where the engine is hooked into the product"). Kept in **S3**, ticked where `status-built.md` shows an engine test.
12. **Blueprint items replaced by the plan** (marked "supersedes blueprint §n" on each item): Dejavu by the in-house engine (D9); the actor system by queues and a Python worker (D1); `MATCH_FOUND`, `rec_7890abc`, `media.tenant_id`, `media.brand_name` and `ui_schema.header_banner` by the plan's response (G9); "the brand's default landing card" by the client's default card (G5, O3); `recognition_logs` by `recognition_events` (G12); "current timestamp" by the capture time (D5).
13. **Queue vocabulary.** The master plan says `match` (`match.py`, a "matcher heartbeat"); the thin slice, which is the approved Step 3 text, names the job and queue `recognize`. Chosen: queue `recognize`; worker file names are left open (gaps).
14. **Who may read the fingerprint schema (review round 1).** G3 says the `fingerprints` schema is "readable only by the worker role"; O6 and plan §B say super admins see the link between stations' ads and review partial overlaps with an A/B audio preview. Chosen: `lookup_api` never reaches the schema; `lookup_admin_api` reaches overlap findings and preview keys only through one narrow path (S3-SHAREDAUDIO-01, P5A-OVERLAP-REVIEW-04). Which path (a SECURITY DEFINER function or a `SELECT` grant on the overlap table only) awaits the user's decision and must be recorded in ADR-002 (gap 48).
15. **Public station reference (review round 1).** G9 removes the leaked `tenant_id`, but the choice endpoint, the engagements endpoint and the station hint all take a station identifier from the app. Chosen: the response carries one public station reference per station (S3-RESPONSE-17), and S3-RESPONSE-14 forbids only `tenant_id` and internal ids. Whether that reference is the station id or an opaque token is still gap 6.
16. **The P4 gate is split (review round 1).** The execution order builds "engagements + caps" after "offline capture/outbox/History/Settings" (P6 work), so P4 cannot close in one gate. Chosen: P4-RESOLUTION-16 covers resolution, grace, hint, the chooser response, the choice endpoint, idempotency, the deferred rules and the default-card fallback; P4-RESOLUTION-17 covers engagement limits and the engagements endpoint after the P6 outbox. App behaviour that was filed here (P4-CHOOSER-05, -06) is retired in favour of P6-CHOOSER-09 and P6-TESTS-07.

---

## S3 — Step 3: engine hookup (Python ingest and recognize workers, installs, `POST /recognize`, basic resolve)

### Architecture

- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-01** Work passes between the API and the fingerprint engine through BullMQ queues on Valkey: the Node API enqueues and Python workers process, with one shared queue per job type (the Python `bullmq` package is the official port that runs the same Lua scripts) (supersedes blueprint §2 and §4, "Actor System Engine" and "wrapping the Python Dejavu library"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (blueprint §2 line 18; plan D1); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D1), §5 (Verified facts, BullMQ), §7 (actor mapping). *Verified by:* cross-language contract test on Valkey 8: a job added by Node on a queue name is completed by the Python worker on the same queue name. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the queue contract: a job Node adds is answered by Python in the contract’s shape, and one past its deadline is dropped undecoded ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it.
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-02** The fingerprint engine runs only in Python workers, and Python is used only where the engine needs it: the Python service has no sign-in, portal or station-data endpoints. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D1). *Verified by:* code review of `services/fingerprinter`: no HTTP endpoint for sign-in, portal or station data. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_the_service_runs_no_http_server.
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-03** Fingerprinting never runs inside the API process or on the request path: `apps/api` holds no audio-decoding or fingerprint code, it hands the clip to a worker through the `recognize` queue and waits, and its event loop stays responsive while recognitions are queued (keeps blueprint §4). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 line 75); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D1), §6 (Holds). *Verified by:* code and dependency check of `apps/api`; load test: the API keeps answering other routes while recognitions are queued. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S3-ARCHITECTURE-03: the API decodes no audio and computes no fingerprints: it only queues clips for the workers ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > while clips wait for a worker the API keeps answering other routes at once: nothing is decoded on its event loop.
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-04** The blueprint's Recognition Supervisor actor is the API's `RecognitionService`, which owns admission, circuit breaking, enqueueing and waiting (supersedes blueprint §4 "Recognition Supervisor Actor"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 lines 82–86, 109); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (actor mapping). *Verified by:* unit tests of `RecognitionService`, one per duty. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey.
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-05** Schedule evaluation runs inside the request as SQL (the resolve function) plus TypeScript policy in the API; there is no scheduler queue, worker or service (supersedes blueprint §4 "Campaign Scheduler Actor"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 lines 99–103, 113); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (actor mapping). *Verified by:* code check: after the worker result the recognize path makes no further queue hop; resolution test shows the decision made inside the request. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > judges "on air" at the moment the clip was heard, in the station’s time zone, not when it arrived ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > returns the station, its card and only what a listener may see, in window inside the period and a window ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: resolveStation: candidates, then the schedule, then the hint, then limits > P4-RESOLUTION-05: two owners, one on air: the one on air, SCHEDULE_UNIQUE, both weighed and only it on air. Re-pointed on 11 October 2026: P4 Piece 2 replaced choose-station.ts by resolve-station.ts, whose rules moved to their own file.
- [ ] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-06** `recognize` and `ingest` are separate queues served by separate worker pools, so bulk ingestion can never starve recognition (keeps blueprint §4 "decouples ingestion from heavy recognition computation"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; §Target architecture); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7. *Verified by:* integration and load test: with the `ingest` queue full of slow jobs, a recognize job is still picked up and answered inside its deadline, and recognition latency holds while a batch of ads is ingested. *Not ticked (built, not yet tested):* separate queues and pools are built; fingerprint-workers.test.ts 'a failed download is tried again after a delay … while clips keep being answered' shows one slow ingest job, not a full ingest queue, and the latency-under-batch half is the P4 load test (P4-TESTS-06).
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-07** Any number of stateless worker processes may consume the `recognize` queue at once, and each job is handled by exactly one of them (supersedes blueprint §4 "load balancing" and "allocates an available worker actor from the pool"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 lines 84, 91–92, 109, 111). *Verified by:* integration test with two workers: N jobs give N results and no job is processed twice. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > two recognize workers share the queue: each clip is answered once, by one of them.
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-08** The clip arrives as one multipart upload of the whole recording, not as a stream (supersedes blueprint §4 "audio stream buffer"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 line 83; plan §Target architecture "one POST"). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test posts a complete file part and gets the result in the same response. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [ ] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-09** The listener app uploads audio and the backend computes the fingerprint; the app never computes or sends fingerprints (kept from blueprint §1 and §8). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §1 (blueprint §1 line 9, §8 line 281). *Verified by:* contract test: the recognize request schema has an `audio` part and no hash field; code search: the app source contains no fingerprint code. *Not ticked (partly built):* server half proven (recognition-schemas.test.ts 'carries the clip itself, never fingerprint hashes: the app does no fingerprinting'; recognize-route.test.ts refuses a fingerprint_hashes field); the "app source contains no fingerprint code" half needs the listener app (Step 4).
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-10** No listener-facing endpoint returns a list of ads (D8). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D8). *Verified by:* API route inventory test: the only listener routes are installs, recognize, choice, engagements, the stations list and client errors. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > only health, sign-in, refresh, sign-out, station sign-up and its email link, an invitation link, client errors and the listener app’s routes (installs, recognize, a station choice, a button tap, a report, the station list) are open without signing in, and none lists ads.
- [x] **S3-ARCHITECTURE-11** No listener-facing credential can create, change or delete a station's ads, action cards, campaigns or schedules (kept from blueprint §3.1 "read-only payload consumption"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §4 (blueprint §3.1 line 57). *Verified by:* API test: an install token sent to every portal write route is answered 401 or 403. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > a listener install token opens no portal route, whether sent as a bearer token or as the portal’s session cookie ; apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > only health, sign-in, refresh, sign-out, station sign-up and its email link, an invitation link, client errors and the listener app’s routes (installs, recognize, a station choice, a button tap, a report, the station list) are open without signing in, and none lists ads.

### Engine

- [x] **S3-ENGINE-01** The engine is the in-house Python package `lookup_fingerprint` (plan name `lookup_fp`) in `services/fingerprinter`, implementing the landmark algorithm on spectral peaks from the Fast Fourier Transform (supersedes blueprint §2 and §4 "Dejavu"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (blueprint §2 line 17; plan D9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D9). *Verified by:* pytest: an excerpt of a reference recording is matched to that reference. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_phone_clip_matches_right_ad_and_offset.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-02** The engine provides its four core parts (fingerprinting, alignment, the database index and the decoder) as its own modernised modules, each with unit tests (D9 replaces "vendor Dejavu's files"). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §5 (Verified facts, Dejavu unmaintained; D9). *Verified by:* repository check: a module for each part exists and each has unit tests. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_decodes_phone_aac_to_canonical_rate ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_fingerprint_is_deterministic ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_align.py :: test_ranks_each_asset_by_the_landmarks_agreeing_on_its_best_offset ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_postgres_lookup_equals_memory_lookup.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-03** The Dejavu package is never a dependency and no Dejavu source file is copied into the repository. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D9; memory/look-up-project-decisions.md); also stated for every phase in slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 and §5 (tagged ALL). *Verified by:* dependency check: `services/fingerprinter/pyproject.toml` lists no Dejavu package; code search finds no Dejavu file. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_no_other_engine_is_a_dependency_or_imported ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_no_dejavu_source_is_copied_in.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-04** SoundFingerprinting, Olaf and audfprint are benchmark baselines only and never runtime dependencies. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D9); also slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (tagged ALL). *Verified by:* dependency check of the worker's runtime requirements. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_no_other_engine_is_a_dependency_or_imported.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-05** The engine does not use the deprecated `scipy.ndimage.filters` module. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §5 (plan §Verified facts, line 245). *Verified by:* code scan finds no import of `scipy.ndimage.filters`; the test suite runs with deprecation warnings from that module treated as errors. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_the_deprecated_scipy_filters_module_is_never_used.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-06** Every audio input, reference ads and listener clips alike, is resampled to one canonical sample rate before fingerprinting; audio is never fingerprinted at its file's native rate. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D9, Verified facts sample-rate trap); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D9), §5 (sample-rate trap). *Verified by:* engine test: a 16 kilohertz phone-like clip matches a 44.1 or 48 kilohertz reference of the same audio, and skipping the resampling breaks matching. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_skipping_resampling_breaks_matching ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_decodes_phone_aac_to_canonical_rate.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-07** Hashes are exact packed integers (no truncated or text hashes) stored in the `bigint` column `fingerprints.hashes_version_1.hash` (supersedes blueprint §6 `hash BYTEA`; since fingerprint version 2, 12 October 2026, `fingerprints.hashes_version_2.hash`, the same type, checked by `test_postgres_index.py` :: test_the_hash_column_is_bigint_in_every_version_and_the_index_uses_the_current_one). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (blueprint §6 line 200; plan D9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D9). *Verified by:* engine unit test: packing then unpacking a landmark returns the same frequency pair and time difference; database test: the column type is `bigint`. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_landmark_hashes_unpack_to_exactly_what_was_packed ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_every_hash_fits_the_bigint_column_with_room_for_the_offset ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_the_hash_column_is_bigint_in_every_version_and_the_index_uses_the_current_one.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-08** The hash lookup is vectorised: one query per clip of the form `hash = ANY($1)` against `fingerprints.hashes_version_1` (now the current version's table (`hash_table_name()`; `fingerprints.hashes_version_2` since 12 October 2026)), with the hashes passed as a bound parameter, never one query per hash and never with hash values written into the SQL text. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D9 "vectorised lookup"); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D9); slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7, line 504). *Verified by:* engine or worker test against Postgres with a statement counter: one lookup statement per clip, with the hashes as a parameter. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_lookup_is_one_query_with_the_hashes_as_a_bound_parameter.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-09** Peak picking uses an amplitude floor relative to the clip's own level, not an absolute level, so quiet phone recordings still produce peaks. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D9). *Verified by:* engine test: the same clip at much lower gain yields a comparable number of hashes and still matches. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_a_much_quieter_recording_gives_a_comparable_fingerprint_and_still_matches.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-10** A match needs at least T aligned hashes; below that threshold the answer is no match. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7, line 505); slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D9, G14). *Verified by:* engine test with T = 7: 6 aligned hashes (T − 1) give NO_MATCH, and 30 aligned hashes with no runner-up give MATCH. The exact-T case (7 aligned with no runner-up gives MATCH) is not yet a test case and should be added. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_decide_threshold_and_margin[<five cases>]. Verification reworded in review round 1 to the cases the test has.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-11** A match also needs the margin between the best and the second-best candidate to be at least R (top-2 margin); when two assets share audio (a jingle) the answer is AMBIGUOUS, never a confident wrong brand. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; G14); slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D9), §6 (G14). *Verified by:* engine test: two references share a jingle; a clip of the shared part has enough aligned hashes but a margin below R and is not a match. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_decide_threshold_and_margin[<five cases>] ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_shared_audio_is_ambiguous_not_a_wrong_brand.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-12** The thresholds T and R are named configuration values of the engine, not constants inside the worker. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; §Phases row 3 Build "thresholds"). *Verified by:* worker test: changing the configured thresholds changes the decision for a borderline fixture. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_the_match_thresholds_are_settings_and_decide_the_outcome ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_the_lookup_is_given_800_milliseconds_and_a_cancelled_lookup_is_lookup_timeout.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-13** The engine sits behind one narrow interface, `recognize(clip) → match`, so it can be swapped without changing the API, the queue contract or the app (D10). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (plan D10); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D10); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §21 (plan D10; memory/look-up-project-decisions.md). *Verified by:* worker test: replacing the engine with a stub that implements the same interface leaves the recognize worker and the API tests passing unchanged. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_the_engine_sits_behind_one_method_so_a_stand_in_engine_works ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_a_clip_is_matched_through_the_one_entry_point.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-14** The engine never returns MATCH on the synthetic negative set (audio that is not in the index). The false-positive limit of at most 0.5 percent against 200 or more real negative clips is measured at the pilot (P8-FIELD-TEST-05, P8-FIELD-TEST-18; D10). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G14; plan §Phases row 0). *Verified by:* engine test and benchmark gate: unrelated synthetic clips never return MATCH, and the gate's false-positive check passes on the synthetic corpus. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_matching.py :: test_unrelated_audio_is_rejected ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_bench.py :: test_gate_passes_on_clean_corpus. Narrowed in review round 1: 14 synthetic negatives cannot show a 0.5 percent bound.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-15** The benchmark measures matching latency stage by stage (decode, fingerprint, lookup, align) with percentiles, so the 2.5-second recognition budget can be checked. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §5 (plan §Verified facts, latency). *Verified by:* benchmark run: the report contains a per-stage timing table with median and 95th percentile. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_bench.py :: test_gate_passes_on_clean_corpus.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-16** The final type of the hash column is confirmed from benchmark data before the engine is hooked up, and the decision is written down. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (plan §Data model row fp.fingerprints_v1 "final type decided in Phase 0"). *Verified by:* document check: ADR-001 or the Step 3 design note states the chosen type and the benchmark figures behind it. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S3-ENGINE-16: ADR-001 records the hash column type and the benchmark figure it rests on.
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-17** Where the engine derives from Dejavu's published algorithm, the repository carries the MIT attribution. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §8 (plan §Repo layout; §Verified facts). *Verified by:* file check: an attribution notice naming Dejavu and its MIT licence is in the engine package (see gaps: D9 says no file is copied). *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_the_dejavu_credit_is_kept.
- [ ] **S3-ENGINE-18** Until real ads are available, self-made test ads may be used to exercise the engine (optional, D10); any that are used are recorded in the corpus manifest as self-made. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D10). *Verified by:* manual check of the corpus manifest. *Not ticked (deferred):* optional (D10): on 12 October 2026 the real-capture corpus (ADR-001, "Real captures, October 2026") used 4 self-made spoken pieces (Windows voices, made-up businesses), marked "self-made" in the notes column of spike/corpus/real-2026-10/manifest.csv, which is kept on the development machine, not in git; bench/manifest.py still has no dedicated self-made column (manual check).
- [x] **S3-ENGINE-19** Clip fingerprints are matched against reference hashes indexed in PostgreSQL (kept from blueprint §1 and §2). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §1 (blueprint §1 line 9, §2 line 19; plan Step 3). *Verified by:* pytest `tests/test_postgres_index.py` with a real database, plus the API end-to-end test in which an uploaded fixture ad is found through the hash index. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_postgres_lookup_equals_memory_lookup ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.

- [ ] **S3-STACK-01** `@nestjs/bullmq` is pinned at exactly 12.0.0. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §2 (plan §Pinned stack, API row). *Verified by:* dependency check of `apps/api/package.json` and the lock file. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D1: BullMQ is used directly from infrastructure/job-queues; @nestjs/bullmq is not a dependency.
- [x] **S3-STACK-02** Node `bullmq` is pinned at exactly 6.3.11. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §2 (plan §Pinned stack, API row). *Verified by:* dependency check of `apps/api/package.json` and the lock file. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S3-STACK-02, S3-STACK-04: the queue, circuit-breaker and multipart libraries are pinned exactly, in the manifest and the lock file.
- [x] **S3-STACK-03** Python `bullmq` is pinned at exactly 3.3.0, the version that pairs with Node `bullmq` 6 (same Lua scripts). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §2 (plan §Pinned stack, Worker row); slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers). *Verified by:* dependency check of the worker's requirements or lock file; the cross-language contract test passes with it. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_the_new_dependencies_are_pinned_exactly ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the queue contract: a job Node adds is answered by Python in the contract’s shape, and one past its deadline is dropped undecoded.
- [x] **S3-STACK-04** The circuit breaker is the `opossum` library, pinned to an exact version and wrapping the enqueue-and-wait call. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Step 3 API, line 164). *Verified by:* dependency check of `apps/api/package.json`; code review of the wrapped call. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S3-STACK-02, S3-STACK-04: the queue, circuit-breaker and multipart libraries are pinned exactly, in the manifest and the lock file ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy.

- [ ] **S3-STACK-06** The Python service lives in `services/fingerprinter`, targets Python 3.12 and is managed with uv. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §8 (plan §Repo layout). *Verified by:* `uv sync` then `pytest` succeed from a clean checkout on Python 3.12 (see gaps: the README installs with venv and pip). *Not ticked (deferred):* S3-D26: no uv.lock yet and the README/test harness install with venv and pip; the move to uv comes with the worker image, which the user deferred on 8 October 2026 to the deploy phase.
- [ ] **S3-STACK-07** Each new dependency of this step (`opossum`, Python `bullmq`, `structlog`, any load-test tool) is checked against the pinned stack and the supported runtimes (Node 24; Python 3.12 in Docker and 3.14 locally) before it is added, pinned to an exact version, and no supported version is raised to fit it. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (global rules "Tests"; plan §Pinned stack). *Verified by:* review: each new dependency has an exact pin and installs on Node 24 and on both Python versions. *Not ticked (deferred):* every new dependency is an exact pin (busboy 1.6.0, opossum 10.0.0, bullmq 6.3.11; Python bullmq 3.3.0, structlog 26.1.0; tested by source-scan and test_engine_rules.py) and installs on Node 24 and Python 3.14; installing on Python 3.12 waits for the worker image (S3-D26), which the user deferred on 8 October 2026 to the deploy phase.

### Workers (shared by ingest and recognize)

- [ ] **S3-WORKERS-01** The queue workers live in `services/fingerprinter/workers/`, one module for ingesting and one for recognizing (plan names `ingest.py` and `match.py`), each with its own tests, and they are what the worker image starts. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; §Repo layout); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §8 (plan §Repo layout). *Verified by:* structure check: both modules exist, each has tests, and the image's commands start them. *Not ticked (deferred):* workers/ingest.py and workers/recognize.py exist with tests (test_workers.py, test_ingest_store.py), but the image still starts bench.run, not the workers (S3-D26).
- [x] **S3-WORKERS-02** The workers call the existing engine package `lookup_fingerprint` for decoding, fingerprinting, lookup and alignment, and contain no second copy of that logic. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; D10); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §2 (plan §Pinned stack, Worker row). *Verified by:* code review plus an import scan: the worker modules import from `lookup_fingerprint` and contain no fingerprint or alignment maths of their own. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_the_workers_hold_no_fingerprint_maths_of_their_own.
- [x] **S3-WORKERS-03** Each running worker writes a heartbeat key with an expiry to the key-value store and keeps refreshing it, so the key disappears soon after the worker stops. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; §Recognition flow step 4); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §23 (plan §Phases row 1); slice-blueprint.md §7 (replaces blueprint §4 "manages worker lifecycle"). *Verified by:* integration test: the key exists while the worker runs and is gone within its expiry after the worker is killed. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_the_heartbeat_key_is_kept_fresh_with_an_expiry_and_removed_on_stop ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_killed_workers_heartbeat_expires_by_itself.
- [x] **S3-WORKERS-04** Processor-heavy work (decoding and fingerprinting) runs in a process pool in both workers, so one clip does not block the worker's event loop, its queue handling or its heartbeat. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8, §14 (plan §Step 3 workers, line 159); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture). *Verified by:* worker test: while a long fingerprint task runs, the heartbeat key keeps being refreshed and a second job starts; two jobs submitted together run in different processes. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_two_jobs_run_in_different_processes_while_the_event_loop_stays_free ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_match_comes_back_in_the_contracts_shape_and_the_clip_is_capped ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_new_recording_is_fingerprinted_and_stored.
- [ ] **S3-WORKERS-05** The worker ships as one Docker image whose entrypoint starts a queue worker (not only the benchmark), with one command for recognizing and one for ingesting. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers); slice-blueprint.md §7 (plan §Render topology); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Render topology). *Verified by:* build the image and run each command against the compose services: each registers on its own queue, the heartbeat key appears and a test job is processed. *Not ticked (deferred):* S3-D26: services/fingerprinter/Dockerfile's entrypoint runs bench.run and does not copy workers/; one image with two worker commands needs the multi-stage uv build, which the user deferred on 8 October 2026 to the deploy phase.
- [ ] **S3-WORKERS-06** The worker image runs as a non-root user. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, line 88); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §3. *Verified by:* image check: `docker run … id -u` does not print 0. *Not ticked (deferred):* the current Dockerfile runs as uid 10001 (non-root), but the image check (docker run … id -u) needs the S3-D26 image build.
- [ ] **S3-WORKERS-07** The worker image is multi-stage, with a build stage and a runtime stage. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §3 (plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Reuse). *Verified by:* Dockerfile check: separate build and runtime stages. *Not ticked (deferred):* S3-D26: the Dockerfile is single-stage; the multi-stage build was deferred by the user on 8 October 2026 to the deploy phase.

### Ingest worker

- [x] **S3-INGEST-01** A completed upload is placed on the BullMQ queue named `ingest` and processed by the Python worker, which brings a good file to READY. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (plan §Target architecture diagram). *Verified by:* end-to-end test: `POST /ads/:id/complete` adds one job to `ingest`, and the worker brings the ad to READY. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-02** Each `ingest` job carries a short-lived presigned GET URL for the uploaded file, created by the API when it enqueues the job. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers, line 152; baseline §4); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §11 (baseline §4 row 3). *Verified by:* integration test: the job's data holds a URL that downloads the object now and is refused after its expiry. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ingest-sweep.test.ts :: the ingest sweep > gives an ad that has waited a job with a short-lived download address and no credentials, once ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-03** The worker holds no storage credentials: no object-storage access key or secret is in its environment, configuration or image. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; baseline §4); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §11 (baseline §4 row 3, "Planned, step 3"). *Verified by:* configuration test: the worker starts and ingests with no storage credential variables set, and its configuration schema has no storage credential field. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_bad_settings_stop_the_worker_naming_the_setting_never_its_value ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the fingerprint jobs > an ingest job carries a download address, never storage credentials.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-04** Before decoding a file fully, the worker inspects its container and audio stream (the plan's "ffprobe" step); a file with no audio stream or in a container outside the allow-list ends FAILED with the matching code. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Build). *Verified by:* worker test: a video-only file and a file in an unlisted container each end FAILED with the matching code. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_file_with_no_audio_stream_is_refused_as_such ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_file_that_is_not_audio_is_refused_as_unreadable ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_container_whitelist ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_the_demuxer_comes_from_the_files_own_first_bytes_never_a_guess.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-05** The worker decodes the upload to the canonical form (one channel, the canonical sample rate) before measuring or fingerprinting it. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Build "canonical decode"). *Verified by:* worker test: stereo 44.1 kilohertz and mono 16 kilohertz versions of one recording both ingest, and both are matched by the same clip. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_stereo_44_kilohertz_and_mono_16_kilohertz_copies_give_the_same_canonical_audio.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-06** The ingest worker fingerprints with the same canonical sample rate and engine parameters as the recognize worker. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; §Verified facts, sample-rate trap). *Verified by:* integration test: an ad ingested from a 44.1 kilohertz file is matched by a 16 kilohertz clip of it. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_canonical_rate_is_the_same_for_references_and_clips.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-07** An upload shorter than 5 seconds is refused: the ad ends FAILED with a duration code and nothing is added to the fingerprint store. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers, line 153; §Phases row 2); slice-blueprint.md §10. *Verified by:* worker test: a 4.9-second file ends FAILED with a duration code and zero hash rows added; a 5.0-second file is accepted. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_ad_must_be_5_to_120_seconds_long ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_unusable_file_is_refused_with_its_reason ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > fingerprints.record_ad_ingest_result > marks a refused file FAILED with its reason, and accepts only the reasons it knows ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_corrupt_file_fails_the_ad_with_a_known_reason_and_stores_nothing.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-08** An upload longer than 120 seconds is refused: the ad ends FAILED with a duration code and nothing is added to the fingerprint store. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers, line 153; §Phases row 2); slice-blueprint.md §10. *Verified by:* worker test: a 120.1-second file ends FAILED with a duration code and zero hash rows added; a 120.0-second file is accepted. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_ad_must_be_5_to_120_seconds_long ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_the_length_is_checked_before_any_fingerprinting ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_corrupt_file_fails_the_ad_with_a_known_reason_and_stores_nothing.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-09** The duration check runs on the decoded audio, before fingerprinting. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Build). *Verified by:* worker test: for a too-long file the fingerprint function is never called. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_the_length_is_checked_before_any_fingerprinting.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-10** Audio that is an exact copy of an existing asset (same SHA-256) is fingerprinted only once: the new ad is linked to the existing asset, no audio asset row and no hash rows are added, and both ads end READY (D7: one shared asset, no duplicate rows in the hash index). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14, §17 (plan §Step 3 workers; §Phases row 2; D7); slice-blueprint.md §10 (plan D7, G8); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D7), §6 (G14). *Verified by:* integration test: ingest the same file for two ads; one `audio_assets` row exists, the row count of the current version's table (`hash_table_name()`; `fingerprints.hashes_version_2` since 12 October 2026) is unchanged by the second ingest, and both ads are READY. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_the_same_file_again_links_to_the_first_recording_and_adds_no_hashes ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the same ad uploaded by a second station links to the same recording: no new fingerprints, READY, and nothing shows it is shared.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-11** Duplicate handling runs before fingerprinting, so no hash is written before the SHA-256 check has been made. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Build); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G8). *Verified by:* integration test: the second ingest of an identical file writes no hash rows, calls no fingerprint function, and links its ad to the first asset. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_exact_copy_is_linked_without_decoding_or_fingerprinting ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_the_same_file_again_links_to_the_first_recording_and_adds_no_hashes.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-12** New audio is stored with `PostgresIndex.add`, which writes all of an asset's hashes with COPY in one transaction, so a failed or interrupted ingest leaves no partial set of hashes. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14, §17 (plan §Step 3 workers, line 154; §Phases row 2 "COPY in one transaction"). *Verified by:* worker test: abort the write half-way; the asset has zero hash rows; a retry writes exactly one full set. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_an_index_can_write_inside_its_callers_transaction ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_new_recording_for_an_ad_that_stopped_waiting_leaves_nothing_behind.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-13** After storing, the worker calls `fingerprints.mark_ingested` (plan name `fp.mark_ingested`), which sets the ad to READY with its audio-asset link and duration filled. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; §Step 1 migration); slice-blueprint.md §10. *Verified by:* integration test: after a good ingest the ad's status is READY, its audio-asset link is set and its duration is filled. *Built as:* S3-D6: the function is fingerprints.record_ad_ingest_result, not fp.mark_ingested. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > fingerprints.record_ad_ingest_result > makes a waiting ad READY with its asset, duration and SHA-256, and records it in the audit trail ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_new_recording_is_stored_with_all_its_hashes_and_the_ad_becomes_ready.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-14** On failure the worker calls the same function with an error code, which sets the ad to FAILED and stores in `processing_error_code` a short code from a known list, never exception text, a stack, a file path or library text. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; §Data model row ads); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (row ads; global rules "Two layers"). *Verified by:* integration test with a corrupt file: status FAILED and a code from the list, with no stack, path or library wording in the stored value. *Built as:* S3-D6, S3-D7: same function, reasons from UPLOAD_REFUSAL_REASONS. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > fingerprints.record_ad_ingest_result > marks a refused file FAILED with its reason, and accepts only the reasons it knows ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a corrupt file ends FAILED with a reason the station can read, and stores no recording ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_corrupt_file_fails_the_ad_with_a_known_reason_and_stores_nothing.
- [ ] **S3-INGEST-15** An ad's processing status is mirrored from its audio asset by a SECURITY DEFINER function: neither the worker role nor the API role updates the ad's processing fields by direct statement, and the API never works the status out from the fingerprint store. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2; §Data model row ads); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (row ads; baseline §8). *Verified by:* database test: the worker can change the status only through the function; the API role reads the new status from `app.ads` with no access to `fingerprints`. *Not ticked (built differently):* conflict 9: the worker changes an ad only through fingerprints.record_ad_ingest_result and cannot read or update app.ads, and since Step 3 the API role can no longer write the upload checksum (engine-hookup-database tests); but the API role still sets PROCESSING and FAILED with the refusal reason itself when it checks an upload at complete (Step 2), under the status guard that refuses READY and NEEDS_REVIEW from it. Moving that into a definer function as well is a change for the user to decide.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-16** An ad's status moves from AWAITING_UPLOAD to PROCESSING, and from PROCESSING to READY, NEEDS_REVIEW or FAILED. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (plan §Data model row ads). *Verified by:* end-to-end test: create gives AWAITING_UPLOAD, complete gives PROCESSING, a good file ends READY and a corrupt file ends FAILED. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > creating an ad and its upload URL > signs exactly Content-Type and Content-Length for 10 minutes, with no checksum parameters, and audits it ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > accepts a real WAV file, moves the ad to processing, audits it, and is safe to repeat ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a corrupt file ends FAILED with a reason the station can read, and stores no recording.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-17** After an upload is processed, the ad holds the SHA-256 of the uploaded bytes. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (plan §Data model row ads). *Verified by:* end-to-end test: after processing, the ad's upload checksum (`upload_content_sha256`) equals the SHA-256 of the uploaded file. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_new_recording_is_stored_with_all_its_hashes_and_the_ad_becomes_ready ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > fingerprints.record_ad_ingest_result > makes a waiting ad READY with its asset, duration and SHA-256, and records it in the audit trail.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-18** For each ad the system records the audio's storage location, processing status, duration and checksum, all of which the blueprint lacked (G8). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G8). *Verified by:* database test after ingest: the ad row has an object key, a status, a duration in milliseconds and a SHA-256. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_new_recording_is_stored_with_all_its_hashes_and_the_ad_becomes_ready ; apps/api/test/ads-and-uploads.test.ts :: ads and direct audio uploads > completing an upload > a file that is not really audio is refused, deleted from storage, and the ad marked FAILED with the reason.
- [ ] **S3-INGEST-19** Each audio asset records the storage key of its canonical (decoded, resampled) audio (plan name `canonical_object_key`). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (plan §Data model row fp.audio_assets). *Verified by:* schema test: the column exists; ingest test: after ingest the key is set and the object exists in storage (see gaps: the built table has no such column). *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D9: no canonical copy is stored and audio_assets has no canonical key column; the original upload is kept and decoded again at re-indexing (P8).
- [ ] **S3-INGEST-20** Each audio asset records its fingerprinting status (plan name `fp_status`) from a fixed list of values. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (plan §Data model row fp.audio_assets). *Verified by:* schema test: the column exists with a fixed list; ingest test: the status ends at the ready value on success and at a failure value with a code on error. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D9: fingerprint_status (READY, NEEDS_REVIEW) exists and ends READY on success (test_ingest_store.py :: test_a_new_recording_is_stored_with_all_its_hashes_and_the_ad_becomes_ready), but a failed file creates no asset row, so the failure lives on the ad (processing_error_code), not on the asset.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-21** A failed `ingest` job is retried with a growing delay (backoff), so a passing fault does not leave the ad FAILED. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Build "Retries with backoff"). *Verified by:* integration test: the first attempt fails with a temporary database error; the job runs again after a delay and the ad ends READY. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a failed download is tried again after a delay and the ad still becomes READY, while clips keep being answered ; apps/api/test/ingest-sweep.test.ts :: the ingest sweep > gives an ad that has waited a job with a short-lived download address and no credentials, once ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_failed_download_is_retried_not_refused.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-22** Retrying an `ingest` job is idempotent: a second run for the same ad leaves one asset, one set of hash rows and the same final status. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Verify "Retries are idempotent"). *Verified by:* integration test: run the same ingest job twice; the row counts in `audio_assets` and the current version's table (`hash_table_name()`; `fingerprints.hashes_version_2` since 12 October 2026) are equal after the first and the second run. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_running_a_job_twice_changes_nothing_the_second_time.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-23** Every ingest worker log line for a job carries the request id of the `complete` call that the job carries, so the worker's lines can be found from the portal's request. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Cross-cutting, correlation id; baseline §8); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §4 (plan line 621; baseline §8 row 1). *Verified by:* integration test: the request id of the `complete` response appears on the worker's ingest log lines. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_log_lines_are_json_with_time_level_message_and_the_jobs_request_id.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-24** The ingest worker decodes an upload under a wall-clock time cap and under input-size and decoded-length caps, so a decode bomb or a very slow file ends FAILED with a stable code within the cap and adds no audio-asset or hash rows. *Source:* baseline §4 row 4 "Audio decoding runs with time and size caps" (Planned, step 3). *Verified by:* worker test: an upload that expands past the decoded-length cap, and a file forced to decode slowly, each end FAILED with a stable code inside the time cap, with zero asset and hash rows added (the cap values for ads are gap 49; the 1-second cap of S3-MATCHER-04 is for listener clips). *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_decoding_stops_a_little_past_the_longest_ad ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_unusable_file_is_refused_with_its_reason ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_task_past_its_hard_cap_is_abandoned_and_the_pool_carries_on ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_file_over_20_megabytes_is_refused_unread ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a corrupt file ends FAILED with a reason the station can read, and stores no recording.
- [x] **S3-INGEST-25** Ingest jobs carry a deadline, and finished ingest jobs — completed, and failed after their last retry — are removed from the key-value store, so the presigned download address they carry does not linger there. *Source:* baseline §8 row 2 "Jobs carry a deadline; finished jobs and their audio are removed from the queue store" (Planned, step 3). *Verified by:* unit test of the ingest job builder: a deadline is set; integration test: after one ingest succeeds and one fails permanently, a scan of the store finds no job key holding the presigned address. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/ingest-sweep.test.ts :: the ingest sweep > gives an ad that has waited a job with a short-lived download address and no credentials, once ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > leaves no listener audio and no recognize job in Valkey, and nothing in the workers’ temporary folders.

### Shared audio between stations

- [ ] **S3-SHAREDAUDIO-01** Fingerprints and audio assets are global (shared by all stations) and live in their own `fingerprints` schema: `lookup_fingerprint_worker` reads it; `lookup_api` has no access to any table in it; `lookup_admin_api` has no direct access to any table in it except the one narrow read path for super-admin overlap review (findings, the two asset numbers and short-lived preview keys) chosen under P5A-OVERLAP-REVIEW-04 (supersedes blueprint §3 "prevents cross-tenant fingerprint visibility" through row-level security; conflict 14). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §3 (blueprint §3 line 44, §3.1 line 63; plan G3); plan O6 (line 661) and §B Overlap review (line 424). *Verified by:* database privilege tests: `lookup_api` is refused on every table in `fingerprints`; `lookup_admin_api` is refused on every table there except through the chosen path, which a test exercises; the worker role can read it. *Not ticked (partly built):* lookup_api and lookup_admin_api hold no privilege on fingerprints (database-catalogue.test.ts 'S1-PRIVILEGES-11: the API roles hold no privilege of any kind on the fingerprints schema or its tables') and the worker reads it (station-isolation-matrix 'S1-PRIVILEGES-24'); the admin's narrow overlap-review path is P5A-OVERLAP-REVIEW-04 (gap 48, user's decision).
- [x] **S3-SHAREDAUDIO-02** `fingerprints.audio_assets` holds no station column of any kind. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §3 (plan §Data model row fp.audio_assets "no station columns"). *Verified by:* database test lists the table's columns and finds none referring to a station. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S3-SHAREDAUDIO-02: a recording names no station: no column of the fingerprints tables refers to a station, client, ad or campaign.
- [x] **S3-SHAREDAUDIO-03** Several stations can each own an ad on the same audio asset, and each keeps its own ad, its own action card, its own campaign and schedule and its own analytics; none of these is shared between stations (D7; replaces the blueprint's one media item, one tenant model). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D7); slice-blueprint.md §13 (blueprint §6 lines 164–166, 201; tagged P4 there, see conflict 4). *Verified by:* isolation test with two stations on one asset: each station's context returns only its own ad, card, campaign and recognition rows. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > row-level security (lookup_api) > S3-SHAREDAUDIO-03: two stations whose ads share one recording each see only their own ad, buttons, campaign and recognitions.
- [x] **S3-SHAREDAUDIO-04** A station is never told that its upload matched another station's audio (O6): its ad simply shows Ready, and its ad responses and portal view have exactly the shape of a first-time upload. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §20 (plan O6); slice-blueprint.md §10 (plan O6; §Phases row 2 Verify); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (plan O6); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (row ads; O6). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: station B uploads station A's file; B's responses are indistinguishable from a first upload, with status Ready. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the same ad uploaded by a second station links to the same recording: no new fingerprints, READY, and nothing shows it is shared.
- [x] **S3-SHAREDAUDIO-05** No API response to a station exposes the audio asset's id or number, its hash count, a sharing flag or anything else from which a station could work out that audio is shared (O6). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §20 (plan O6; §Data model row fp.audio_assets). *Verified by:* contract test over the ad list and ad detail responses: no asset identifier, hash count or sharing field is present. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the same ad uploaded by a second station links to the same recording: no new fingerprints, READY, and nothing shows it is shared.
- [x] **S3-SHAREDAUDIO-06** When Station B uploads the same ad as Station A for its own client, it links to the same asset, no new fingerprint rows are created, and B's list shows the ad as Ready (MVP step 4). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Verify); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 4). *Verified by:* integration test: the hash-table row count before and after B's ingest is equal, both ads point at one asset, and B's ad status is READY. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the same ad uploaded by a second station links to the same recording: no new fingerprints, READY, and nothing shows it is shared.
- [x] **S3-SHAREDAUDIO-07** After a shared upload, neither station can see the other's ad or card for that audio, nor any sign that the audio is shared. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §17 (plan §Phases row 2, Verify; O6); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 4). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: each station's ad list and ad detail routes return only its own ad and card, with no field that differs from a non-shared ad's. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the same ad uploaded by a second station links to the same recording: no new fingerprints, READY, and nothing shows it is shared.

### Recognize job

- [x] **S3-QUEUE-01** An admitted clip is put on the BullMQ queue named `recognize` as one job. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram). *Verified by:* contract test: the API adds one job to `recognize` and the Python worker consumes from the same name. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the queue contract: a job Node adds is answered by Python in the contract’s shape, and one past its deadline is dropped undecoded.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-02** The recognize job carries the request id. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; baseline §8); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §4 (plan line 621). *Verified by:* integration test: the worker's log line for the job carries the same request id the API returned. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-03** The recognize job carries the clip itself, base64-encoded (plan name `audioB64`); the worker fetches nothing from storage for a recognition. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5). *Verified by:* integration test: the worker matches a clip with no object-storage access configured. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_bad_settings_stop_the_worker_naming_the_setting_never_its_value ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-04** The recognize job carries the codec, equal to the validated request value. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5). *Verified by:* unit test of the job builder. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-05** The recognize job carries a deadline (plan name `deadlineAt`), an absolute time after which the result is no longer wanted, because `waitUntilFinished` only stops waiting and does not cancel a job. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; §Verified facts, BullMQ); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §5 (Verified facts, BullMQ); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* unit test of the job builder: the deadline equals the enqueue time plus the wait limit. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-06** The recognize job carries the fingerprint algorithm version to match against (plan name `fpVersion`), equal to the configured current version. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5). *Verified by:* unit test of the job builder. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-07** The recognize job's data holds exactly the request id, the audio, the codec, the deadline and the fingerprint version: no install id or hash, no token, no station id, no station hint and no capture time. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; memory/security-by-design.md); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* contract test of the job payload schema: exactly those five fields. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the fingerprint jobs > a recognize job holds exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, the deadline and the fingerprint version.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-08** A recognize job is tried once only (`attempts: 1`); the queue never retries it by itself. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* integration test: a job whose worker handler fails is not processed a second time. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-09** A completed recognize job is removed from the key-value store at once (`removeOnComplete`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; baseline §8); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* integration test: after a successful recognition a scan of the store finds no key holding that job or its audio. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > leaves no listener audio and no recognize job in Valkey, and nothing in the workers’ temporary folders.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-10** A failed recognize job is removed from the key-value store at once (`removeOnFail`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; baseline §8); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* integration test: after a job fails or expires a scan of the store finds no key holding that job or its audio. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey.
- [ ] **S3-QUEUE-11** Listener audio never lingers in the key-value store after its job has finished, whatever the outcome (match, no match, failure, timeout) (kept from blueprint §4 and §8.1 "temporary audio buffers"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; §Phases row 3 Verify); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §15 (baseline §8 row 2). *Verified by:* integration test after a mixed run: no remaining key's value contains base64 audio. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D2 and deploy check 5 of the security baseline: a clip leaves Valkey’s memory with its job whatever the outcome (fingerprint-workers “leaves no listener audio …”, recognize-route timeout test), but stays in Valkey’s append-only file on disk until Valkey next rewrites that file.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-12** A live recognize job's deadline is 2.5 seconds (2,500 milliseconds) after it is enqueued (kept from blueprint §4's 2.5-second recognition limit). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5; blueprint §4 line 109). *Verified by:* unit test: for a live request the deadline minus the enqueue time is 2,500 milliseconds. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done.
- [x] **S3-QUEUE-13** The API does not rely on the wait timeout to cancel a job: an abandoned job is dropped by the worker through its deadline and removed from the store. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §7 (plan §Verified facts, BullMQ; §Recognition flow step 7). *Verified by:* integration test: after a timed-out request, resume the worker; the job returns `EXPIRED` without decoding and no job key remains. *Built as:* S3-D2, S3-D3: the API also removes a job no worker took. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the queue contract: a job Node adds is answered by Python in the contract’s shape, and one past its deadline is dropped undecoded ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_job_past_its_deadline_is_dropped_without_decoding.

### Recognize worker

- [x] **S3-MATCHER-01** A recognize job picked up after its deadline is dropped: the worker returns `EXPIRED` without decoding or fingerprinting the clip. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8, §14 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; §Step 3 workers); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §15 (baseline §8 row 2). *Verified by:* worker test (pytest): a job with a deadline in the past returns `EXPIRED` and the decode function is never called. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_job_past_its_deadline_is_dropped_without_decoding ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the queue contract: a job Node adds is answered by Python in the contract’s shape, and one past its deadline is dropped undecoded.
- [x] **S3-MATCHER-02** The recognize worker's steps run in this order: drop if past the deadline, decode (1-second cap), resample to the canonical rate, fingerprint, look up (800-millisecond database timeout), align, decide. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8, §14 (plan §Step 3 workers, lines 156–157; §Recognition flow step 7); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram). *Verified by:* worker test with stage timings or spies: each stage runs once, in that order; a 16 kilohertz phone clip of a 44.1 kilohertz reference ad matches that ad. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_clip_goes_through_each_stage_once_in_order ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_job_past_its_deadline_is_dropped_without_decoding ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [x] **S3-MATCHER-03** The worker decodes only container formats on an allow-list; any other format is refused with the unsupported-format code and is not decoded. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7 "whitelisted"; baseline §4); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* worker test: a file outside the allow-list returns the unsupported-format code. *Built as:* S3-D2: the job reports a format outside the allow-list with the failure code INVALID_AUDIO. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_clip_in_a_container_that_is_not_allowed_is_invalid_audio ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_container_whitelist ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_a_file_with_no_audio_signature_never_reaches_ffmpeg.
- [ ] **S3-MATCHER-04** Decoding a listener clip is capped at 1 second of wall-clock time; a clip that takes longer is abandoned with a timeout code and the job ends. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; §Step 3 workers; baseline §4); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §11 (baseline §4 row 4). *Verified by:* worker test: a decode forced to run past 1 second stops and returns a timeout code. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D10: the 1-second decode deadline is checked as each frame is decoded (test_workers.py test_a_decode_that_runs_past_its_time_is_decode_timeout), and the hard cap that abandons the pool task is 2.5 seconds for the whole clip (test_a_task_past_its_hard_cap_is_abandoned_and_the_pool_carries_on), so a decode stuck inside one frame is stopped at 2.5 seconds, not 1.
- [x] **S3-MATCHER-05** Decoding is also capped by input size and by decoded length, so a small file cannot expand without limit (a decode bomb is stopped with a code). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (baseline §4); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §11 (baseline §4 row 4). *Verified by:* worker test: an oversize input and a clip that decodes to more than the allowed length each return a code. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_a_clip_over_256_kilobytes_is_refused_before_it_is_decoded ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_byte_cap ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_duration_cap.
- [x] **S3-MATCHER-06** The hash lookup runs under an 800-millisecond statement timeout; a lookup forced past it is cancelled and the job returns an error code. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; §Step 3 workers); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* worker test: a lookup slowed with a lock or `pg_sleep` is cancelled at about 800 milliseconds and returns an error code. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_the_lookup_is_given_800_milliseconds_and_a_cancelled_lookup_is_lookup_timeout ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_postgres_index.py :: test_the_lookup_is_cancelled_at_its_statement_timeout.
