- [x] **S3-MATCHER-07** The recognize worker returns either a result holding the status, the audio asset number (plan name `asset_key`), the aligned hash count (`aligned`), the margin and the match offset in milliseconds (`offset_ms`), or an error code. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers, line 158); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram). *Verified by:* cross-language contract test: the Node side receives exactly those five fields with the right types for a match and for a no-match, validated against the shared worker-result shape. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the queue contract: a job Node adds is answered by Python in the contract’s shape, and one past its deadline is dropped undecoded ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an unrelated clip gets NO_MATCH, with a recognition id and no card ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_match_comes_back_in_the_contracts_shape_and_the_clip_is_capped ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_failures_are_one_code_each ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the fingerprint jobs > a recognize result names the recording by its number and carries no infinite margin.
- [x] **S3-MATCHER-08** The worker returns the matched audio asset's number, never an ad id or a station id (supersedes blueprint §4 "Matched media_item_id"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 line 96; plan G3, G13, D7). *Verified by:* contract test of the result fields; database test: the worker's role cannot read `app.ads`. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the fingerprint jobs > a recognize result names the recording by its number and carries no infinite margin ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_ingest_store.py :: test_the_worker_role_cannot_read_or_change_station_data_directly ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-24: the fingerprint worker works only in the fingerprints schema.
- [x] **S3-MATCHER-09** The worker keeps the clip in memory and drops the audio buffers when the job ends: the clip and its decoded samples are never written to disk and not kept between jobs (kept from blueprint §4 "calculate Fourier Transform hashes in memory"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; baseline §4; blueprint §8.1); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* worker test: after a run of jobs the worker's temporary directory is unchanged and no file under its working directory has changed. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_listener_clip_never_touches_the_disk ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > leaves no listener audio and no recognize job in Valkey, and nothing in the workers’ temporary folders.

### Listener installs

- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-01** `POST /api/v1/installs` registers a listener install and returns an install token (a signed JSON Web Token) that `POST /api/v1/recognize` accepts. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Step 3 API, line 161; G11; baseline §3); slice-blueprint.md §4 (plan Step 3, G11); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §10 (baseline §3 row 9), §23 (plan §Phases row 1); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the call returns a signed token, and that token is accepted by the recognize route. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > registers with no sign-in, cookie or token, and gives a token the recognize route accepts.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-02** A listener is anonymous: install registration needs no sign-in, there is no listener account, and no listener registration or sign-in route exists other than `POST /api/v1/installs` (supersedes blueprint §3.1 "anonymous or authenticated listening context"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan G11; blueprint §3.1 item 1); slice-blueprint.md §4 (blueprint §3.1 line 57; plan G11, §Data model row installs). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: registration succeeds with no cookie and no token; API route inventory test: no other listener account route exists. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > registers with no sign-in, cookie or token, and gives a token the recognize route accepts ; apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > only health, sign-in, refresh, sign-out, station sign-up and its email link, an invitation link, client errors and the listener app’s routes (installs, recognize, a station choice, a button tap, a report, the station list) are open without signing in, and none lists ads.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-03** The server stores only a keyed hash (HMAC with a server-side pepper) of the app's install id, never the raw id, and never logs the raw id. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Data model row installs); slice-blueprint.md §4; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (row installs). *Verified by:* API test: after registration the stored `install_id_hash` equals the keyed hash of the sent id, and the raw id appears in no table and in no log line. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > stores only a keyed hash of the install id, with the platform and the app version, and no verdict ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > never logs the install token, the raw install id or the audio.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-04** The install row records the platform and the app version sent at registration. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Data model row installs). *Verified by:* database assertion after registration: both columns are filled from the request. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > stores only a keyed hash of the install id, with the platform and the app version, and no verdict.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-05** Install registration accepts exactly two platform values, ANDROID and IOS; any other value is refused with a validation error. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §2 (blueprint §2 line 16, §6 line 214); slice-plan-recognition.md §15. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: registration succeeds for ANDROID and for IOS and is refused for any other value; database test of `listener_installs_platform_check`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > refuses a platform other than ANDROID or IOS, an install id that is not a UUID, and unknown fields ; apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > the database refuses a platform it does not know, whoever writes it ; apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > stores only a keyed hash of the install id, with the platform and the app version, and no verdict.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-06** Registering an install id that is already registered creates no second row and never fails with a server error. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Data model row installs; Foundation migration `listener_installs_install_id_hash_unique`; global rules "Two layers"). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: two registrations with the same install id both succeed and one row exists. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > registering the same install again keeps one row, never fails, and updates the app version.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-07** The install-token signing secret and the install-id pepper are part of the validated configuration: the API refuses to start when either is missing, weak or a placeholder, and the start-up message never contains the value. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (baseline §6; plan §Reused from Tonse-hub). *Verified by:* `startup-checks.test.ts`: start-up fails for each missing or placeholder value, and the failure message does not contain the value. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > S3-INSTALLS-07: refuses a missing, short, placeholder or reused install-token secret or install-id pepper, never printing it.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-08** The install registration route is covered by the baseline per-address rate limit, like every URL. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (baseline §2). *Verified by:* request-security style test: requests beyond the configured allowance from one address are answered 429 in the error envelope. *Built as:* S3-D18: listener routes have their own per-address limits instead of the portal baseline. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > registrations are limited per address, strictly: installs cost nothing to make.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-09** The recognize route (`POST /api/v1/recognize`) requires a valid install token; a missing or invalid token is answered 401 `INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID` (supersedes blueprint §3, an anonymous `/recognize`). The choice and engagements endpoints require it from P4 (P4-CHOICE-07, P4-ENGAGEMENTS-08). `POST /api/v1/client-errors` needs no install token (S2-CLIENT-ERRORS-07), so a crash before registration can be reported; whether the stations list needs one is part 3 gap 7. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §3 (blueprint §3 line 29; plan G11 "Anonymous `/recognize` endpoint has no abuse control → install JWT", Recognition flow steps 2 and 9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the recognize route without a token and with a tampered token returns 401 with that code. Narrowed in review round 1 (it named every listener route). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > refuses a missing, malformed, tampered or expired install token with 401 INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID, before the size or anything else.
- [ ] **S3-INSTALLS-10** A Play Integrity or App Attest verdict is only a soft signal, read at install registration: a registration with no verdict or a failed verdict still succeeds (O10). The verdict is stored in the nullable column of S3-INSTALLS-12. *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (plan O10). *Verified by:* API test: a registration with no verdict and one with a failed verdict both succeed. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D12: Step 3 reads no verdict (RegisterInstallInput refuses any verdict field); checking Play Integrity/App Attest is decided with the app in Step 4, so "a failed verdict still succeeds" cannot be tested yet.
- [x] **S3-INSTALLS-11** Devices without Google Play (for example Huawei phones) can still register and identify (O10). *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (plan O10). *Verified by:* API test: registration with no integrity token issues an install token, and that token can identify. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > works for a phone without Google Play: no integrity verdict is needed ; apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > registers with no sign-in, cookie or token, and gives a token the recognize route accepts.
- [ ] **S3-INSTALLS-12** `listener_installs` can record the optional device-integrity verdict (Play Integrity or App Attest) in a nullable column, used only as a soft signal; registration still succeeds without a verdict, so devices without Google Play keep working. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9i (plan §Data model row installs "optional integrity verdict", line 369; plan O10, line 665) [D501]; moved from P8-INSTALLS-01 in review round 1 (conflict 7). *Verified by:* schema test: a nullable verdict column exists; API test: registration with no verdict succeeds and stores NULL, and one with a verdict stores it. Not built: the built `listener_installs` has no verdict column (the verdict values are part 5 gap 14). *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D12: the nullable integrity_verdict column exists and stays NULL (listener-installs 'stores only a keyed hash …'), but storing a sent verdict waits for Step 4.

### Recognition request and edge checks

- [x] **S3-REQUEST-01** Recognition is `POST /api/v1/recognize` (path and method kept from blueprint §7); a GET on the path does not run recognition and is answered in the error envelope. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 lines 224–226); slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1; §Step 3 API). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: POST works; GET answers with the error envelope. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes multipart only: a JSON body is refused with 415, and GET runs nothing ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-02** The recognize route accepts `multipart/form-data` only (kept from blueprint §7); a JSON body sent to it is refused in the error envelope with a catalogue code. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1; blueprint §7); slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 line 228). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a multipart request is processed; a JSON body is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes multipart only: a JSON body is refused with 415, and GET runs nothing ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-03** The request carries the clip as a file part named `audio`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request without an `audio` part is refused with a validation error naming the field. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names the audio when the clip is missing, and refuses unknown fields without echoing them.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-04** The request carries the clip length in milliseconds (plan name `clip_ms`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request without the clip length is refused with a validation error naming the field. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names clip_duration_milliseconds when it is missing.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-05** The request carries the audio codec (plan name `codec`) from an allowed list. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request without the codec, or with a codec outside the allowed list, is refused with a validation error naming the field. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names codec when it is missing ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize request fields > refuses an unknown codec, a key or hint that is not a UUID, a time that is not a timestamp, and any extra field.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-06** The request may carry an optional station hint (plan name `station_hint`) taken from "My station"; a request without it is valid, and a hint that is not a valid station id is refused with a validation error. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1); slice-blueprint.md §11 (tagged P4 there; see conflict 2). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the same clip is processed with and without the hint; a malformed hint is refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > accepts a valid station hint, refuses one that is not a station id ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize request fields > reads the text fields of the multipart form into their values.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-07** Every recognition request, live or deferred, carries an `idempotency_key` that is a UUID created at capture. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1, line 476); slice-blueprint.md §11 (tagged P4 there; see conflict 1). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request with no key, or with a key that is not a UUID, is refused with a validation error, including a live request. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize request fields > needs %s, live or deferred ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names idempotency_key when it is missing ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize request fields > refuses an unknown codec, a key or hint that is not a UUID, a time that is not a timestamp, and any extra field.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-08** Every recognition request, live or deferred, carries `captured_at`, the phone's time when the clip started. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1); slice-blueprint.md §11 (tagged P4 there). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request without `captured_at`, or with a value that is not a timestamp, is refused with a validation error. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names captured_at when it is missing ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize request fields > refuses an unknown codec, a key or hint that is not a UUID, a time that is not a timestamp, and any extra field.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-09** Every recognition request, live or deferred, carries `sent_at`, the phone's time when this attempt was sent. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1); slice-blueprint.md §11 (tagged P4 there). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request without `sent_at`, or with a value that is not a timestamp, is refused with a validation error. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize request fields > refuses an unknown codec, a key or hint that is not a UUID, a time that is not a timestamp, and any extra field ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names sent_at when it is missing.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-10** Every recognition request, live or deferred, carries a yes-or-no `deferred` flag. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (plan §Recognition flow step 1); slice-blueprint.md §11 (tagged P4 there). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request without the flag is refused with a validation error. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names is_deferred when it is missing.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-11** The route reads the install token from a request header and the request id from `X-Request-Id`. *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §11 (plan §Recognition flow step 1, line 477). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request with both headers is processed, and the response echoes the request id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes the caller’s request id when it is a UUID, carrying it to the answer and the worker’s job, and replaces any other.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-12** The recognize route refuses unknown fields instead of ignoring them (400 `VALIDATION_FAILED`), and its validation errors name the field path and the reason, never the submitted value. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §2 (baseline §2). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test on the recognize route: an extra field is refused, and no error contains a submitted value. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > names the audio when the clip is missing, and refuses unknown fields without echoing them ; apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > rejects unknown fields without echoing their names ; apps/api/test/request-security.test.ts :: request-layer security > input validation > names the bad field and a reason code, never the submitted value.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-13** The API handles a recognize request in this order, each check refusing before later work is done: request id, install token, rate limit, size and first-bytes check, (from P4) the idempotency claim, admission and circuit breaker, enqueue. There is no separate gateway product (supersedes blueprint §3 "Multi-Tenant API Gateway" box). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Target architecture diagram, lines 304–305; §Recognition flow steps 2–4); slice-blueprint.md §3 (blueprint §3 lines 28–29); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram). *Verified by:* API tests: an oversize clip with a bad token gets 401; an invalid token is refused before the rate-limit counter moves; a rate-limited install with a valid clip gets 429 and nothing is enqueued; every refusal carries a request id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > refuses a missing, malformed, tampered or expired install token with 401 INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID, before the size or anything else ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > an invalid token does not use up any install’s allowance ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > counts per install: the seventh clip in a minute is refused with 429 and nothing is queued, while another install on the same address goes on.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-14** On the recognize route a caller's `X-Request-Id` is kept when it is a valid UUID; a missing or hostile value is replaced by a generated UUID. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2, line 479). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test on `POST /api/v1/recognize`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes the caller’s request id when it is a UUID, carrying it to the answer and the worker’s job, and replaces any other.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-15** Every recognize response, success or error (200, 4xx and 503), carries the request id in the `X-Request-Id` header, equal to the id in the body. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2, line 479). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test over 200, 4xx and 503 answers. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > refuses a missing, malformed, tampered or expired install token with 401 INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID, before the size or anything else ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-16** The recognize route verifies the install token before any other work on the request; a missing, malformed, expired or wrongly signed token is answered 401 `INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID` in the error envelope with a request id, and no queue job is created. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §10 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: each of the four bad-token cases returns 401 with that code and a request id, and the queue's job count is unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > refuses a missing, malformed, tampered or expired install token with 401 INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID, before the size or anything else.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-17** The recognize route accepts a body of at most 256 kilobytes; a larger one is answered 413 in the error envelope with a catalogue code and a request id, before any job is queued. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §10, §15 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 9; §Step 3 API, line 163); slice-blueprint.md §11; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a clip at the limit is processed; one byte over is answered 413 and the queue is unchanged (see gaps: 256,000 or 262,144 bytes). *Built as:* S3-D13: 256 KiB read as 262,144 bytes. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes a clip of exactly 256 KiB and refuses one byte more with 413, before any job.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-18** The size limit is enforced while the body is being read, so an oversize upload is never buffered whole or put on the queue. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently, line 93; §Recognition flow step 2). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a multi-megabyte upload is cut off with 413, the process's memory stays bounded, and no job is created. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > cuts a multi-megabyte upload off once it passes the limit, without reading the rest ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes a clip of exactly 256 KiB and refuses one byte more with 413, before any job.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-19** The recognize route has its own 256-kilobyte multipart limit, while the general 100-kilobyte JSON body limit still applies to JSON routes. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (baseline §2; plan §Recognition flow step 2). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a 200-kilobyte clip is accepted on the recognize route while a 200-kilobyte JSON body on another route is still refused. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes a clip of exactly 256 KiB and refuses one byte more with 413, before any job ; apps/api/test/error-responses.test.ts :: error responses never expose internals > oversized JSON bodies get a 413.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-20** The clip's first bytes are checked for an accepted audio container signature (`ftyp` for MP4 and AAC, `OggS` for Ogg); the declared content type and the file name are not trusted. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2; §Step 3 API "magic-byte check"; baseline §4); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a text file named `clip.m4a` with content type `audio/mp4` is refused; a valid clip with a misleading file name is accepted. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > checks the clip’s first bytes, not its name or declared type ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the clip’s first bytes > must be the container of the codec sent.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-21** A clip whose first bytes match no accepted signature is answered 415 in the error envelope with a catalogue code and a request id, and no queue job is created. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §10 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: PNG bytes, an empty file and random bytes each return 415 and the queue is unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > checks the clip’s first bytes, not its name or declared type ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the clip’s first bytes > must be the container of the codec sent.
- [x] **S3-REQUEST-22** The clip length must be between 2,000 and 15,000 milliseconds; a value outside is refused in the error envelope before the clip is queued. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §15 (plan §Recognition flow step 2; §Step 3 API, line 163); slice-blueprint.md §11; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end boundary test: 1,999 and 15,001 are refused with no job created; 2,000 and 15,000 are accepted. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > a clip of %s ms is answered %s.

### Rate limits

- [x] **S3-RATELIMIT-01** Recognition requests are rate-limited per install, not per client address, because Zambian mobile networks put many listeners behind one shared address. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2; §Step 3 API; G11; baseline §3); slice-blueprint.md §3 (blueprint §3 line 29; G11); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §10 (baseline §3 row 9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: two installs behind one address each get their own allowance; one install using two addresses shares one allowance. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > counts per install: the seventh clip in a minute is refused with 429 and nothing is queued, while another install on the same address goes on ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > counts per install whatever the address: clips of one install from two addresses share one allowance.
- [x] **S3-RATELIMIT-02** The live allowance per install has a per-minute limit (plan example: 6 per minute). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2, line 481); slice-blueprint.md §3. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with the configured limit: the request after the last allowed one within a minute (the seventh, at the example value) is answered 429. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > counts per install: the seventh clip in a minute is refused with 429 and nothing is queued, while another install on the same address goes on.
- [x] **S3-RATELIMIT-03** The live allowance per install has a per-day limit (plan example: 60 per day). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2); slice-blueprint.md §3. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with a fake clock or a lowered limit: the request after the daily allowance is answered 429 even when the per-minute allowance is free. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-install-limits.test.ts :: per-install recognition limits > the daily allowance holds when the minute’s allowance is free again.
- [x] **S3-RATELIMIT-04** A request over the per-install limit is answered 429 `RATE_LIMITED` in the error envelope with a request id, and no queue job is created for it. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §10 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the 429 body has code `RATE_LIMITED` and a request id; the queue's job count is unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > counts per install: the seventh clip in a minute is refused with 429 and nothing is queued, while another install on the same address goes on.
- [x] **S3-RATELIMIT-05** The per-install limits are configuration values, not constants in code (the plan gives them only as examples). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2 "e.g."; O14). *Verified by:* API test: changing the configured limits changes the request at which 429 first appears. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-install-limits.test.ts :: per-install recognition limits > applies the configured allowance: with 3 a minute, the fourth clip in a minute is the first refused.
- [x] **S3-RATELIMIT-06** The per-install counters live in the shared key-value store, so the limit holds across several API instances. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (baseline §2; plan §Target architecture "2+ instances"). *Verified by:* API test: two application instances sharing one store enforce one combined allowance for the same install. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-install-limits.test.ts :: per-install recognition limits > two API instances count one install together: its allowance is shared, not doubled.
- [ ] **S3-RATELIMIT-07** The per-address limit on the recognize route is only a high ceiling, a named configuration value well above one install's allowance, so listeners behind one shared address are not blocked in normal use. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan G11, line 293); slice-blueprint.md §3 (G11); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: more installs than one install's allowance each identify from one address without 429; configuration test: the ceiling is a multiple of the per-install limit. *Not ticked (partly built):* RECOGNITIONS_PER_CLIENT_ADDRESS_PER_MINUTE (300) and LISTENER_REQUESTS_PER_CLIENT_ADDRESS_PER_MINUTE (600) are named config far above 6; recognize-route "counts per address too…" passes 5 installs × 6 requests from one address (all 400s, not identifications) and listener-installs "the listener routes have their own per-address ceiling…" shows the ceiling above the portal's; missing: a config check plus test that the recognize per-address limit is a multiple of the per-install limit (app-config only checks the 600 ceiling ≥ 10×; 300 could be set to 1), and an e2e where more than 6 installs identify (200) from one address.

### Admission, circuit breaker and wait

- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-01** When the circuit breaker is open, a recognition request is answered at once (well under the 2.5-second wait) with 503 `RECOGNITION_BUSY` and a `Retry-After` header, and no queue job is created (kept from blueprint §4 "circuit breaking during traffic spikes"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5, §10, §15 (plan §Recognition flow steps 4 and 9; §Step 3 API, line 164); slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 line 85); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G11). *Verified by:* API test: force the breaker open; the next request returns 503 with that code, the header and a request id, and the queue's job count is unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-02** When the queue depth is over its threshold, a recognition request is answered 503 `RECOGNITION_BUSY` with `Retry-After`, and no job is created. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 4; §Step 3 API); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API test: fill the `recognize` queue above the configured threshold with the worker paused; the next request returns 503 with the header and creates no job. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with too many clips waiting: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-03** The queue depth used for admission is a cached value refreshed in the background, not read from the key-value store on every request. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 4 "cached queue depth"). *Verified by:* unit test: many admission checks in a row cause at most one depth read per refresh interval. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > reads the queue depth and the heartbeat in the background about once a second, however many clips arrive.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-04** When no worker heartbeat key exists, a recognition request is answered 503 `RECOGNITION_BUSY` with `Retry-After` at once (not after the 2.5-second wait), and no job is left in the queue (replaces blueprint §4 "manages worker lifecycle"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 4; §Step 3 API); slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 line 84). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: stop the worker and let its heartbeat expire; the next request returns 503 at once with a request id and the queue's job count is unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_killed_workers_heartbeat_expires_by_itself ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-05** Every 503 from the recognize route carries a `Retry-After` header holding a positive number of seconds. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 4; §Step 3 API); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API tests for each refusal cause (breaker open, queue depth, missing heartbeat) assert the header. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with too many clips waiting: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > past the limit of recognitions at once on this instance, reading clips included, refuses the next at once as busy and takes clips again once places free up ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-06** Every 503 from the recognize route uses the error envelope with a request id and a plain message, and never names the queue, the worker, the breaker or the key-value store. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 9; global rules "Two layers"; baseline §2). *Verified by:* leak test over each 503 cause: the body matches the envelope and contains none of the internal-detail patterns. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with too many clips waiting: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > past the limit of recognitions at once on this instance, reading clips included, refuses the next at once as busy and takes clips again once places free up ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > every refusal is the shared error envelope: code, message and request id, and the fields only when the request failed validation.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-07** The API waits for a live job's result with `waitUntilFinished` for at most 2,500 milliseconds (kept from blueprint §4 "strict recognition timeout limit (e.g., 2.5 seconds max)"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §7, §15 (plan §Recognition flow step 6; §Step 3 API, line 165); slice-blueprint.md §7 (blueprint §4 line 109). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with a worker that never answers: the request returns between 2.5 seconds and 2.5 seconds plus a small stated margin. *Built as:* S3-D3: the API waits on its own QueueEvents listener registered before the add, not job.waitUntilFinished; same 2,500 ms limit. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-08** The enqueue and the wait run inside the circuit breaker, so timeouts and worker failures count as breaker failures. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §7 (plan §Recognition flow step 6). *Verified by:* API test: a run of timed-out requests opens the breaker, after which requests fail fast without waiting. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the recognition circuit breaker > opens after repeated timeouts, refuses at once while open, and half-opens after its reset time.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-09** When the wait ends without a result, the request is answered 503 `RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT` in the error envelope with a request id. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §7, §10 (plan §Recognition flow steps 6 and 9); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with the worker paused but its heartbeat present: the response is 503 with that code. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-10** The circuit breaker opens after the configured number of repeated failures; the next request is then refused at once without waiting. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Verify); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API test: repeated worker failures open the breaker and the next request gets 503 at once. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the recognition circuit breaker > opens after repeated timeouts, refuses at once while open, and half-opens after its reset time.
- [x] **S3-ADMISSION-11** After its reset time the circuit breaker half-opens and lets a trial request through; it closes again when that request succeeds and opens again when it fails. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Verify "breaker opens, then half-opens"); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API test with a fake clock: after the reset time one request reaches the worker; on success normal service resumes, on failure the breaker opens again. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the recognition circuit breaker > opens after repeated timeouts, refuses at once while open, and half-opens after its reset time.

### Station resolution, basic

- [ ] **S3-RESOLUTION-01** Station resolution happens in one SECURITY DEFINER database function, `recognition.resolve(audio asset number, effective capture time, receipt time, station hint)` (plan name `recog.resolve(asset_key, captured_at_eff, received_at, hint)`), which pins `search_path` and is executable by `lookup_api` only, not by PUBLIC or the worker role. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8; §Step 1 migration; G3); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G3), §11 (plan §Step 1 migration); slice-blueprint.md §3 (G3). *Verified by:* database test: the function exists with those four inputs, is SECURITY DEFINER, pins `search_path`, and only `lookup_api` may execute it. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D19: the definer function is recognition.find_stations_for_audio(audio asset number, effective capture time), with no receipt-time or hint input, and the station is chosen in TypeScript (features/recognition/choose-station.ts); its SECURITY DEFINER, pinned search_path, no-PUBLIC and worker-refused facts are tested (portal-sessions-database "every SECURITY DEFINER function pins…", database-privileges "no function of ours is executable by PUBLIC…", engine-hookup "the API role reaches the keys…"); the user should accept the split or ask for recognition.resolve.
- [ ] **S3-RESOLUTION-02** The resolve function takes the capture time and the receipt time as two separate inputs from its first version, and decides whether a campaign's active period and time windows cover the ad at the capture time, never at the receipt time or "the current timestamp" (supersedes blueprint §4 "current timestamp"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule, lines 470–472; §Step 1 migration; §Step 3 API; the period and window lines are tagged P4 there, see conflict 3); slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 lines 115, 117; plan D5). *Verified by:* table-driven database test: with the two times one hour apart the schedule is evaluated at the capture time; a clip captured inside the period and a window and received after it ended is on air; a clip captured before them and received inside them is not. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D19: the function takes only the effective capture time (receipt-time checks are P4), so "two separate inputs from its first version" is not built; the capture-time half is tested (recognize-route "judges "on air" at the moment the clip was heard…": heard inside a window 3 h ago, sent now → MATCH; heard now → NO_MATCH); the "captured before, received inside → not on air" case is untested.
- [ ] **S3-RESOLUTION-03** `recognition.resolve` is the only way the API role reads across stations, and it returns only public card data (station name, frequency and logo; client name and logo; ad title; the card), never engagement limits, counters, internal ids of other stations' campaigns or any other station-internal field. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan G3; baseline §1); slice-blueprint.md §3 (G3); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G3; baseline §1). *Verified by:* database test as `lookup_api` with no station context: direct selects on ads, campaigns and action cards return nothing, while the function returns exactly the public fields. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D19 returns every owning station's row to the API and chooses in TypeScript; the listener-field and no-direct-select halves are tested (engine-hookup "returns the station, its card and only what a listener may see…", "as the API role with no station context…"); see GAP.
- [ ] **S3-RESOLUTION-04** After a worker match the recognize route resolves the station by calling `recognition.resolve` once. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Step 3 API, line 166); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a matched clip returns Station A's card, and a database spy or log shows one call per matched recognition. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D19: after a worker MATCH the route calls recognition.find_stations_for_audio once (recognition.service.ts answerClaimedRecognition) and chooses in TypeScript; MATCH with Station A's card is tested (recognize-route "answers MATCH…") but nothing asserts one call per matched recognition (spy or log line).
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-05** A station is a candidate only if its status is ACTIVE: nothing of a station goes live to listeners until an admin has verified it (D6), so stations in PENDING_VERIFICATION, PENDING_REVIEW, REJECTED or SUSPENDED status never produce a card. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 508); slice-blueprint.md §8; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D6). *Verified by:* table-driven database test over the four statuses, plus an API end-to-end test: an ad published by a pending, rejected or suspended station yields no card for that station. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > ignores stations that are not active, campaigns that are not active, archived ads, and ads on other recordings ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > never answers with a station that is not active.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-06** A station is a candidate only through a campaign whose status is ACTIVE; DRAFT, PAUSED and ENDED campaigns contribute no candidate. *Changed by P4 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, Q3 of the P4 design; P4-TIMERULE-09):* a campaign PAUSED or ENDED after the clip was heard still makes its station a candidate, shown with the client's default card; DRAFT campaigns, and those paused or ended before the clip was heard, still contribute none, which the evidence below shows. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §8. *Verified by:* table-driven database test over the three statuses. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > ignores stations that are not active, campaigns that are not active, archived ads, and ads on other recordings.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-07** A station is a candidate only if that campaign's ad uses the matched audio asset. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §8. *Verified by:* database test: a station with an active campaign on a different asset is not a candidate. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > ignores stations that are not active, campaigns that are not active, archived ads, and ads on other recordings.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-08** A candidate is in window when its campaign's active period (the blueprint's start and end dates) contains the capture time and one of its time windows contains the capture time (kept from blueprint §4). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 509; §Step 3 tests); slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 line 115). *Verified by:* table-driven database test: inside the period and a window is in window; inside the period but outside every window, outside the period at a window's hours, one instant before the period start and one at the period end are out of window. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > returns the station, its card and only what a listener may see, in window inside the period and a window ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > judges the period as [start, end): one instant before the start and exactly at the end are out.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-09** With one station owning the matched ad and its campaign in window, the response is MATCH with that campaign's card, holding exactly the buttons saved for it (kept from blueprint §4 "resolves dynamic Schema UI for active campaign"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Step 3 tests; §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 line 102); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram "one match card"). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: Station A's published ad, captured inside its window, returns MATCH with the buttons saved for the ad. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-10** When the matched ad is outside its schedule and exactly one station owns it, the response carries that station's client default card (O3; MVP step 6) (supersedes blueprint §4 "the brand's default landing card", which had no model). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §15, §20 (plan §Recognition flow step 8; §Step 3 API, line 168; O3; G5); slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 line 121); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O3), §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 6). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test "outside the schedule gives the fallback": one owner outside its windows, client with a default card; the response carries the default card. *Built as:* S3-D19: "owns it" counts ACTIVE stations with an ACTIVE campaign on the audio; a second station owning the audio with no live campaign is ignored (as in the fingerprint-workers case). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > gives the client’s default card outside the schedule (stored as MATCH_DEFAULT), and no card when there is none ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > outside its schedule the clip gets the client’s default card.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-11** When the matched ad is outside its schedule and the client has no default card, the response is NO_MATCH with no card. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §15 (plan §Step 3 API, line 168); slice-blueprint.md §8; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O3). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: one owner outside its schedule, client without a default card; the status is NO_MATCH and no card is returned. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > gives the client’s default card outside the schedule (stored as MATCH_DEFAULT), and no card when there is none.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-12** A recognition that ends with a client's default card is recorded with outcome `MATCH_DEFAULT`, distinct from `MATCH`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Data model row recognition_events; Foundation migration `recognition_events_outcome_check`); slice-blueprint.md §8. *Verified by:* database assertion after an out-of-window recognition: the stored outcome is `MATCH_DEFAULT`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > gives the client’s default card outside the schedule (stored as MATCH_DEFAULT), and no card when there is none.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-13** A change to an ad's buttons made in the portal (link address, phone number, label) is what the next recognition returns, with no app release, app-store review or deployment (kept from blueprint §8.2 "Instant Dynamic Content Control"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §12 (blueprint §8 line 283); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (Holds). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: change the call number with `PUT /ads/:id/card`, recognise again with the same app build, and the response carries the new number. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > publishing > a card saved while the ad is live reaches listeners at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > returns a button edited in the portal on the very next recognition.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-14** An ad starts and stops being returned to listeners purely by its campaign's dates and time windows: no manual database edit, deployment or status change is needed when a window opens or closes (kept from blueprint §8.3 "Resilient Scheduling"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §12 (blueprint §8 line 285); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (Holds). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with a controlled clock: the same published ad gives the campaign card inside its window and the fallback outside, with no write in between. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > judges "on air" at the moment the clip was heard, in the station’s time zone, not when it arrived ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > returns the station, its card and only what a listener may see, in window inside the period and a window.
- [x] **S3-RESOLUTION-15** Station managers can change an ad's offer content in the portal — the blueprint's "offer codes", for example an offer code inside a WhatsApp button's first message, or a card offer-text field once the contract has one — and the next recognition returns the new content with no app update or deployment (kept from blueprint §8.2). *Source:* blueprint §8 item 2 "update link destinations, call numbers, or offer codes via the portal in real time"; plan §Blueprint analysis, Holds "portal-editable payloads (§8.2)". *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: change a WhatsApp action's first message that carries an offer code with `PUT /ads/:id/card`, recognise again with the same app build, and the response carries the new text; extend the test to any offer-text field the contract gains (part 3 gap 6). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > publishing > a card saved while the ad is live reaches listeners at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > returns a button edited in the portal on the very next recognition ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.

### Schedule evaluation

- [x] **S3-SCHEDULE-01** Time windows are evaluated in the station's own time zone (default `Africa/Lusaka`), never in server time or UTC (defines blueprint §4's undefined "local time"). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §19 (plan G6; §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 line 117; G6); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G6). *Verified by:* table-driven database test: a window 08:00 to 10:00 for a station in `Africa/Lusaka` contains 06:30 UTC and not 08:30 UTC; the same instant is in window for one station and out of window for a station in another zone with the same local hours. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > returns the station, its card and only what a listener may see, in window inside the period and a window ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > runs an overnight window past midnight, belonging to the day it starts ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > judges "on air" at the moment the clip was heard, in the station’s time zone, not when it arrived ; apps/api/test/stations-and-clients.test.ts :: station access, the station profile and clients > who can reach a station > a member reads the station profile, with their role.
- [x] **S3-SCHEDULE-02** Days of the week are ISO numbers, 1 for Monday to 7 for Sunday. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §19 (plan G6; §Phases row 4 Verify "ISO days"). *Verified by:* table-driven test: a window for day 1 matches a Monday and not a Sunday; day 7 matches a Sunday. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window past midnight: open late on the listed day (Friday 22:00) ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window Sunday into Monday: Monday 00:30 belongs to Sunday ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window Sunday into Monday: Monday 23:30 is not Sunday ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window is closed on a day not listed (Saturday) ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > runs an overnight window past midnight, belonging to the day it starts.
- [x] **S3-SCHEDULE-03** A window whose local end time is earlier than its local start time runs past midnight and stays open until the end time. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §19 (plan G6; §Data model row campaign_windows; §Phases row 4 Verify "midnight crossing"); slice-blueprint.md §8 (G6); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G6). *Verified by:* table-driven test: a 22:00 to 02:00 window contains 23:30 and 01:30 and does not contain 02:30, 03:00 or 21:00, with the day taken from the start side (see gaps). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > runs an overnight window past midnight, belonging to the day it starts ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window past midnight: still open early the next day (Saturday 01:59) ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window past midnight: closed at its end the next day (Saturday 02:00) ; apps/api/test/campaign-time-windows-database.test.ts :: time windows > a window past midnight: early hours belong to the previous day, so Friday 01:00 is closed.

### Recording recognitions

- [x] **S3-RECORDING-01** The recognize route records a recognition event for every clip that reaches the engine through the SECURITY DEFINER function `recognition.record_recognition` (plan name `recog.record_recognition`), which pins `search_path` and is executable by the API role only; the API role has no other way to write `recognition_events`, and analytics rows are written only through such functions in the `recognition` schema. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Step 3 API; §Step 1 migration; §Data model roles); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram), §10 (Roles and row-level security), §11 (plan §Step 1 migration). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: one new row per request; database test: PUBLIC and the worker role cannot execute the function. *Built as:* S3-D16, S3-D22: built as recognition.claim_recognition (inserts the PENDING row) plus recognition.finish_recognition, not one record_recognition function. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > the API role reaches the keys and events only through these functions, and the worker not at all ; apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations > the API role cannot write recognition events directly ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: database privileges of the API role > database-wide > no function of ours is executable by PUBLIC (protects future SECURITY DEFINER functions) ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > privileges > every SECURITY DEFINER function pins its search path with pg_catalog first and pg_temp last.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-02** A recognition event is recorded for NO_MATCH results and failures too, not only for matches (supersedes blueprint §6, which logged matches only). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan G12); slice-blueprint.md §10 (blueprint §6 line 215; G12); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G12). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: an unrelated clip adds one row with outcome NO_MATCH. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > an attempt that fails after it was claimed is recorded as ERROR, never left PENDING, and the same key can be tried again at once.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-03** Each recorded event holds the install, the request id, the capture time, the receipt time and the outcome. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Data model row recognition_events; G12). *Verified by:* database assertion after a MATCH and after a NO_MATCH: each of these columns is filled. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-04** Each recorded event holds the engine's measurements: aligned hash count, match margin, match offset in milliseconds and fingerprint version, equal to the values in the worker's result. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Data model row recognition_events); slice-blueprint.md §10 (G12); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G12), §9 (row recognition_events). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the stored values equal those in the worker's result. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-05** Each recorded event holds the request's measurements: the clip duration in milliseconds (equal to the clip length sent) and the latency in milliseconds (positive and no greater than the request's own duration). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15; slice-blueprint.md §10 (G12); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G12), §9 (row recognition_events). *Verified by:* API test reads both fields after a recognition. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-06** On a match the event records the matched audio asset and, when resolved, the station, the campaign and the action card shown, the card being a snapshot used for replays; after a NO_MATCH all four are empty (supersedes blueprint §6 `recognition_logs.media_item_id`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Data model row recognition_events); slice-blueprint.md §10 (blueprint §6 line 213); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (row recognition_events). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test reads the four references on the stored row after a MATCH and after a NO_MATCH. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a finished recognition sent again is answered the same, with the same id, and the worker is not asked twice.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-07** The recognition id is a UUIDv7 generated by the API; it is the public `recognition_id`, equals the stored row's id, and ids sort in creation order (supersedes blueprint §6 `recognition_logs.id`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §15 (plan §Standing requirements; §Data model row recognition_events; conventions §Scaling rules); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (row recognition_events). *Verified by:* unit test: the id's version is 7 and ids sort in creation order; API test: the response id equals the stored id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: UUID version 7 recognition ids > have the version 7 shape and carry the time they were made ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: UUID version 7 recognition ids > sort in the order they were made, even many in one millisecond ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-08** Finalising a recognition sets its outcome to a terminal value; no completed recognition stays `PENDING`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 514). *Verified by:* database assertion after each completed recognition. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > an attempt that fails after it was claimed is recorded as ERROR, never left PENDING, and the same key can be tried again at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > gives the client’s default card outside the schedule (stored as MATCH_DEFAULT), and no card when there is none ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > a new key makes one PENDING recognition; finishing it sets the outcome and when it completed.
- [x] **S3-RECORDING-09** Finalising sets `completed_at` on the recognition row; it stays empty while the row is `PENDING`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 514). *Verified by:* database assertion for every terminal outcome. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > a new key makes one PENDING recognition; finishing it sets the outcome and when it completed ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > an attempt that fails after it was claimed is recorded as ERROR, never left PENDING, and the same key can be tried again at once.

### Response

- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-01** A successful recognition response's `status` is exactly one of `MATCH`, `CHOOSE_STATION` (produced from P4) or `NO_MATCH`; the value `MATCH_FOUND` is never used (supersedes blueprint §7 line 234). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9; G9); slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 line 234; G9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G9). *Verified by:* contract test: the response schema's `status` lists exactly those three values, and the API never emits another. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-found-status is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > ambiguous-status is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-campaign is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > no-match is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > choose-station is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-02** The response carries `recognition_id`, an opaque UUID (version 7, no prefix), in every status including NO_MATCH (supersedes blueprint §7 `"rec_7890abc"`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9; G9); slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 line 235); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G9). *Verified by:* contract test and API end-to-end test parse it as a version 7 UUID. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > recognition-id-version-4 is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an unrelated clip gets NO_MATCH, with a recognition id and no card.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-03** The response carries `request_id`, equal to the `X-Request-Id` response header. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11 (top-level shape). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the body field equals the header for MATCH and for NO_MATCH. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-04** The response carries `is_deferred`, a yes-or-no value present in every status. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* contract test. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > missing-is-deferred is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > no-match-deferred-with-offset is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > judges "on air" at the moment the clip was heard, in the station’s time zone, not when it arrived.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-05** The response carries `captured_at`, the server-normalised capture time, equal to the stored value. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* contract test plus a database comparison. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > records the recognition: install, request id, times, outcome, the match facts, where it resolved, and when it completed ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > corrects the capture time by the phone’s clock: a phone 3 hours slow still records when it was really heard.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-06** A `MATCH` response carries one `result` and no `candidates`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11 (top level `{status, recognition_id, request_id, is_deferred, captured_at}` plus `result` or `candidates[]`). *Verified by:* contract test against the response schema with a MATCH fixture. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-campaign is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-without-result is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-07** A `NO_MATCH` response carries neither `result` nor `candidates`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9). *Verified by:* contract test with a NO_MATCH fixture. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > no-match is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > no-match-with-result is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > no-match-with-candidates is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was.
