- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-08** A `result` carries `kind`, required and one of the schema's listed values. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9, line 516); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* contract test (the values are not given in the sources; see gaps). *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > unknown-result-kind is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-client-default is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > gives the client’s default card outside the schedule (stored as MATCH_DEFAULT), and no card when there is none.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-09** A `result` carries `station` with its name, frequency and logo, and its public station reference (S3-RESPONSE-17); the app's "via <station>" line is built from them. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* contract test: the three station fields are present. *Built as:* S3-D20: the public station reference is the station id. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-with-images is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-10** A `result` carries `client` with its name and logo (supersedes blueprint §7 `media.brand_name`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 lines 237–238). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test compares both with what the station saved. *Built as:* S3-D21: logos are null until the portal can set them. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-11** A `result` carries the ad's `title` (supersedes blueprint §7 `media.title`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: `title` equals the ad's title. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-12** A `result` carries `card` with `schema_version`, `layout`, `header_image` and `actions[]`, and the card validates against the shared action-card schema (supersedes blueprint §7 `ui_schema`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* contract test: the card validates against `packages/contracts` action-card schema. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > a card sent to a listener keeps every rule of a saved card: a repeated button id, a short Zambian number, a link that is not canonical https, a shortener or a map at 0,0 is refused ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-13** A card's `header_image`, when present, carries its address, width, height and size in bytes (plan names `url`, `w`, `h`, `bytes`), so the app can reserve its space and count the data it costs (supersedes blueprint §7 `ui_schema.header_banner`, a bare URL). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9; §Mobile B, data saver); slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 line 243). *Verified by:* contract test: a card with a header image has all four fields. *Built as:* S3-D21: images are {address, width_pixels, height_pixels, size_bytes}; the API sends null until the portal can set them. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-with-images is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > header-image-not-https is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-14** The response body holds only the fields in the plan's response shape: no field named `tenant_id` (supersedes blueprint §7 `media.tenant_id`), and no internal id — no audio asset number or id, no campaign id, no install id, no match scores — and no station-internal field such as the engagement limit; the response schema forbids additional properties. The one station identifier allowed is the public station reference of S3-RESPONSE-17. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9; G3, G9); slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 line 239; G9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G9). *Verified by:* contract test: the schema has `additionalProperties: false`, has no `tenant_id` and no internal id field, and real API output validates against it for every status. Reworded in review round 1 (conflict 15). *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > tenant-id is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > audio-asset-number is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-scores is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > station-engagement-limit is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was.
- [ ] **S3-RESPONSE-15** The recognize response validates against `packages/contracts/recognize-response.schema.json`; the API and the app are both tested against that one schema and its shared fixtures, so the app renders any card without card-specific code. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10, §15 (plan §Step 3 API, line 167; §Repo layout); slice-blueprint.md §1 (blueprint §1 line 9). *Verified by:* API contract test validates real MATCH and NO_MATCH responses against the schema; app test parses the same shared fixtures. *Not ticked (partly built):* API half done: the committed recognize-response.schema.json equals the zod schema (recognition-schemas "%s matches its zod schema…") and real MATCH and NO_MATCH answers parse (recognize-route "answers MATCH…", "answers NO_MATCH…"); the app half (the listener app parsing the same shared fixtures) is Step 4.
- [x] **S3-RESPONSE-16** An engine outcome of AMBIGUOUS (enough aligned hashes but a margin below R) is stored as AMBIGUOUS and reported to the listener as `NO_MATCH` with no card; it is never a MATCH for either brand, and the word AMBIGUOUS never appears in a response. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8, §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; G9, G14); slice-blueprint.md §11 (G9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G9). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with the shared-jingle fixture: the response status is NO_MATCH with no card while the stored outcome is AMBIGUOUS. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers NO_MATCH for nothing matched and for an AMBIGUOUS result, storing each as what it was ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > ambiguous-status is refused by zod for its expected reason, and by the JSON Schema unless zod only.
- [ ] **S3-RESPONSE-17** Each `result.station` (and, from P4, each `CHOOSE_STATION` candidate) carries one public station reference — the station id or an opaque token, whichever the user chooses (gap 6) — and that same value is what the choice endpoint (`station_id`), the engagements endpoint (`station_id`) and the request's station hint accept. *Source:* plan §The listener's station choice `{station_id}` (line 520); plan §Engagements `{recognition_id, station_id, …}` (line 524); plan §Recognition flow step 1, `station_hint` (line 475); plan G9. *Verified by:* contract test: every `result.station` and candidate has the reference; API end-to-end test: the reference from a response, posted as the hint, the choice or an engagement, is accepted. Added in review round 1 from the rewrite of S3-RESPONSE-14 (conflict 15). *Not ticked (partly built):* S3-D20 makes the station id the reference; result.station.id is in the contract and in the MATCH answer (recognize-route "answers MATCH…"), and the hint accepts any station UUID (recognize-route "accepts a valid station hint, refuses one that is not a station id"); missing: an e2e posting a response's station id back as the hint; the choice and engagement endpoints are P4.

### Errors

- [x] **S3-ERRORS-01** A clip that passes the edge checks but is not usable audio (for example a valid `ftyp` signature with a corrupt body) is answered 400 `INVALID_AUDIO` with a request id. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9, line 517); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with a corrupt clip. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a clip that starts like an M4A but cannot be decoded is 400 INVALID_AUDIO from the real worker, recorded as such, with none of the decoder’s words.
- [ ] **S3-ERRORS-02** Every recognition error uses the one envelope `{error:{code,message,request_id}}` with exactly those three fields, and every code it uses (`INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID`, `INVALID_AUDIO`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `RECOGNITION_BUSY`, `RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT` and the 413 and 415 codes) is an entry in the API's error catalogue with a plain public message. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9; G9; §Cross-cutting, error model). *Verified by:* table-driven API test over every error case: exactly three fields, and the code is in the catalogue. *Not ticked (built differently):* the shared error envelope (Step 1, security baseline §2) adds fields to a validation error, so a 400 VALIDATION_FAILED has four members; every other recognition error has exactly code, message and request id, and every code is in the catalogue (recognize-route “every refusal is the shared error envelope …”).
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-03** Before the P4 time rules exist, a request whose capture time would break the database's capture-time check never surfaces as a database error or a 500: it is refused in the error envelope with a catalogue code, and nothing is queued. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (Foundation migration `recognition_events_captured_at_check`; global rules "Catch the technical type first"; plan §Cross-cutting, error model). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request with `captured_at` three days in the past returns a 4xx envelope with a catalogue code and a request id, and the body matches none of the leak patterns. *Built as:* S3-D15: clock correction and the 24 h / 2 min bounds are built now, so the refusal is 422 CAPTURE_EXPIRED / CAPTURE_TIME_INVALID before the claim. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > refuses a clip heard over a day ago or claiming a future time, in the envelope, with nothing queued or stored ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > refuses a clip heard more than 24 hours ago, and one from more than 2 minutes in the future.
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-04** When a job fails, the Python worker returns a stable error code from a known list, never the exception's text (`str(exc)`), class name or traceback; the job's failure reason holds only the code. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8, §14 (plan §Recognition flow step 7; §Step 3 workers; baseline §8; global rules "Two layers"); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §6 (plan line 626), §15 (baseline §8 row 1). *Verified by:* worker test: force a database error and a decode error; the returned value and failure reason hold only a known code and no traceback text (for example undecodable audio returns `INVALID_AUDIO`). *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_unexpected_error_is_logged_with_its_stack_and_answered_with_a_code_only ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_failures_are_one_code_each ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_job_the_wrapper_cannot_even_read_still_fails_with_a_code_and_a_log_line ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_decode.py :: test_error_text_is_only_the_code ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > a worker failure is answered with a code of ours: unreadable audio is 400 INVALID_AUDIO, anything else never shows the worker’s words.
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-05** The API turns a worker error code into a catalogue error; the worker's code text is never copied into the public message, and an unknown or internal code is answered with the generic 500 or 503 envelope, the code appearing only in the server log. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §8 (plan §Cross-cutting, error model; global rules "Two layers"). *Verified by:* API test with a job that returns an unknown code. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > a worker failure is answered with a code of ours: unreadable audio is 400 INVALID_AUDIO, anything else never shows the worker’s words ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the recognition circuit breaker > turns an unknown worker code into ENGINE_FAILED, so worker text never travels on.
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-06** On the Node side, BullMQ job errors (failed, timed out, worker missing) are caught, logged with the request id and stack trace, and answered with a catalogue code. *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §6 (plan §Cross-cutting, every boundary "BullMQ jobs on both sides"). *Verified by:* API test: a job the worker fails is answered with a catalogue code, and the log line has the request id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > a worker failure is answered with a code of ours: unreadable audio is 400 INVALID_AUDIO, anything else never shows the worker’s words ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-07** The Python workers install a process-level hook (`sys.excepthook`) that logs any uncaught exception as one structured JSON line with its stack trace. *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §3 (plan §Cross-cutting chokepoints, line 620); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (global rules "Fix it centrally", "Cover every boundary"). *Verified by:* pytest: an uncaught exception in a worker produces one JSON line with level error or critical and a traceback field. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_uncaught_errors_and_failed_background_tasks_become_structured_lines ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_unexpected_error_is_logged_with_its_stack_and_answered_with_a_code_only.
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-08** The Python workers install an asyncio loop exception handler that logs unhandled task errors as one structured JSON line with the stack trace and, where a job is active, its request id. *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §3 (plan line 620); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-recognition.md §21. *Verified by:* pytest: a task that raises without being awaited is logged as one JSON line with a traceback. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_failed_background_task_is_logged_once_with_its_own_request_id_and_stack ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_uncaught_errors_and_failed_background_tasks_become_structured_lines.
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-09** The public and private layers stay separate for queue work: the caller gets a code and a plain message, while the server log gets the full message, stack trace, route or job name, and request id. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (global rules "Two layers"; plan §Cross-cutting, error model). *Verified by:* for a forced worker failure, the HTTP body holds only the envelope while the log line with the same request id holds the stack trace. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > a worker failure is answered with a code of ours: unreadable audio is 400 INVALID_AUDIO, anything else never shows the worker’s words.
- [x] **S3-ERRORS-10** The fingerprint worker's database and key-value connection failures go through the central error path. At start-up a failure produces one structured JSON line with the stack trace, no connection string or password, and a non-zero exit. During a job it produces one structured line with the stack trace and the job's request id, and the job returns a stable code. *Source:* global rules §Fix it centrally ("Fix the chokepoints first — the global/uncaught handler and the DB-connection failure path"); plan §Cross-cutting, chokepoints "The DB connection-failure path, at boot and in the health check" (line 618). *Verified by:* process test: start the worker with Postgres unreachable → exactly one fatal JSON line with a stack, no password in the output, non-zero exit; stop Postgres during a job → the job result is a code and the log line carries the request id and the stack. (The API's version is S1-ERRORS-23 and S1-HEALTH-02.). *Built as:* Postgres is lost mid-job by ending the worker’s own connection (pg_terminate_backend), as a database restart would, rather than by stopping the shared container. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_connection_failures.py :: test_a_worker_that_cannot_reach_postgres_at_start_up_stops_with_one_fatal_line_and_no_password ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_connection_failures.py :: test_a_worker_that_cannot_reach_valkey_at_start_up_stops_with_one_fatal_line_and_no_password ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_connection_failures.py :: test_a_recognize_job_that_loses_the_database_fails_with_a_code_and_the_next_job_connects_again ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_connection_failures.py :: test_an_ingest_job_that_loses_the_database_fails_with_a_code_its_request_id_and_the_stack.

### Logging

- [x] **S3-LOGGING-01** The Python workers log with `structlog` as JSON, one object per line, to standard output, each line with timestamp, level and message, and with the stack trace on errors. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; §Cross-cutting, structured logs; global rules "Logging"); slice-blueprint.md §7; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §5 (plan line 622). *Verified by:* worker test capturing standard output: every line parses as JSON with those fields; an error line has a traceback. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_log_lines_are_json_with_time_level_message_and_the_jobs_request_id ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_an_unexpected_error_is_logged_with_its_stack_and_answered_with_a_code_only ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_uncaught_errors_and_failed_background_tasks_become_structured_lines.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-02** Every worker log line written while handling a job carries that job's request id, for both `recognize` and `ingest` jobs. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Step 3 workers; baseline §8); slice-blueprint.md §7 (global rules "Correlation id"); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §4 (plan line 621), §15 (baseline §8 row 1). *Verified by:* integration test: the request id returned by the API appears on the worker's log lines for that job. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_log_lines_are_json_with_time_level_message_and_the_jobs_request_id ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-03** The presigned download URL of an ingest job is never written to a log line, because it carries a token. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §14 (plan §Reused from Tonse-hub, Do differently; global rules "Never log PII or secrets"). *Verified by:* log-capture test on an ingest run: no line contains the URL's signature parameters. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_audio_and_download_addresses_never_reach_a_log_line_and_long_values_are_cut.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-04** Each admission refusal is logged on the server with the request id and its cause (breaker open, queue depth, missing heartbeat, and from P4 the in-flight limit), so the request id from a 503 finds the reason. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Step 3 tests "a request id that appears in the logs"; global rules "Correlation id"). *Verified by:* log-capture test: after a 503 the log has one JSON line with that request id and the cause. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with too many clips waiting: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > past the limit of recognitions at once on this instance, reading clips included, refuses the next at once as busy and takes clips again once places free up ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the circuit breaker > after repeated failures the breaker opens and clips are refused at once as busy.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-05** API and worker logs never contain listener audio, as bytes or as base64, after recognition or ingest runs. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3 Verify "no audio in logs"), §21 (baseline §4, §7); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §7 (plan line 635), §11 (baseline §4 row 4). *Verified by:* log-capture test with a known clip over API and worker output: no line contains any fragment of the clip's bytes or base64, and no line is longer than a set size. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > never logs the install token, the raw install id or the audio ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > leaves no listener audio and no recognize job in Valkey, and nothing in the workers’ temporary folders ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_audio_and_download_addresses_never_reach_a_log_line_and_long_values_are_cut.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-06** API and worker logs never contain the install token or the raw install id; an install appears only as a keyed-hash prefix. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (plan §Cross-cutting, structured logs; baseline §7); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §7 (plan line 635). *Verified by:* log-capture test across install registration and recognize: the token string and the raw install id sent by the test client appear in no line. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > never logs the install token, the raw install id or the audio ; apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > stores only a keyed hash of the install id, with the platform and the app version, and no verdict ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the fingerprint jobs > a recognize job holds exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, the deadline and the fingerprint version.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-07** Request bodies of the recognize route are never logged, even with request-detail logging (`LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS`) switched on. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (baseline §7; global rules "Never log PII or secrets"). *Verified by:* log-capture test with `LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS` on: no line contains multipart content. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/log-request-details.test.ts :: request logging with LOG_REQUEST_DETAILS on > a listener’s recognize request logs neither the clip nor its fields nor the install token, even with request details on.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-08** Before the workers' logging is switched on, the existing engine call sites are checked for what they will start to emit, so no audio, path or parameter dump reaches production logs. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (global rules "Before making logging louder"). *Verified by:* review note at the gate, plus the log-capture tests above. *Built as:* review note in the gate record in docs/design/step-3-engine-hookup.md. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_engine_rules.py :: test_the_engine_writes_nothing_and_the_workers_write_only_through_worker_logging ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_audio_and_download_addresses_never_reach_a_log_line_and_long_values_are_cut ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > never logs the install token, the raw install id or the audio.
- [x] **S3-LOGGING-09** Searching the logs for the request id of a 503 returned while the workers are stopped finds the full private error in the structured logs (MVP step 8; slice step 6). *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 8), §30 (plan §Verification at the end of the slice step 6); slice-plan-recognition.md §16. *Verified by:* log-capture test: the captured log line with that request id holds the message and stack trace. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.

### Privacy

- [x] **S3-PRIVACY-01** A listener's clip is never stored on the server: not in the database, not in object storage, not on disk and not in logs; it is discarded after matching (kept from blueprint §8.1 "raw audio binaries are immediately discarded post-processing"). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §12 (blueprint §8 line 281; baseline §4); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (Holds); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §11 (baseline §4 row 4, "Planned, step 3"). *Verified by:* end-to-end test after a run of recognitions: no listener clip object in storage, no audio column in any table, no temporary file, no job key in Valkey and no audio in captured logs. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S3-PRIVACY-01: no table can hold a clip: the only binary columns are keyed hashes, checksums and the encrypted two-factor secret ; apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S3-PRIVACY-01: nothing on the listener path can write a file or store a clip: no file system or object storage in the recognition, installs and queue code ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > leaves no listener audio and no recognize job in Valkey, and nothing in the workers’ temporary folders ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > never logs the install token, the raw install id or the audio ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_workers.py :: test_a_listener_clip_never_touches_the_disk.

### Database functions and privileges

- [x] **S3-DATABASE-01** The recognition idempotency-key table (`app.recognition_idempotency_keys`) is touched only by a narrow SECURITY DEFINER function added with recognition in Step 3; the API role has no direct privilege on it. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (baseline §1, "functions: Next / step 3"; Foundation migration comment); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §8 (baseline §1 row 7). *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`: as `lookup_api`, a direct select, insert, update or delete on the table is refused or sees nothing, while the function works. *Built as:* S3-D16: the functions are recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition; UPDATE is not asserted directly but row-level security with no policy leaves it nothing to see (as accepted for S1-PRIVILEGES-10). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > the API role reaches the keys and events only through these functions, and the worker not at all ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: station isolation and role privileges, table by table > what each role may do > S1-PRIVILEGES-10: the API role can neither read nor write recognition idempotency keys directly ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > row-level security > S1-ISOLATION-04: row-level security is on for every table except the listed global ones, and every table with a station has a station policy ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > a new key makes one PENDING recognition; finishing it sets the outcome and when it completed.
- [x] **S3-DATABASE-02** Every SECURITY DEFINER function this step adds (`recognition.resolve`, `recognition.record_recognition`, the idempotency-key function and `fingerprints.mark_ingested`; and in P4 the claim, choice and engagement functions) pins `search_path` and returns only the columns its caller needs. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (baseline §1; memory/security-by-design.md). *Verified by:* database test for each function: a fixed `search_path` with `pg_catalog` first and `pg_temp` last, and returned columns that match an allow-list (the existing check in apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts extends to them). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > each Step 3 function returns only what its caller needs, and only that caller may run it ; apps/api/test/portal-sessions-database.test.ts :: sign-in and session functions > privileges > every SECURITY DEFINER function pins its search path with pg_catalog first and pg_temp last.

### Names and structure

- [x] **S3-STRUCTURE-01** Names this step introduces are full words with units, following conventions: the plan's short forms `recog`, `fp`, `asset_key`, `clip_ms`, `offset_ms`, `audioB64`, `fpVersion`, `t_off`, `w` and `h` do not ship in code, the database, queue job fields or the request and response schemas. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (plan §Standing requirements; conventions §Names in code and §Names in the database; memory/readable-names-and-structure.md). *Verified by:* scan of `apps/`, `services/`, `packages/` and the migrations for those short forms returns nothing. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S3-STRUCTURE-01: none of the plan’s short names survive in the API, the portal, the contracts or the workers ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S1-NAMING-01, S1-NAMING-02, S1-MIGRATIONS-06: schemas app, fingerprints and recognition exist, owned by the schema owner; fp and recog do not.
- [x] **S3-STRUCTURE-02** Yes-or-no fields read as questions: `is_deferred` rather than `deferred`, and `is_in_window` and `is_chosen` rather than the plan's `in_window` and `chosen`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (conventions "Booleans read as yes/no questions"; `is_` prefix in the database). *Verified by:* catalogue test: every new yes-or-no column starts with `is_`; contract schema review. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > names > S3-STRUCTURE-02: every yes-or-no column and function output is named as a question (is_ or has_).
- [ ] **S3-STRUCTURE-03** New database functions are verb phrases in the `recognition` and `fingerprints` schemas, and new constraints, indexes and policies follow the naming patterns in conventions. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (conventions §Names in the database). *Verified by:* catalogue test: every new object's name matches its pattern. *Not ticked (built differently):* S3-D4 puts the sweep's function in app (app.find_ads_waiting_for_ingest), not in recognition or fingerprints; constraint names are covered for every schema by database-catalogue S1-NAMING-07, but no test checks that function names are verb phrases or that new indexes and policies follow the patterns.
- [ ] **S3-STRUCTURE-04** The API side of this step is laid out as feature folders under `apps/api/src/features/` (recognition, listener installs), each with its own module imported once in `app.module.ts`; queue and storage clients live under `infrastructure/`, and no feature imports from another feature's folder. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (plan §Standing requirements; conventions §Structure (API)); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §8 (plan §Repo layout, apps/api). *Verified by:* structure review and an import scan. *Not ticked (partly built):* features/recognition and features/listener-installs each have one module imported once in app.module.ts, and the queue clients live in infrastructure/job-queues; no import-scan test exists, and the no-cross-feature-import rule is broken (see GAP).
- [x] **S3-STRUCTURE-05** `packages/contracts` holds the JSON Schemas for the recognize request and the recognize response. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §8 (plan §Repo layout, packages/contracts). *Verified by:* contract test: recognize responses from the API validate against `recognize-response.schema.json`, loaded from the package. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the committed JSON Schemas > recognize-response.schema.json matches its zod schema — run `npm run emit-json-schemas` after changing it ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the committed JSON Schemas > recognize-request-fields.schema.json matches its zod schema — run `npm run emit-json-schemas` after changing it ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the recognize response > match-campaign is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > answers MATCH with the station, the client, the title and exactly the saved buttons, in the contract’s shape.
- [x] **S3-STRUCTURE-06** The Python worker tests use the same shared fixtures from `packages/contracts`. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §8 (plan §Repo layout, packages/contracts). *Verified by:* test review: worker result tests load fixture files from `packages/contracts`. *Evidence:* services/fingerprinter/tests/test_job_fixtures.py :: test_a_valid_shared_fixture_fits_its_contract ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_job_fixtures.py :: test_an_invalid_shared_fixture_is_refused_by_its_contract ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_job_fixtures.py :: test_the_recognize_worker_answers_a_shared_job_with_a_shared_result ; services/fingerprinter/tests/test_job_fixtures.py :: test_the_ingest_worker_answers_a_shared_job_with_a_shared_result ; packages/contracts/test/job-fixtures.test.ts :: the $folder fixtures > %s is valid for zod and for the JSON Schema.

### Security baseline

- [ ] **S3-SECURITY-01** The security baseline's Step 3 rows move to "Built", each naming a passing test: install token and per-install rate limits; worker without storage credentials; decoding caps and clip deletion; request id in worker logs and code-only failures; job deadline and removal; the worker's single status function; the idempotency-key functions. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (baseline §1, §3, §4, §8; plan §Standing requirements; §Execution, gate). *Verified by:* gate check: each of those rows in `docs/security-baseline.md` names a passing test file. *Not ticked (checked at the gate; no test can hold it):* every Step 3 row of docs/security-baseline.md is Built and names test files that exist and pass, recorded in the gate record in docs/design/step-3-engine-hookup.md.

### Tests and gate

- [x] **S3-TESTS-01** Cross-language contract test: a job added by Node (`add` plus `waitUntilFinished`) is processed by the Python worker on Valkey 8, and its result resolves on the Node side. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16 (plan §Step 3 tests; §Phases row 1 Verify; §Verified facts, BullMQ); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §2 (Pinned stack, Worker row); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §23 (plan §Phases row 1 Verify); slice-blueprint.md §2. *Verified by:* the test starts a real Python worker and a real Node queue against the compose Valkey and asserts the returned value. *Built as:* S3-D3: Node uses RecognizeQueue.addAndWait (its own QueueEvents listener), not job.waitUntilFinished. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > the queue contract: a job Node adds is answered by Python in the contract’s shape, and one past its deadline is dropped undecoded.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-02** The same contract test proves that a timeout rejects: when the worker does not answer in time (paused or not running), the Node wait fails instead of hanging. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §2; slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §23. *Verified by:* the test pauses the worker and asserts the wait rejects after the limit. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [ ] **S3-TESTS-03** Because the Python `bullmq` package is still classed "Alpha", the cross-language contract test is blocking, not optional: it runs in the default test command and a failure fails the build. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §5 (plan §Verified facts, BullMQ, line 256). *Verified by:* test-run check: the default command runs it, and a deliberately broken worker makes the run fail. *Not ticked (checked at the gate; no test can hold it):* the cross-language tests (apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts) run in the default npm test and cannot be skipped (source-scan “no unit or API test is skipped or singled out …”); a run with a deliberately broken recognize worker failed with exit code 1 and “the recognize worker stopped while starting”, recorded in the gate record in docs/design/step-3-engine-hookup.md.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-04** API end-to-end test with real audio files: upload a synthetic ad through the real upload route to MinIO, run the real ingest worker, and see the ad reach READY. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16 (plan §Step 3 tests, line 171). *Verified by:* the test polls the ad until READY. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an uploaded ad is fingerprinted by the Python ingest worker and becomes READY, with exactly the hashes the engine makes for it.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-05** The same test posts a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of that ad and gets MATCH with Station A's card, whose buttons equal the ones saved for the ad (Call, WhatsApp and Map in the slice walk-through; slice step 4). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16 (plan §Step 3 tests; §Verification at the end of the slice step 4); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §30 (slice step 4). *Verified by:* the response status is MATCH and the card's buttons equal the saved ones. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > a phone-like 8-second AAC clip of the published ad gets MATCH with exactly the station’s saved buttons.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-06** The same suite posts an unrelated clip and gets NO_MATCH, with a `recognition_id` and no card (MVP step 6: unknown audio gives No match). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16 (plan §Step 3 tests); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 6). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test with an unrelated clip. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > an unrelated clip gets NO_MATCH, with a recognition id and no card.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-07** The same suite posts a matching clip outside the ad's schedule and gets the fallback: the client's default card, not the campaign card. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16 (plan §Step 3 tests "outside the schedule → fallback"). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > outside its schedule the clip gets the client’s default card.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-08** The same suite stops the worker and gets 503 with a request id in the envelope, and that request id appears in a captured server log line holding the full private detail (MVP step 8; slice step 6). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16 (plan §Step 3 tests; §Verification at the end of the slice step 6); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 8), §30 (slice step 6). *Verified by:* the test asserts status 503, the envelope, and a log line with the same request id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-09** In the worker-stopped case no response body leaks internals: it matches none of the leak patterns (exception class names, SQL state codes, "Fatal error", file paths, stack text, `Traceback`, queue or host names). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §16 (plan §Verification at the end of the slice step 6; global rules "Tests"); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §30 (slice step 6). *Verified by:* the shared leak scanner run over the 503 body. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
