- [x] **S3-TESTS-16** With the workers paused, the API answers 503 within 2.5 seconds plus a small stated margin. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Verify); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* end-to-end test measuring the response time. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-17** After a recognition run, no job keys remain in the key-value store. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Verify). *Verified by:* end-to-end test: a key scan for the `recognize` queue's job keys returns none. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > leaves no listener audio and no recognize job in Valkey, and nothing in the workers’ temporary folders ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > sends the worker exactly the request id, the clip, the codec, a 2.5-second deadline and the fingerprint version, tried once and removed when done.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-18** After a recognition run, no temporary files remain on the API or the worker. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Verify); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* end-to-end test: the temporary directories of both processes hold no files created during the run. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/source-scan.test.ts :: source scan > S3-PRIVACY-01: nothing on the listener path can write a file or store a clip: no file system or object storage in the recognition, installs and queue code ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > leaves no listener audio and no recognize job in Valkey, and nothing in the workers’ temporary folders.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-19** The leak regression suite injects a Python traceback from the worker and checks the API response: it carries a catalogue code and a request id, and no `Traceback`, file path or exception text. *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §7 (plan §Cross-cutting, leak regression suite, line 632); slice-plan-recognition.md §21. *Verified by:* `error-responses.test.ts` (or a new file) case against `POST /api/v1/recognize`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > a worker failure is answered with a code of ours: unreadable audio is 400 INVALID_AUDIO, anything else never shows the worker’s words.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-20** The leak regression suite sends a malformed (truncated) multipart body to `POST /api/v1/recognize`: the answer is a 400 envelope with no parser text. *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §7 (plan line 632 "bad multipart"); slice-plan-recognition.md §21. *Verified by:* API test case in the leak suite. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > answers a cut-off multipart body with 400 and none of the parser’s words.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-21** The leak regression suite sends an oversize audio upload (over 256 kilobytes) to `POST /api/v1/recognize`: the answer is a 413 envelope with no parser text. *Source:* slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §7 (plan line 632 "oversize"); slice-plan-recognition.md §21. *Verified by:* API test case in the leak suite. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > takes a clip of exactly 256 KiB and refuses one byte more with 413, before any job ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > cuts a multi-megabyte upload off once it passes the limit, without reading the rest.
- [x] **S3-TESTS-22** Log-capture tests for recognition exist and run in the standard test command; they assert that the stack trace and the request id are present on error lines, and that no raw install id (across install registration and recognize) and no audio appear. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (plan §Cross-cutting, leak regression suite, line 635); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §7 (plan line 635). *Verified by:* the log-capture tests are listed in the standard test run and pass. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > never logs the install token, the raw install id or the audio ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > with the recognize worker stopped: first 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT (its heartbeat is still fresh), whose request id finds the full detail in the log; then 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY at once ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > a worker failure is answered with a code of ours: unreadable audio is 400 INVALID_AUDIO, anything else never shows the worker’s words ; apps/api/test/listener-installs.test.ts :: listener app installs > stores only a keyed hash of the install id, with the platform and the app version, and no verdict.
- [ ] **S3-TESTS-23** Slice walk-through in the portal: sign in as Station A, create an ad for "Brand A", upload a synthetic ad made with the benchmark's synthetic-audio module, watch it move from Processing to Ready, set Call, WhatsApp and Map buttons, schedule it for now, and publish. *Source:* slice-plan-portal.md §9 (plan §Verification at the end of the slice step 3); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §30 (slice step 3.2). *Verified by:* manual walk-through recorded with screenshots, then the same journey automated in Playwright with the ingest worker running. *Not ticked (partly built):* apps/portal/e2e/new-ad-journey.spec.ts "a station puts an ad on air…" runs with the workers and covers Brand A, upload, Processing → "Ready for listeners", Call/WhatsApp/Directions (+Website), schedule and publish; differences: the audio is the API's jingle (apps/api/scripts/sample-audio.ts), not the benchmark's synthetic-audio module; the schedule is 07:00–09:00, not "now"; no manual walk-through with screenshots is recorded.
- [ ] **S3-TESTS-24** Gate: the recognition flow is built with its rate limits, circuit breaker, heartbeats and match thresholds (master Phase 3 "Build"), and every S3 item in this checklist is ticked with its named test or explicitly deferred with a reason the user has seen. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Build), §21 (plan §Execution "Nothing overlooked"; memory/nothing-overlooked.md). *Verified by:* gate check against this checklist. *Not ticked (checked at the gate; no test can hold it):* every S3 item is ticked with its named test or shown to the user with its reason in the Step 3 gate report, recorded in the gate record in docs/design/step-3-engine-hookup.md.

---

## P4 — Station resolution and scheduler, full

*Changed on 11 October 2026, before P4 Piece 2 (§8.4 of the P4 design, docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md; P4-D36 and ALL-CHECKLIST-06): 19 items the design adds are listed in their areas, each marked "Added on 11 October 2026"; the items the user's answers of 11 October 2026 change are reworded, deferred or retired, each naming the answer. Nothing was ticked by this change.*

### Capture time and receipt time

- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-01** Every recognition keeps two separate times, `captured_at` (when the clip was heard, normalised by the server) and `received_at` (when the server first got it), and the server never substitutes one for the other. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule, lines 470–472; §Data model row recognition_events). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a deferred clip captured two hours before it is sent produces a row whose two times differ by about two hours. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-TIMERULE-01: a clip heard 3 hours before it was sent keeps both times, about 3 hours apart.
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-02** The grace period is measured around the capture time, not the receipt time. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule, line 471). *Verified by:* table-driven database test: a clip captured 5 minutes after a window's end and received 3 hours later is in window; a clip captured 3 hours after the end is out of window whatever its receipt time. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/time-windows-with-grace-database.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-02: the grace is measured around the capture time: heard 5 minutes after a slot and received 3 hours later is on air; heard 3 hours after it is not.
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-03** Which stations are in-window candidates is decided at the capture time, not the receipt time. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule; step 8, line 509). *Verified by:* database test with two stations whose windows differ: the in-window set for a deferred clip equals the set whose windows contained the capture time. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > recognition.find_station_candidates > lists every station that owns the audio, each with its own card and its client’s default card ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > a clip of an ad that is on air > judges "on air" at the moment the clip was heard, in the station’s time zone, not when it arrived.
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-04** Engagement limits are checked at the receipt time: a clip captured before the limit was reached but received after it is treated as over the limit. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule, line 472; step 8, line 513). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: fill a campaign's limit, then send a deferred clip captured before it was reached; the response carries the client's default card. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-TIMERULE-04: a clip heard before the limit was reached but received after it gets the client’s usual card
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-05** Station status is checked at the receipt time: a station that was ACTIVE when the clip was captured but is not ACTIVE when it is received does not get its campaign card shown. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule; step 8, line 513), §9 (step 8). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: capture while the station is active, suspend it, then send the clip; the suspended station's card is not in the response (see gaps on whether a default card may be shown instead). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-05: a clip heard while the station was active and sent after it was suspended gets no buttons ; apps/api/test/time-rule-split.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-08 (resolveStation): the station status follows the processing time: suspended after the clip was heard, no buttons at all.
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-06** Campaign status is checked at the receipt time: a campaign that was ACTIVE at capture but is PAUSED or ENDED at receipt does not get its campaign card shown, and the client's default card is used (O13). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1, §9, §20 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule and step 8; O13); slice-blueprint.md §8 (plan O13); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O13). *Verified by:* API end-to-end and database tests: capture inside the window, pause the campaign, then send the deferred clip; the response carries the client's default card. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-06: a clip heard on air and sent after the campaign was paused gets the client’s usual buttons; one heard after the pause gets none ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-06, P4-TIMERULE-09: a campaign paused or ended after the clip was heard is still a candidate, no longer active; one paused before is none.
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-07** The card version returned is the one current at the receipt time, not the one current at the capture time. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1, §9 (plan §Recognition flow, time rule; step 8, line 513). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: capture a clip, edit the card (a new version), then send the deferred clip; the response carries the new version's buttons; a new recognition after an edit returns the newest version. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-07: a clip heard before the card was edited and sent after gets the new buttons ; apps/api/test/time-rule-split.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-08 (resolveStation): the card version follows the processing time: edited after the clip was heard, the new buttons.
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-08** A table-driven test suite proves the split: windows, active period and candidates follow `captured_at`; limits, station suspension and card version follow `received_at`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §1, §19 (plan §Phases row 4, Verify); slice-blueprint.md §8 (plan §Phases row 4 Verify). *Verified by:* the named suite runs in the standard test command and has at least one case for each of the six items in which the two times lead to different answers. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/time-rule-split.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-08: the time window follows the capture time: heard in its slot, processed after the slot ended, the clip is on air ; apps/api/test/time-rule-split.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-08: the tap limit follows the processing time: reached after the clip was heard, it is reached for this clip ; apps/api/test/time-rule-split.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-08 (resolveStation): the candidates follow the capture time: the campaign paused after the clip was heard still answers, with the client’s usual buttons.
- [x] **P4-TIMERULE-09** The database records when each campaign's status last changed (`campaigns.status_changed_at`); a campaign that is PAUSED or ENDED with its status changed after the capture still makes its station an owner, shown with the client's default card (O13), while one paused before the capture is not a candidate. A station's campaigns fold into one candidate in this order: the campaign in window whose slot started last; else the one whose slot ended last within 7 days; else the latest period start not after the capture; else the first to start; then the campaign id. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §4.1, §5.1, P4-D4; user's answer, 11 October 2026 (P4 design Q3). *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts`: a campaign paused after the capture is a candidate with `is_campaign_active` false, one paused before it is not, and `status_changed_at` moves only when the status changes; `apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts`: the fold order, including a campaign of another client that starts in 3 days. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36); the fold order was amended after review, because "the latest period start" could pick a campaign of another client that has not started yet. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-06, P4-TIMERULE-09: a campaign paused or ended after the clip was heard is still a candidate, no longer active; one paused before is none ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-09: when a campaign status changed is recorded by the database alone: it moves only when the status changes, and no caller can set it ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: none on air: the campaign whose slot ended last within 7 days, so a campaign of another client that starts in 3 days never stands in for the one just on air ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: a station’s two campaigns on the recording fold into one candidate: on air, the one whose slot started last.

### Clock correction and time bounds

- [x] **P4-CLOCK-01** The server corrects the phone's clock (O11): effective capture time = `captured_at` + (`received_at` − `sent_at`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §18, §20 (plan §Recognition flow step 2, line 483; O11; §Phases row 3 Verify); slice-blueprint.md §11 (O11); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O11). *Verified by:* unit test with a phone clock 3 hours slow, 3 hours fast and exact; API end-to-end test: a clip from a phone 3 hours slow is accepted (not `CAPTURE_EXPIRED`), resolves against the right window, and is stored within seconds of the true time. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > is corrected by how far the phone clock is off, judged from when it says it sent the clip ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > P4-CLOCK-01: is corrected the same way for a phone 3 hours fast, and left as it is for an exact clock ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-CLOCK-01, -02: a phone 3 hours slow is judged against the window it was really heard in, and stored within seconds of the true time.
- [x] **P4-CLOCK-02** The corrected capture time, not the phone's raw value, is what is stored in `captured_at` and passed to station resolution. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 8; §Data model row recognition_events "server-normalised clip start"); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (row recognition_events). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: with a skewed phone clock the stored `captured_at` equals the corrected value and the schedule decision matches the true capture time. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-CLOCK-01, -02: a phone 3 hours slow is judged against the window it was really heard in, and stored within seconds of the true time.
- [x] **P4-CLOCK-03** The phone's own capture time is kept unchanged on the recognition row for audit (plan name `client_captured_at`). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Data model row recognition_events); slice-blueprint.md §10; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* schema test: the column exists; API test: it holds the value the app sent. *Built as:* S3-D15: column client_captured_at. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > corrects the capture time by the phone’s clock: a phone 3 hours slow still records when it was really heard.
- [x] **P4-CLOCK-04** The measured clock skew is kept on the recognition row for audit, in milliseconds with the unit in the column name (plan name `clock_skew_ms`), equal to `received_at` − `sent_at`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3; slice-blueprint.md §10; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* database test after a request from a skewed clock. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: P4-CLOCK-04: a request from a phone 3 hours slow keeps its measured clock skew on the row, exactly the receipt time minus the phone’s send time ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-CLOCK-04: stores the exact clock skew of a phone 30 days off, beyond what an integer holds.
- [x] **P4-CLOCK-05** A clip whose effective capture time is more than 24 hours before receipt is answered 422 `CAPTURE_EXPIRED`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §10, §18 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 9; §Phases row 3 Verify); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end boundary test: 24 hours minus one second is accepted; 24 hours plus one second (and 25 hours) returns 422 with that code and a request id. *Built as:* S3-D15: brought forward from P4; the exact bounds are unit-tested, the route's 422 at 3 days. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > refuses a clip heard more than 24 hours ago, and one from more than 2 minutes in the future ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > refuses a clip heard over a day ago or claiming a future time, in the envelope, with nothing queued or stored.
- [x] **P4-CLOCK-06** A clip whose effective capture time is more than 2 minutes after receipt is answered 422 `CAPTURE_TIME_INVALID` (O12). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3, §10, §18, §20 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 9; O12); slice-blueprint.md §11 (O12); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O12). *Verified by:* API end-to-end boundary test: 2 minutes minus one second ahead is accepted; 2 minutes plus one second (and 3 minutes) ahead returns 422 with that code. *Built as:* S3-D15: brought forward from P4; the exact bounds are unit-tested, the route's CAPTURE_TIME_INVALID at +5 min. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > refuses a clip heard more than 24 hours ago, and one from more than 2 minutes in the future ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > refuses a clip heard over a day ago or claiming a future time, in the envelope, with nothing queued or stored.
- [x] **P4-CLOCK-07** `CAPTURE_EXPIRED` and `CAPTURE_TIME_INVALID` are permanent: sending the same clip again gets the same answer, and no queue job is created for either. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2, line 484); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the same expired clip sent twice returns 422 both times and the queue's job count is unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-CLOCK-07: the same expired clip sent twice gets 422 CAPTURE_EXPIRED both times, and nothing is queued or stored.
- [x] **P4-CLOCK-08** The time bounds the API enforces match the database check on `recognition_events` (capture time between receipt minus 24 hours and receipt plus 2 minutes), so a clip just outside either bound gets a 422 envelope, never a 500. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Data model row recognition_events CHECK; §Recognition flow step 2). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test at both bounds; database test: a direct insert outside the bounds is refused by `recognition_events_captured_at_check`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-CLOCK-08: at exactly 24 hours old and exactly 2 minutes ahead a clip is taken and stored; a millisecond past either is a 422, never a 500 ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > the rules the tables keep > P4-CLOCK-08: a recognition heard more than 24 hours before it arrived, or more than 2 minutes after, is refused by recognition_events_captured_at_check; the exact bounds are kept ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > refuses a clip heard more than 24 hours ago, and one from more than 2 minutes in the future.

### Deferred clips

- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-01** A recognition is marked deferred when the app says so (`deferred` is true). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2, line 485); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a request with the flag true and a fresh capture time is stored and answered with `is_deferred` true. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-01: a send the app flags as deferred, with a fresh capture time, is stored and answered as deferred ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-22: a clip that went Busy as a live send and is then sent from the outbox is stored and answered as deferred.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-02** A recognition is also marked deferred when receipt is more than 120 seconds after the effective capture time, even if the app's flag is false. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: flag false with a gap of 121 seconds gives `is_deferred` true; 119 seconds gives false; the response and the stored flag agree. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-02: unflagged, a clip that arrives 121 seconds after it was heard is deferred and one at 119 seconds is not; the answer and the row agree ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > counts a clip as deferred when the app says so, or when it arrives over two minutes after it was heard.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-03** Deferred requests have their own per-install allowance, separate from the live one (plan example: 10 per hour), so deferred sending never uses up the live allowance, nor the reverse. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow step 2, line 481); slice-blueprint.md §3 (O14). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: after the deferred allowance is used up a live request from the same install still succeeds, and after the live allowance is used up a deferred request still succeeds. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-03, -04, -10: deferred sends have their own allowance (4 an hour here): the 5th new key gets 429 while a live send goes on, and the reverse.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-04** A deferred request over the deferred allowance is answered 429 `RATE_LIMITED`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §3 (plan §Recognition flow steps 2 and 9). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the request after the last allowed deferred one in the hour returns 429 with that code. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-03, -04, -10: deferred sends have their own allowance (4 an hour here): the 5th new key gets 429 while a live send goes on, and the reverse.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-05** Under load, deferred requests are refused before live ones: there is a load level at which a deferred request gets 503 while a live request is still admitted. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 4, line 496); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API test: with the queue depth between the deferred threshold and the live threshold, a deferred request returns 503 and a live request is processed. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-05: with the queue depth between the deferred limit (20) and the live limit (50), a deferred send gets 503 and a live one is processed ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-ADMISSION-02, P4-DEFERRED-05: with the deferred places held by jobs a worker keeps, a third deferred send gets 503 while a live send is still admitted.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-06** A deferred request that is shed gets a `Retry-After` between 60 and 300 seconds, randomised (jittered) for each response. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 4); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API test: 50 shed deferred requests all have a header value within 60 to 300, and the values are not all equal. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-ADMISSION-02, P4-DEFERRED-06: fifty deferred sends refused for lack of a deferred place each get a Retry-After within 60–300, not all equal, and none is counted against the listener.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-07** A deferred recognize job is enqueued with lower priority than live jobs, so a waiting live job is always picked first. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5, line 499); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* integration test: with the worker paused, enqueue a deferred job and then a live job; on resume the live job is processed first. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-queue-priority.test.ts :: the recognize queue: deferred jobs wait behind live ones > P4-DEFERRED-07: with no worker running, a deferred job and then a live job are added; a worker started afterwards takes the live job first ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > P4-DEFERRED-07 with the real worker: a deferred job added first and a live job added after are taken live first by a Python worker started afterwards.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-08** A deferred recognize job's deadline is 5 seconds (5,000 milliseconds) after it is enqueued. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §6 (plan §Recognition flow step 5); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* unit test: for a deferred request the deadline minus the enqueue time is 5,000 milliseconds. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-queue-priority.test.ts :: the recognize queue: deferred jobs wait behind live ones > P4-DEFERRED-08: through the route, a deferred send’s job has priority 10 and a deadline 5,000 ms after it was added; a live send’s has no priority and 2,500 ms.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-09** The API waits for a deferred job's result for at most 5,000 milliseconds. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §7 (plan §Recognition flow step 6, line 500); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API test with a worker that never answers: a deferred request returns after about 5 seconds, a live one after about 2.5. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-09: with a worker that never answers, a deferred send gives up after about 5 seconds and a live one after about 2.5.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-10** The deferred rate limit and the shedding thresholds are read from validated configuration, starting from the plan's example numbers until the load test sets them (O14). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §20 (plan O14; §Recognition flow steps 2 and 4). *Verified by:* configuration test: changing the configured values changes behaviour. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > P4-DEFERRED-10, P4 design §5.11: the settings for clips sent later, the update gate and re-registration default to the plan’s numbers ; apps/api/test/startup-checks.test.ts :: production configuration fails closed > P4-DEFERRED-10, P4 design §5.11: each of those settings is refused out of its range, and refused when it breaks its rule against another ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-03, -04, -10: deferred sends have their own allowance (4 an hour here): the 5th new key gets 429 while a live send goes on, and the reverse.
- [ ] **P4-DEFERRED-11** The final deferred rate-limit and shedding numbers are set from the recognition load-test results and written down (O14). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §20 (plan O14; §Phases row 3 Verify); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O14). *Verified by:* review: an ADR or configuration change cites the load-test report, and the configured values match it. *Deferred to P8 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q10, P4-D22):* the final numbers come from the P8 load tests on Render; until then the plan's example numbers stay the configured defaults (deferred 10 an hour, queue depth 20 against 50, `Retry-After` 60–300 seconds, a 5-second wait).
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-12** Gate: master Phase 3 also builds the deferred-clip rules (clock correction, the 24-hour and 2-minute bounds, the deferred flag, the deferred allowance, shedding, priority and the 5-second deadline), and each is ticked with its named test. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Build "deferred-clip rules"). *Verified by:* gate check against this checklist. *Built as:* in P4 piece 4 (13 October 2026) the deferred allowance, the replay allowance, the deferred share of the places in flight, shedding with a jittered `Retry-After`, the lower priority, the 5-second wait and the deferred breaker joined Step 3's clock correction, bounds and flag; each rule is ticked with its own test (P4-CLOCK-01 to -08, P4-DEFERRED-01 to -10, -13, -14, P4-ADMISSION-02, -03); the final numbers wait for P8 (P4-DEFERRED-11). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > is corrected by how far the phone clock is off, judged from when it says it sent the clip ; apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: the capture time > P4-CLOCK-01: is corrected the same way for a phone 3 hours fast, and left as it is for an exact clock ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-CLOCK-01, -02: a phone 3 hours slow is judged against the window it was really heard in, and stored within seconds of the true time ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-01: a send the app flags as deferred, with a fresh capture time, is stored and answered as deferred ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-22: a clip that went Busy as a live send and is then sent from the outbox is stored and answered as deferred ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-03, -04, -10: deferred sends have their own allowance (4 an hour here): the 5th new key gets 429 while a live send goes on, and the reverse ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-ADMISSION-02, P4-DEFERRED-06: fifty deferred sends refused for lack of a deferred place each get a Retry-After within 60–300, not all equal, and none is counted against the listener ; apps/api/test/recognize-queue-priority.test.ts :: the recognize queue: deferred jobs wait behind live ones > P4-DEFERRED-07: with no worker running, a deferred job and then a live job are added; a worker started afterwards takes the live job first ; apps/api/test/fingerprint-workers.test.ts :: the fingerprint workers, end to end > P4-DEFERRED-07 with the real worker: a deferred job added first and a live job added after are taken live first by a Python worker started afterwards ; apps/api/test/recognize-queue-priority.test.ts :: the recognize queue: deferred jobs wait behind live ones > P4-DEFERRED-08: through the route, a deferred send’s job has priority 10 and a deadline 5,000 ms after it was added; a live send’s has no priority and 2,500 ms.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-13** Every text field of a recognize request comes before the audio part: an audio part that arrives before all the text fields, or a text part after the audio part, is answered 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` with `{path: "audio", code: "audio_part_before_fields"}`. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.2, contract §1.2, P4-D14. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/recognize-request-order.test.ts`: an audio part before the fields gives 400 `audio_part_before_fields`. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-request-order.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, the order of checks > P4-DEFERRED-13: an audio part before the text fields, or any part after the audio, is 400 audio_part_before_fields.
- [x] **P4-DEFERRED-14** A send of an idempotency key the install already holds, with fewer than 5 attempts, is counted against a replay allowance (`RECOGNITION_REPLAYS_PER_INSTALL_PER_HOUR`, 60 an hour), not against the live or the deferred allowance; past it, 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with the seconds left. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.2, P4-D14. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts`: a held key resent after a 503 is not counted against the live or deferred allowance; the 61st send of held keys in an hour gets 429 and the live allowance is untouched. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36); amended after review, because a replay of a finished key never claims, so its attempt count never grows and one install could otherwise replay without limit. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-14: a key resent after a 503 counts against the replay allowance, not the deferred one, until its fifth attempt ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-DEFERRED-14: replays of a finished key count against the replay allowance: the 61st send of held keys in an hour gets 429 with the seconds left, and the live allowance is untouched.

### Idempotency

- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-01** Before any queue work, the API claims the request through one database function (plan name `recog.claim`) that inserts a recognition row with outcome `PENDING`, and does nothing if the key already exists. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3, line 486). *Verified by:* database test: the first call for an (install, key) pair inserts one `PENDING` row and reports "claimed"; a second call inserts nothing and reports the existing row. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-01: a second claim of the same key inserts no row, and returns the documented columns.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-02** The idempotency claim happens after the size and first-bytes check and before admission and the circuit breaker. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (plan §Target architecture diagram, line 305); slice-plan-recognition.md §3; slice-blueprint.md §3. *Verified by:* API test: a repeated key with an invalid body is refused without a replay; a repeated valid key is answered from the stored result without a new job even when admission would refuse new work. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-request-order.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, the order of checks > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-02: a repeated key with an invalid body is refused, not replayed; a finished key is replayed while admission refuses new work.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-03** The idempotency key is scoped to the install: the same key sent by two different installs produces two independent recognitions. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Data model row recognition_events `UNIQUE(install_id, idempotency_key)`; conventions §Scaling rules). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: two installs send the same key and each gets its own `recognition_id`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > keys belong to their install: another install with the same key gets its own recognition.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-04** The claim is atomic: of two requests with the same key arriving together, exactly one wins. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3 `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; §Phases row 3 Verify). *Verified by:* concurrency test: two simultaneous sends of the same key create exactly one recognition row. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-04: two claims of one key at the same moment leave one recognition, claimed once.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-05** The recognition id is created at claim time as a UUIDv7, is the public `recognition_id`, and stays the same across replays and re-claims. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Data model row recognition_events; G9); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* unit test: the id is version 7; API end-to-end test: the id returned on a replay and after a re-claim equals the first id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-rules.test.ts :: UUID version 7 recognition ids > have the version 7 shape and carry the time they were made ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a finished recognition sent again is answered the same, with the same id, and the worker is not asked twice ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > the same key again: in progress while fresh, finished once done, and claimed again after a timeout, keeping its first receipt time.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-06** When the key already exists with a terminal outcome, the stored result is replayed with HTTP 200 and the same `recognition_id`, status and card. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3, line 487); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: send a clip, get MATCH, send the same key again; the second response is 200 with the same id, status and card. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a finished recognition sent again is answered the same, with the same id, and the worker is not asked twice.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-07** A replay creates no worker job. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3; §Phases row 3 Verify). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the queue's total job count is unchanged by the second send. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a finished recognition sent again is answered the same, with the same id, and the worker is not asked twice.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-08** A replay creates no second recognition row. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Phases row 3 Verify "one row"). *Verified by:* database assertion in the replay test: exactly one row exists for the (install, key) pair. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a finished recognition sent again is answered the same, with the same id, and the worker is not asked twice.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-09** A replay leaves every counter untouched (engagement-limit counters and analytics counts). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3 "counters untouched"; §Phases row 3 Verify). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: counters read before and after the replay are equal. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-replays.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize: replays and retries of one key > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-09: a recognition and its replay leave the campaign’s tap counter as it was.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-10** A replay returns the card stored with the first result (the card snapshot on the row), not a card resolved again, even after the station has edited its card. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Data model row recognition_events "card_id (a snapshot for replay)"); slice-blueprint.md §12; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: edit the card between the first send and the replay; the replay still returns the first card version. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-replays.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-10: a card edited between the send and the replay: the replay shows the version first shown.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-11** Outcomes fall in three groups: `PENDING`; terminal (`MATCH`, `MATCH_DEFAULT`, `CHOOSE_STATION`, `NO_MATCH`, `AMBIGUOUS`, `INVALID_AUDIO`), each of which is replayed; and transient (`TIMEOUT`, `BUSY`, `ERROR`), which may be claimed again. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Data model row recognition_events; §Recognition flow step 3); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* table-driven API test: a repeat after each terminal outcome returns the stored result without a new job; a repeat after each transient outcome runs again (the replay status for `INVALID_AUDIO` is open; see gaps). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-replays.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-06, -07, -08, -11: a finished CHOOSE_STATION is answered again the same way, with no job and no stored change ; apps/api/test/recognize-replays.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-11: a clip the worker could not use is answered 400 INVALID_AUDIO again, with no job ; apps/api/test/recognize-replays.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-11, -13: a key refused as busy is tried again on the same recognition, then answered like any other ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: a repeat after a timeout tries again on the same recognition ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: an attempt that fails after it was claimed is recorded as ERROR, never left PENDING, and the same key can be tried again at once.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-12** When the key exists with outcome `PENDING` and the row is younger than 30 seconds, the request is answered 409 `RECOGNITION_IN_PROGRESS` with a request id, and no second job is created. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4, §10 (plan §Recognition flow steps 3 and 9); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a second send while the first is still being matched returns 409 with that code. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a repeat while the first is still being worked on gets 409 RECOGNITION_IN_PROGRESS.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-13** When the key exists with a transient outcome (`TIMEOUT`, `BUSY` or `ERROR`), the request re-claims the row and is processed again. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3, line 489); slice-blueprint.md §11; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: stop the worker, send (503), start the worker, send the same key again; the second send returns a real result, neither 409 nor a replayed failure. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a repeat after a timeout tries again on the same recognition ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > an attempt that fails after it was claimed is recorded as ERROR, never left PENDING, and the same key can be tried again at once.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-14** When the key exists with outcome `PENDING` and the row is 30 seconds old or older (stale), the request re-claims the row and is processed again. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3, lines 488–489); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API test with a fake clock: a `PENDING` row aged 31 seconds is re-claimed and processed; aged 29 seconds it returns 409. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > a PENDING claim older than 30 seconds is claimed again (its attempt was abandoned) ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > the same key again: in progress while fresh, finished once done, and claimed again after a timeout, keeping its first receipt time ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a repeat while the first is still being worked on gets 409 RECOGNITION_IN_PROGRESS.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-15** A re-claim adds one to `attempt_count`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* database assertion: after one failed attempt and one retry the row's attempt count is 2. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a repeat after a timeout tries again on the same recognition ; apps/api/test/engine-hookup-database.test.ts :: engine hookup: database functions > recognition.claim_recognition and finish_recognition > the same key again: in progress while fresh, finished once done, and claimed again after a timeout, keeping its first receipt time.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-16** A re-claim keeps the first `received_at` and `captured_at`; station and campaign status, the tap limit and the card version are read when the attempt is processed. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3; §Data model "first receipt"); slice-blueprint.md §11; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9; user's answer, 11 October 2026 (P4 design Q9, P4-D5). *Verified by:* database assertion: after a retry sent minutes later, `received_at` and `captured_at` are unchanged and the claim returns both; the receipt-time rules for deferred clips stay P4-TIMERULE-04 to -07. Reworded on 11 October 2026 (user's answer to Q9): it said "so limits, statuses and card version are still judged at the first receipt time", but no history of statuses or card versions is kept, and a retry comes seconds or hours later. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-16, P4-IDEMPOTENCY-22, P4-DEFERRED-01: a retry keeps and returns the first receipt and capture times, and is deferred if either attempt was, never turned back.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-17** A re-claim replaces the row's `request_id` with the latest attempt's request id. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Data model row recognition_events "request_id (latest attempt)"); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* database assertion: after a retry the row holds the second request's id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a repeat after a timeout tries again on the same recognition.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-18** A request that fails with a transient error after the claim (503 busy, 503 timeout, internal error) leaves the row in the matching transient outcome, never in `PENDING`, so an immediate retry is not blocked by the 30-second rule. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4 (plan §Recognition flow step 3; §Data model transient outcomes). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: after a 503 the row's outcome is BUSY or TIMEOUT; a retry one second later is processed, not answered 409. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > with no worker heartbeat: 503 RECOGNITION_BUSY with Retry-After at once, nothing queued ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > when the workers cannot answer > when no worker answers in time: 503 RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT after 2.5 seconds (plus at most 1 second), recorded as TIMEOUT, and the clip is not left in Valkey ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > the idempotency key > a repeat after a timeout tries again on the same recognition ; apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > an attempt that fails after it was claimed is recorded as ERROR, never left PENDING, and the same key can be tried again at once.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-19** Idempotency keys are cleaned up once they can no longer matter (a phone can no longer retry that clip). *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §1 (conventions §Scaling rules); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §20 (conventions; tagged ALL there, see conflict 6). *Verified by:* job test: the clean-up removes keys older than the retry horizon and keeps newer ones (see gaps: the horizon and the scheduler are not stated). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-idempotency-key-clean-up.test.ts :: the recognition idempotency key clean-up > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-19: keys older than 48 hours are deleted and newer ones kept, and the run logs how many went under its own request id ; apps/api/test/recognition-idempotency-key-clean-up.test.ts :: the recognition idempotency key clean-up > deletes in batches of 1,000 until a batch comes back short: 2,500 old keys go in one run ; apps/api/test/recognition-idempotency-key-clean-up.test.ts :: the recognition idempotency key clean-up > the database refuses to delete keys younger than 25 hours, whoever asks, since a phone may still retry them.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-20** Idempotency test: the same key sent twice gives the same `recognition_id` and one row, and the campaign's counter moves once, at the tap, never at a recognition or its replay. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4, §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Verify); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* one API end-to-end scenario: send a key, tap Call once, send the key again; the replay has the same `recognition_id`, there is one recognition row, and the campaign's counter is 1. Reworded on 11 October 2026 (user's answer to Q2 of the P4 design, P4-D10): it said "counters incremented once", but counters move only at a tap; moved to gate part 2 (P4-RESOLUTION-17) by the user's answer to Q11. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-20: a key, a tap and the key again: the same recognition id, one recognition row, and the counter at 1, moved by the tap alone
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-21** Concurrency test: two concurrent sends of the same key give exactly one worker job and exactly one 409 (the other request gets 200). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §4, §18 (plan §Phases row 3, Verify); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test firing both requests together and counting jobs and status codes. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-request-order.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, the order of checks > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-21: two sends of one key at the same moment give one worker job, one 200 and one 409 with Retry-After.
- [x] **P4-IDEMPOTENCY-22** A re-claim stores `is_deferred` as the stored value or the new one (true is never turned back to false), keeps the first `received_at` and `captured_at`, and returns the stored capture time, so a retry is resolved at the first capture moment; the claim stores the clock skew exactly (`bigint`). *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §4.4, P4-D2, P4-D18; contract §1.2 ("the `is_deferred` that is stored and answered is the computed one"). *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts`: a re-claim with `is_deferred` true on a row stored false stores and returns true, never back to false, and returns the first capture time; `apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts`: a clip that went Busy as a live send and was then sent from the outbox is stored and answered as deferred. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36); amended after review: `claim_recognition` is dropped and created again in the P4 migration (its `integer` skew parameter was the column's only writer), and its tests keep their names, so the ticked evidence of P4-IDEMPOTENCY-03, -05, -14 and -15 still names tests that exist. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-22: a clip that went Busy as a live send and is then sent from the outbox is stored and answered as deferred.

### Admission

- [x] **P4-ADMISSION-01** When too many recognition requests are already in flight in one API process, a new one is answered 503 `RECOGNITION_BUSY` with `Retry-After`; the in-flight limit is a named configuration value. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §5 (plan §Recognition flow step 4, line 492); slice-blueprint.md §7. *Verified by:* API test with the limit set to 1: while one request waits on the worker, a second returns 503 with the header. *Built as:* S3-D17: RECOGNITIONS_IN_FLIGHT_LIMIT, set to 4 in this test file. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize > checks before any work is done > past the limit of recognitions at once on this instance, reading clips included, refuses the next at once as busy and takes clips again once places free up.
- [x] **P4-ADMISSION-02** The places in flight are checked when the text fields arrive, before any audio is read; a deferred send (`is_deferred` "true") must also find one of the `RECOGNITIONS_DEFERRED_IN_FLIGHT_LIMIT` places (16 of the 32), so live clips always find the rest; a refusal there carries the deferred `Retry-After` (60–300) and is never counted against the listener's allowance. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.2, §5.3, P4-D15. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts`: with the deferred limit at 2 and a worker that holds jobs, a third deferred send gets 503 with a `Retry-After` in [60, 300] while a live send is still admitted; a send refused for lack of a place is not counted against the listener's allowance. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36); amended after review, because a deferred burst could take every place before its fields were read. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-ADMISSION-02, P4-DEFERRED-06: fifty deferred sends refused for lack of a deferred place each get a Retry-After within 60–300, not all equal, and none is counted against the listener ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-ADMISSION-02, P4-DEFERRED-05: with the deferred places held by jobs a worker keeps, a third deferred send gets 503 while a live send is still admitted ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, clips sent later > P4-ADMISSION-02: with every place taken, a deferred send refused for lack of a place gets the deferred Retry-After, never the live 10 ; apps/api/test/recognize-request-order.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognize, the order of checks > the place in flight before the per-install allowance: with every place taken, an install whose minute is used up gets 503, not 429.
- [x] **P4-ADMISSION-03** Deferred sends have their own circuit breaker: their timeouts never open the live breaker, live admission looks only at the live breaker, deferred admission refuses while either breaker is open, and neither breaker counts `INVALID_AUDIO`. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.3, P4-D15 (fact 22: `opossum` 10.0.0 counts a filtered error as a success). *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/recognition-breakers.test.ts`: ten deferred sends that time out open the deferred breaker and leave the live one closed; a live breaker that opens refuses deferred sends too; `apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts`. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognition-breakers.test.ts :: the live and deferred recognition breakers > P4-ADMISSION-03: ten deferred sends that time out open the deferred breaker and leave the live one closed with no failure; live clips go on, deferred ones are refused ; apps/api/test/recognition-breakers.test.ts :: the live and deferred recognition breakers > P4-ADMISSION-03: a live breaker that opens refuses deferred sends too, since the workers are in trouble for everyone ; apps/api/test/recognition-breakers.test.ts :: the live and deferred recognition breakers > a clip the worker could not read opens neither breaker, live or deferred.

### Multi-station resolution

- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-01** `recognition.resolve` decides in this order: candidate stations, then schedule, then the listener's station hint, then engagement limits. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (plan §Target architecture diagram, line 312). *Verified by:* table-driven resolution tests in which each later rule applies only after the earlier ones leave more than one candidate or a decision to make. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: resolveStation: candidates, then the schedule, then the hint, then limits ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-05: one on air outranks a hint for an owner off air: the listener most likely heard the one on air ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-LIMITS-05: a campaign at its tap limit shows the usual card, and stays on air for the chooser.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-02** Each time window is widened by the campaign's grace period on both sides when deciding whether a candidate is in window ("± grace"; the plan wins over the wireframes' "after each slot", conflict 8). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 509); slice-blueprint.md §8 (G6, O3); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G6). *Verified by:* table-driven database test: inside the grace before the start and after the end gives in window; just outside the grace gives out of window. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/time-windows-with-grace-database.test.ts :: P4-TESTS-04: 9 minutes before the start, inside the grace ; apps/api/test/time-windows-with-grace-database.test.ts :: P4-TESTS-04: 11 minutes before the start, outside the grace ; apps/api/test/time-windows-with-grace-database.test.ts :: P4-TESTS-04: 9 minutes after the end, inside the grace ; apps/api/test/time-windows-with-grace-database.test.ts :: P4-TESTS-04: 11 minutes after the end, outside the grace.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-03** The grace period is 10 minutes by default (O3). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §20 (plan O3; §Data model row campaigns; Foundation migration); slice-blueprint.md §8 (O3); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O3). *Verified by:* database test: a new campaign's grace period is 10 minutes; a clip captured 9 minutes after a window's end is in window and 11 minutes after is not, and the same before the start. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/time-windows-with-grace-database.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-03: a new campaign has a grace of 10 minutes, applied on both sides of a slot.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-04** When exactly one station owns the matched ad, that station is used with resolution `SINGLE_OWNER` and no chooser is shown. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §19 (plan §Recognition flow step 8; §Phases row 4 Verify "one owner"); slice-blueprint.md §13. *Verified by:* resolution test case "one owner": that station, its card and resolution `SINGLE_OWNER`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-04: one owner on air: its campaign card, SINGLE_OWNER, one candidate row ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-D8: a single-owner match writes one row, and a schedule-unique match writes a row for each owner with a card, only the winner in window.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-05** When several stations own the ad and exactly one is in window, the station whose schedule is live wins, with resolution `SCHEDULE_UNIQUE` (D7; MVP step 5: with only Station A in window, the app shows A's card). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §19 (plan §Recognition flow step 8; D7); slice-blueprint.md §13 (D7); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D7); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 5). *Verified by:* resolution test case "two owners with one in window": the in-window station, its card and `SCHEDULE_UNIQUE`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-05: two stations own the recording and one has it on air: that one’s buttons, stored as SCHEDULE_UNIQUE with both stations weighed ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-05: two owners, one on air: the one on air, SCHEDULE_UNIQUE, both weighed and only it on air.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-06** When several stations are in window and the station hint is one of them, the hinted station is used with resolution `HINT` and status MATCH. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §19 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 511); slice-blueprint.md §13. *Verified by:* resolution test case "two in window with a matching hint". *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-06: with the hint naming one of the stations on air: that station’s buttons, stored as HINT ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-06: two on air and the hint names one: that one, HINT.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-07** When several stations are in window and there is no hint, the response status is `CHOOSE_STATION` and the listener is asked "Which station are you listening to?" (D7). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §19 (plan §Recognition flow step 8; D7); slice-blueprint.md §13 (D7); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §4 (D7). *Verified by:* resolution test case "two in window with no hint": `CHOOSE_STATION` with exactly two candidates, each with a full card. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-07, -09, P4-CHOOSER-04: two stations with it on air and no hint: always asked, both stations’ full cards inline, never a match ; apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-07: on air, the station whose slot started last is listed first.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-08** When several stations are in window and the hint names a station that is not among them, the hint is ignored and the response status is `CHOOSE_STATION`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §19 (plan §Recognition flow step 8 "Otherwise"; §Phases row 4 Verify); slice-blueprint.md §13. *Verified by:* resolution test case "hint for a station outside the set": `CHOOSE_STATION` with the two in-window stations and without the hinted one. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-08: a hint for a station that does not have it on air, or that is unknown, is ignored: still asked, without that station ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-08: a hint for an owner off air while two are on air is ignored: the chooser of the two, the third weighed but not shown.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-09** Until air-play monitoring exists (Phase 9), a tie between several in-window stations is settled only by the hint or by asking the listener; the server never guesses a station. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §13 (plan §Recognition flow step 8 and Phase 9 upgrade; D7). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: two in window with no hint always gives `CHOOSE_STATION`, never a MATCH for one of them. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-07, -09, P4-CHOOSER-04: two stations with it on air and no hint: always asked, both stations’ full cards inline, never a match ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-07, -09: two on air and no hint: the server never guesses, it asks, with both cards, by name when the slots started together.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-10** The station hint sent with a recognize request is stored on the recognition event; a request without one stores nothing there. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9 (plan §Data model row recognition_events); slice-blueprint.md §11 ("the hint is stored on the event"). *Verified by:* API test reads `hinted_station_id` after requests with and without a hint. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-10: the hint is stored on the recognition when its station exists, and nothing without one ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-10: the hint is stored only for a station that exists, and nothing without one.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-11** Finalising sets the recognition's resolution to one of `SINGLE_OWNER`, `SCHEDULE_UNIQUE`, `HINT`, `LISTENER_CHOICE` or `UNRESOLVED`; the database refuses other values, and a `CHOOSE_STATION` outcome stores `UNRESOLVED` until a choice is made (G12: each event records how the station was resolved). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 514; §Data model row recognition_events); slice-blueprint.md §10 (G12); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G12), §9. *Verified by:* schema test of the check; table-driven test: each resolution case stores its value. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-11: a CHOOSE_STATION finished with the resolution UNRESOLVED is stored: true ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-11: the table itself refuses a resolution that does not fit the outcome, and an unknown one.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-12** Finalising writes one `recognition_candidates` row per candidate station, keyed by (recognition, station), holding that station's campaign, the card it would show (the same card sent inline for it), whether it was in window (`is_in_window`) and whether it was chosen (`is_chosen`) (G12). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8; §Data model row recognition_candidates); slice-blueprint.md §10 (G12); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G12), §9. *Verified by:* database assertion: a two-station recognition has two candidate rows with the right campaign, card and in-window values, and neither chosen. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-12: a two-station chooser writes two candidate rows with the right campaign, card and in-window values, neither chosen.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-13** A station sees only its own candidate rows and cannot tell which other stations were candidates for the same recognition. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Data model row recognition_candidates; D7); slice-blueprint.md §10; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* row-level-security test: with station A's context only A's candidate row is visible for a two-station recognition. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-13: each station sees only its own candidate row of a recognition both were weighed for, and not the recognition while nobody chose.
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-14** A suspended station's card, campaign card or default card, is never returned in any branch of resolution: live and deferred clips, single owner, schedule unique, hint, chooser and default card (MVP step 6). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §19 (plan §Phases row 4 Verify; baseline §5; §End-to-end verification step 6); slice-blueprint.md §8 (G15); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 6). *Verified by:* API end-to-end tests for each branch with the station suspended. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-14: a suspended station’s buttons are never given: one owner, the schedule, the hint, a chooser, a usual card, a clip sent later ; apps/api/test/recognize-replays.test.ts :: P4-D13, P4-RESOLUTION-14: a station suspended since is left out of a replay: a match becomes NO_MATCH with the same id.
- ~~P4-RESOLUTION-15~~ — *Retired on 11 October 2026 (user's answer to Q5 of the P4 design, P4-D21): O3 fixes one out-of-window behaviour, the client's default card, and no other value exists, so campaigns get no out-of-window policy column. The reason is recorded in ADR-003. The id is not reused.*
- [x] **P4-RESOLUTION-16** Gate, first part: Phase 4 builds `recognition.resolve` in full — candidates, time windows, grace, hint, `CHOOSE_STATION` with inline cards, the choice endpoint, idempotency, the deferred rules and the default-card fallback — and every P4 item outside engagement limits, the engagements endpoint, P4-TIMERULE-04 and the three items moved to gate part 2 (P4-IDEMPOTENCY-20, P4-DELIVERY-02, -04) is ticked with its named test, or set aside with a reason the user has seen (P4-DEFERRED-11, P4-TESTS-06 and -07 deferred to P8, Q10; P4-RESOLUTION-15 retired, Q5; P4-DELIVERY-03 superseded by the user's reorder of 10 October 2026). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §19 (plan §Phases row 4, Build); plan §Execution order (lines 35–40). *Verified by:* gate check against this checklist, at the end of P4 Piece 4. Split in review round 1 (conflict 16); narrowed on 11 October 2026 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q10, Q11 and Q13). *Gate record:* `docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md`, "Gate record (15 October 2026)". Set aside with reasons the user has seen: P4-DEFERRED-11, P4-TESTS-06 and -07 (P8, Q10), P4-RESOLUTION-15 (retired, Q5), P4-DELIVERY-03 (superseded by the reorder of 10 October 2026). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-07, -09, P4-CHOOSER-04: two stations with it on air and no hint: always asked, both stations’ full cards inline, never a match ; apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-02, -03: none has it on air and no hint: asked, each station with its usual buttons, the one on air most recently first ; apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: P4-CHOICE-01, -04 to -06, P6-TESTS-07 (part 2): a shown station’s id is accepted with 204 and no body, and the recognition takes the listener’s choice with that station’s campaign and card ; apps/api/test/recognize-replays.test.ts :: P4-IDEMPOTENCY-10: a card edited between the send and the replay: the replay shows the version first shown ; apps/api/test/recognize-deferred.test.ts :: P4-CLOCK-01, -02: a phone 3 hours slow is judged against the window it was really heard in, and stored within seconds of the true time ; apps/api/test/time-rule-split.test.ts :: P4-TIMERULE-08 (resolveStation): the time window follows the capture time: heard in its slot and processed after it ended, the campaign’s own buttons.
