### Default-card fallback

- [x] **P4-FALLBACK-01** When no station is in window and the hinted station owns the ad, the response uses the hinted station's client default card. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §19 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 512); slice-blueprint.md §13. *Verified by:* resolution test case "none in window" with a hint: two owners, none in window, hint for one of them gives that station's client default card. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-01: none has it on air and the hint names an owner: that owner’s usual buttons, stored as MATCH_DEFAULT by HINT ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-01: none on air and the hint owns it: its usual card, MATCH_DEFAULT, HINT.
- [x] **P4-FALLBACK-02** When no station is in window, there is no usable hint and several stations own the ad, the response is `CHOOSE_STATION` among the owners, each shown with its client default card, not its campaign card. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §19 (plan §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §13. *Verified by:* resolution test case "none in window" with several owners and no hint. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-02, -03: none has it on air and no hint: asked, each station with its usual buttons, the one on air most recently first ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-02, -03: none on air, several owners, no hint: a chooser of usual cards, the slot that ended last first, none in 7 days last.
- [x] **P4-FALLBACK-03** In that out-of-window chooser the owners are ordered most recently aired first: the owner whose last slot ended most recently, looking back 7 days (none in those 7 days: last), ties by name. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §13. *Verified by:* database test: two owners whose last slots ended at different times give the later `last_slot_ended_at` to the one that aired last, and the resolution rules put it first. "Most recently aired" was defined on 11 October 2026 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q4, P4-D7). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-03: gives the start of the latest slot the clip falls in, and the end of the slot that ended last within 7 days, started in the period ; apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-02, -03: none has it on air and no hint: asked, each station with its usual buttons, the one on air most recently first ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-02, -03: none on air, several owners, no hint: a chooser of usual cards, the slot that ended last first, none in 7 days last.
- [x] **P4-FALLBACK-04** Each client can have a default action card (`default_action_card_id`; plan name `default_card_id`), which is the blueprint's "brand default landing card" (G5): setting it stores the reference, and an out-of-window recognition returns that card. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G5; blueprint §4 Fallback Logic). *Verified by:* API test: setting a client's default card stores the reference; an out-of-window recognition returns it. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/client-default-card-route.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-04: setting it stores the reference, and a clip heard out of the ad’s slots gets that card.
- [x] **P4-FALLBACK-05** Station OWNERs and MANAGERs set or clear a client's default card through a portal route (`PUT stations/:stationId/clients/:clientId/default-action-card`), only to a version of that client's cards, with the audit event `client_default_card_changed`; an ANALYST gets 403 and another station's client 404. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.10, P4-D20. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/client-default-card-route.test.ts`. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/client-default-card-route.test.ts :: P4-FALLBACK-05: every change is audited with the previous and the new card ids, never a card’s content; clearing takes the card away ; apps/api/test/client-default-card-route.test.ts :: refuses a card that is not one of this client’s versions: another client’s, another station’s, or none at all, and changes nothing ; apps/api/test/client-default-card-route.test.ts :: only owners and managers may set it: an analyst gets 403 and nothing changes ; apps/api/test/client-default-card-route.test.ts :: another station’s client is not found: through its own station, through this one, or by an id that is not one.

### Station chooser response

- [x] **P4-CHOOSER-01** A `CHOOSE_STATION` response carries `candidates[]` and no top-level `result`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §10 (plan §Recognition flow step 9, line 515). *Verified by:* contract test against the response schema with a chooser fixture. *Evidence:* packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: P4-CHOOSER-01: a chooser answer carries its candidates and no top-level result, for zod and for the JSON Schema ; apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-07, -09, P4-CHOOSER-04: two stations with it on air and no hint: always asked, both stations’ full cards inline, never a match.
- [x] **P4-CHOOSER-02** A `CHOOSE_STATION` response lists at most 4 candidates: with several stations on air, the 4 whose current slots started last; with none on air, the 4 whose last slots ended last (P4-FALLBACK-03); ties by name. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8; §Target architecture diagram); slice-blueprint.md §11 (D7); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: with five stations in window the response has exactly 4 candidates, the ones whose slots started last. The order was decided on 11 October 2026 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q4, P4-D7). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-CHOOSER-02: five stations with it on air give the four whose slots started last; the fifth is weighed and not shown ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: P4-CHOOSER-02: five on air give the four whose slots started last; the fifth is weighed, not shown ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: offers from 2 to 4 stations.
- [x] **P4-CHOOSER-03** Each candidate carries the station's name, frequency and logo, and its public station reference (S3-RESPONSE-17). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 511); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* contract test: every candidate validates with those three station fields. *Logo:* every station carries its `logo` field, null until stations' pictures reach listeners, which Phase 5b builds with the picture screens (S3-D21, P4-D37). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-CHOOSER-03, -07: every station in an answer carries its name, frequency, city (or null) and no logo, and nothing else ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: a result and a candidate carry the same public station: id, name, frequency, city (null when unknown) and logo.
- [x] **P4-CHOOSER-04** Each candidate carries that station's full card inline (layout and actions), so all candidate cards arrive in the same response. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9 (plan §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §11; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §7 (Target architecture diagram). *Verified by:* contract test: every candidate's card validates against the action-card schema. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-07, -09, P4-CHOOSER-04: two stations with it on air and no hint: always asked, both stations’ full cards inline, never a match ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-rules.test.ts :: a chooser carries every shown station’s full card inline, with the station’s public facts and city (P4-CHOOSER-03, -04) ; packages/contracts/test/recognition-schemas.test.ts :: the choosers offer 2, 3 and 4 stations with a card each, every one a default card only out of window.
- ~~P4-CHOOSER-05~~ — *Retired in review round 1 (5 October 2026): app behaviour, kept as P6-CHOOSER-09 (part 3); the API side is P4-CHOOSER-01 to -04. The id is not reused.*
- ~~P4-CHOOSER-06~~ — *Retired in review round 1 (5 October 2026): the app flow is P6-TESTS-07 (part 3) and needs the P6 outbox; the API side is the P4-CHOICE tests. The id is not reused.*
- [x] **P4-CHOOSER-07** Every station in an answer (a MATCH's station and every chooser candidate) carries its city, or null when the station has none; the database keeps a station's city NULL or 1–80 characters and never blank, so no answer can fail its own contract check. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §4.1, contract §2.1 (`ListenerStation`), P4-D27. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts`: `stations_city_check` refuses an 81-character and a blank city, and `find_station_candidates` returns the city; `apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts`: every station in an answer carries its city. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-CHOOSER-07: the city of a station is none, or 1 to 80 characters and not blank ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-CHOOSER-07: carries the city of the station, or none ; apps/api/test/recognize-station-resolution.test.ts :: P4-CHOOSER-03, -07: every station in an answer carries its name, frequency, city (or null) and no logo, and nothing else.

### Engagement limits

- [x] **P4-LIMITS-01** A campaign may carry an optional engagement (tap) limit (`engagement_limit`; plan name `engagement_cap`); with none set there is no limit, and a set limit must be above zero (supersedes blueprint §4 "budget cap / click quotas", which had no columns). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 lines 101, 119; G4); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G4). *Verified by:* API test: a schedule saved with and without a limit; database check that a limit must be above zero. *(partly built)* *Built so far:* the column and its above-zero check (Foundation migration, no test). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: station resolution: database functions > the rules the tables keep > P4-LIMITS-01: a tap limit must be above zero, and none means no limit ; apps/api/test/cards-schedules-publishing.test.ts :: buttons, schedules and publishing > treats a missing engagement limit as no limit
- [x] **P4-LIMITS-02** An engagement limit counts deduplicated engagements (button taps): a tap repeated with the same recognition and action counts once (O4). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §20 (plan O4; §Engagements); slice-blueprint.md §8 (G4, O4); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O4). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the same tap posted three times moves the campaign's counter by one. *Built as:* also the per-install cap (Q12, P4-D10): one install counts at most `ENGAGEMENTS_COUNTED_PER_INSTALL_PER_CAMPAIGN_PER_DAY` (5) taps on one campaign in 24 hours. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-02, P4-LIMITS-02: the same tap posted three times answers 204 each time, stores one row and moves the counter once ; apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-LIMITS-02, Q12: one install counts at most its daily cap of taps on one campaign; more are stored, not counted
- [x] **P4-LIMITS-03** Limit usage is counted in a `campaign_counters` table, one counter per campaign, with row-level security. *Source:* slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G4), §9 (row campaign_counters). *Verified by:* schema test: the table exists with one counter per campaign and row-level security on. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > indexes and partitions > P4-LIMITS-03: tap limits are counted in campaign_counters, one row per campaign, with row-level security on
- [x] **P4-LIMITS-04** The counter is changed in one atomic statement (`UPDATE … RETURNING`), never by reading and then writing, so concurrent requests cannot both pass a full limit. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §19 (plan §Data model row campaign_counters; §Recognition flow step 8); slice-blueprint.md §8 (G4); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* the limit-race test (P4-LIMITS-06) plus a code review of the counter statement. *Built as:* `record_engagement` counts with one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE counted_engagement_count < limit RETURNING` (StationResolution migration), reviewed 14 October 2026; no read then write. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-LIMITS-04, -06: 100 taps at once against a limit of 10 count exactly 10, and every one is stored
- [x] **P4-LIMITS-05** The engagement limit is counted at the tap, each distinct tap once and only on the campaign's own buttons; when a clip is processed and the campaign is already at its limit, listeners get the client's default card instead of the campaign card (O4; MVP step 6). A recognition moves no counter. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §9, §20 (plan §Recognition flow step 8, line 513; O4); slice-blueprint.md §8 (blueprint §4 line 119; O4); slice-plan-cross-cutting.md §28 (O4), §29 (plan §End-to-end verification step 6). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: fill the limit with taps, identify again; the response carries the default card; concurrency test: parallel taps around the limit never push the counter past it (P4-LIMITS-06). Reworded on 11 October 2026 (user's answer to Q2 of the P4 design, P4-D10): it said "at the receipt time the engagement limit is reserved atomically", but reserving at every recognition would use the limit up on listeners who never tap. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-LIMITS-05: taps count only on the campaign’s own card; once the limit is reached, a clip is answered with the client’s usual card, and a tap on it is stored and never counted ; apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-IDEMPOTENCY-20: a key, a tap and the key again: the same recognition id, one recognition row, and the counter at 1, moved by the tap alone
- [x] **P4-LIMITS-06** Limit race: 100 concurrent taps against a limit of 10 count exactly 10: the campaign's counter ends at exactly 10 and never exceeds the limit. (What the 90 uncounted taps receive is gap 31.) *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §19 (plan §Phases row 4 Verify "Cap race: 100 concurrent taps against a cap of 10 count exactly 10"; §Data model row campaign_counters); slice-blueprint.md §8; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* concurrency test firing 100 parallel engagement requests, with the per-install cap of P4-ENGAGEMENTS-14 switched off: the campaign's counter ends at exactly 10 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q12). Reworded in review round 1 ("exactly 10 succeed" had no source). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-LIMITS-04, -06: 100 taps at once against a limit of 10 count exactly 10, and every one is stored

### Station choice endpoint

- [x] **P4-CHOICE-01** The API exposes `POST /api/v1/recognitions/{id}/choice` with a body holding the chosen `station_id`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §11 (plan §The listener's station choice, line 520; §Phases row 4 Build); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: after a `CHOOSE_STATION` recognition, posting one candidate's station id succeeds. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-01, -04 to -06, P6-TESTS-07 (part 2): a shown station’s id is accepted with 204 and no body, and the recognition takes the listener’s choice with that station’s campaign and card.
- [x] **P4-CHOICE-02** The choice endpoint is idempotent: sending the same choice again succeeds and changes nothing more. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §11 (line 521); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: the same choice posted twice succeeds both times; one candidate row is chosen and no counter moves on the second call. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-02, P6-OUTBOX-05 (a choice): the same choice sent three times answers 204 each time, keeps one chosen row and moves no counter.
- [x] **P4-CHOICE-03** A choice is valid only for a station in that recognition's candidate set; any other station id is refused in the error envelope with a catalogue code and changes nothing. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §11, §19 (plan §The listener's station choice; §Phases row 4 Verify); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: posting a non-candidate station is refused, and the recognition and its candidate rows are unchanged. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-03: a station that was not shown is refused with 422 STATION_NOT_A_CANDIDATE, and nothing changes.
- [x] **P4-CHOICE-04** An accepted choice sets the recognition's resolution to `LISTENER_CHOICE`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §11 (line 522); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* database assertion after the call. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-01, -04 to -06, P6-TESTS-07 (part 2): a shown station’s id is accepted with 204 and no body, and the recognition takes the listener’s choice with that station’s campaign and card.
- [x] **P4-CHOICE-05** An accepted choice marks that station's candidate row as chosen, and exactly one candidate row of the recognition is chosen. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §11 (plan §Data model row recognition_candidates); slice-blueprint.md §11; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* database assertion after the call. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-01, -04 to -06, P6-TESTS-07 (part 2): a shown station’s id is accepted with 204 and no body, and the recognition takes the listener’s choice with that station’s campaign and card ; apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-02, P6-OUTBOX-05 (a choice): the same choice sent three times answers 204 each time, keeps one chosen row and moves no counter.
- [x] **P4-CHOICE-06** After an accepted choice the recognition's resolved station is the chosen station, so the recognition counts in that station's data and in no other candidate's resolved data. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §11 (plan §Data model rows recognition_events and recognition_candidates; §End-to-end verification step 5). *Verified by:* row-level-security test: the chosen station sees the recognition as resolved; the other candidate sees only its own unchosen candidate row. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations: candidates, choices, taps and counters > P4-CHOICE-06: after the listener chose station A, A sees the recognition as its own, and B sees only its own candidate row, unchosen ; apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-01, -04 to -06, P6-TESTS-07 (part 2): a shown station’s id is accepted with 204 and no body, and the recognition takes the listener’s choice with that station’s campaign and card.
- [x] **P4-CHOICE-07** The choice endpoint requires a valid install token, like the recognize route; without one it answers 401 `INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Engagements; G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > P4-CHOICE-07: without a valid install token the answer is 401 INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID, and nothing changes.
- [x] **P4-CHOICE-08** Choices, taps and reports share one per-install allowance (`RECOGNITION_FOLLOW_UPS_PER_INSTALL_PER_MINUTE`, 60 a minute); past it they get 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After`. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.11, P4-D29. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts` (the allowance gives 429), `apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts`. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: P4-CHOICE-08 (choice half): past the follow-up allowance an install gets 429 RATE_LIMITED with Retry-After, counted per install and before the path and the body ; apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: P4-CHOICE-08 (tap half): taps share one allowance with choices and reports: once choices and a report have used the minute, a tap is refused 429 and not stored ; apps/api/test/recognition-reports-route.test.ts :: checks run in the follow-ups’ order: the token (401), then the allowance shared with choices (429), then the path (400), then the body (400).

### Engagements endpoint

- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-01** The API exposes `POST /api/v1/engagements` with a body holding `recognition_id`, `station_id`, `action_id`, `occurred_at` and `sent_at` (when the phone sent it, so the server can correct the phone's clock), and records each button tap as a row in `engagement_events` (G4: the click tracking the blueprint lacked). *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Engagements, line 524; §Phases row 4 Build); slice-blueprint.md §11; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §6 (G4). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a tap on a matched card's button is accepted and stored once; a body missing any of the five fields is refused with a validation error. `sent_at` was added on 11 October 2026 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q14). *Built as:* `features/engagements` (P4 design §5.8): the follow-ups' guards, the contract's `RecordEngagementInput`, then `recognition.record_engagement`. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-01, -09, -10: a tap on a button of the card shown answers 204 and is stored once with the station, the recognition, the button’s id, its type from the card and its time
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-02** Engagements are deduplicated per (`recognition_id`, `action_id`) by a unique constraint: a repeated send stores no second row and still returns success, so offline retries are idempotent. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Engagements, line 525; §Data model row engagement_events); slice-blueprint.md §10; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* database test of the unique constraint; API end-to-end test: the same tap posted three times yields one row and three success responses. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-02, P4-LIMITS-02: the same tap posted three times answers 204 each time, stores one row and moves the counter once
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-03** A tap's time is first corrected by the phone's clock (`occurred_at` + (receipt − `sent_at`)); a corrected time earlier than the recognition's `received_at` is stored as `received_at`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Engagements, line 525); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API test: a tap dated before the recognition is stored with the recognition's receipt time. The correction was added on 11 October 2026 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q14). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-03: the phone’s clock is corrected, and a tap dated before the recognition is stored at the recognition’s receipt ; apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: correctTapTime (P4-ENGAGEMENTS-03) > moves the tap by how far the phone’s "sent at" is from the receipt: a phone 3 hours slow
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-04** A corrected tap time (P4-ENGAGEMENTS-03) later than the server's current time is stored as the current time. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (line 525); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API test: a tap dated a day in the future is stored with the server's time at receipt. The correction was added on 11 October 2026 (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q14). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-04: a tap dated a day in the future is stored at the server’s time of receipt
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-05** A tap counts only when the named station is in the recognition's candidate set. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Engagements, line 526); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a tap naming a non-candidate station stores no row and moves no counter. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-05: a tap naming a station not shown, or on a "No match", is refused 422 and stores nothing; P4-ENGAGEMENTS-10: a button not on the card is 422 ACTION_NOT_ON_CARD ; apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > A4: a tap on an unanswered chooser records that station as the listener’s choice and counts; a tap naming another station afterwards is 422 STATION_ALREADY_CHOSEN
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-06** A tap counts only when the recognition is completed. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (line 526); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API test: a tap for a recognition still `PENDING`, or for an id that does not exist, stores nothing. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-06, -07: a tap for a recognition still pending, for an unknown id or for another install’s recognition answers 404 and stores nothing
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-07** A tap counts only when it comes from the same install that made the recognition. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (line 526); slice-blueprint.md §11. *Verified by:* API end-to-end test: a second install posting a tap for the first install's recognition stores nothing and moves no counter. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-06, -07: a tap for a recognition still pending, for an unknown id or for another install’s recognition answers 404 and stores nothing
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-08** The engagements endpoint requires a valid install token; without one it answers 401 `INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID`. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Engagements "from the same install"; G11). *Verified by:* API end-to-end test. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-08 and the follow-ups’ order: the token (401), then the allowance shared with choices and reports (429), then the body (400)
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-09** Each stored engagement holds the station and the recognition (both required), the action id, the action type and the time it occurred; the action type is taken from the card on the server, since the request does not carry it. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Data model row engagement_events); slice-blueprint.md §10; slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* schema test: `station_id` and `recognition_id` are NOT NULL; database assertion after a tap: all five columns are filled and the action type matches the card's action. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-01, -09, -10: a tap on a button of the card shown answers 204 and is stored once with the station, the recognition, the button’s id, its type from the card and its time ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: database catalogue > indexes and partitions > P4-LIMITS-03: tap limits are counted in campaign_counters, one row per campaign, with row-level security on
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-10** The `action_id` the app reports for a tap is the `id` of that action on the card shown (kept from blueprint §7). *Source:* slice-blueprint.md §11 (blueprint §7 line 246). *Verified by:* API test: an engagement for an action id that is on the shown card is accepted and stored with that id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-01, -09, -10: a tap on a button of the card shown answers 204 and is stored once with the station, the recognition, the button’s id, its type from the card and its time ; apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-05: a tap naming a station not shown, or on a "No match", is refused 422 and stores nothing; P4-ENGAGEMENTS-10: a button not on the card is 422 ACTION_NOT_ON_CARD
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-11** A station sees only its own engagement rows. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §12 (plan §Data model row engagement_events; §End-to-end verification step 5); slice-plan-decisions-data-model.md §9. *Verified by:* row-level-security test: with station A's context, station B's engagement rows are invisible. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: row-level security between stations: candidates, choices, taps and counters > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-11: a station sees only its own taps and tap counters
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-12** The one-engagement-per-(`recognition_id`, `action_id`) rule still holds when `engagement_events` is partitioned by month: the uniqueness lives in a small unpartitioned table, so the same tap retried after a month boundary is still stored once and the retry still gets success. *Source:* conventions §Scaling rules "Uniqueness that must hold across months lives in a small unpartitioned table"; plan §Data model row engagement_events "`UNIQUE(recognition_id, action_id)` makes offline retries idempotent" (line 372). *Verified by:* database test: insert an engagement whose time falls in month N, retry the same pair with a time in month N+1; exactly one row exists and the second call returns success. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/listener-follow-ups-database.test.ts :: listener follow-ups: database functions > recognition.record_engagement > P4-ENGAGEMENTS-12: a tap retried in the next month is still stored once, and both sends succeed
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-13** A tap on a chooser recognition with no choice yet records that station as the listener's choice, in the same transaction and under the same row lock as the choice route (`resolution = LISTENER_CHOICE`); a later tap or choice for another station of that recognition gets 422 `STATION_ALREADY_CHOSEN` and stores nothing. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.8, contract amendment A4, P4-D12. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts`: taps on two candidate stations of one chooser recognition: the first gets 204, the second 422, the recognition's chosen station is the first and only its campaign's counter moves; `apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts`: after a tap on Station A of an unanswered chooser, a choice of Station B gets 422 `STATION_ALREADY_CHOSEN`. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Built as:* recognition.record_engagement records the tapped station as the choice under the recognition’s row lock, as the choice route does (P4 design §8.4, A4). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/engagements > A4: a tap on an unanswered chooser records that station as the listener’s choice and counts; a tap naming another station afterwards is 422 STATION_ALREADY_CHOSEN ; apps/api/test/station-choice-route.test.ts :: POST /api/v1/recognitions/{recognition_id}/choice > A4, P4-ENGAGEMENTS-13 (choice half): after a tap on Station A of a chooser nobody answered, a choice of Station B gets 422 STATION_ALREADY_CHOSEN
- [x] **P4-ENGAGEMENTS-14** One install counts at most `ENGAGEMENTS_COUNTED_PER_INSTALL_PER_CAMPAIGN_PER_DAY` taps per campaign in 24 hours (5; 0 switches the cap off; a setting, so changing it needs no migration); its further taps are stored with `is_counted` false and answered 204. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §5.8, §5.11, P4-D10; user's answer, 11 October 2026 (P4 design Q12, 5 counted taps per phone per campaign per day). *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts`: with the cap at 2, one install's third tap that would count on one campaign within 24 hours is stored, not counted, and answered 204, while another install's tap still counts. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). The remaining risk, many installs from many addresses, is an open security-baseline row until device integrity (Play Integrity) is checked in P8. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/engagements-route.test.ts :: P4-LIMITS-02, Q12: one install counts at most its daily cap of taps on one campaign; more are stored, not counted ; apps/api/test/listener-follow-ups-database.test.ts :: P4-ENGAGEMENTS-14: one install counts at most the cap on one campaign in 24 hours; another install still counts, and so does the first a day later.

### Database

- [x] **P4-DATABASE-01** The new station-owned tables for resolution and engagements (candidates, engagement events, limit counters) have row-level security, a `station_id` and composite foreign keys, like the foundation tables. *Source:* slice-plan-recognition.md §21 (baseline §1; plan G2). *Verified by:* `station-isolation.test.ts` extended: station A's context sees none of station B's rows in each new table and cannot insert rows carrying B's id. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: P4-RESOLUTION-13: each station sees only its own candidate row of a recognition both were weighed for, and not the recognition while nobody chose ; apps/api/test/station-isolation.test.ts :: P4-ENGAGEMENTS-11: a station sees only its own taps and tap counters ; apps/api/test/station-isolation-matrix.test.ts :: S1-TESTS-13: station A's context cannot insert rows carrying station B's id ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: S1-ISOLATION-15: every station-owned table has a mandatory station_id ; apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-01: every link from the candidates, taps and counters to a station’s campaigns or cards carries the station, like the foundation tables.
- [x] **P4-DATABASE-02** The API role can only read the analytics tables added in Phase 4: as `lookup_api` with a station context, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `engagement_events`, `recognition_candidates` and `campaign_counters` are each refused with "permission denied". Their rows are written only through narrow SECURITY DEFINER functions in the `recognition` schema (claim, finalise, choice, engagement, limit reservation), which pin `search_path` and are executable only by `lookup_api`. *Source:* plan §Roles and RLS (ADR-002) "Analytics : read-only for app_api; writes only via SECURITY DEFINER recog.* functions" (line 388). *Verified by:* `database-privileges.test.ts`: the nine direct writes are denied; each definer function succeeds, has a pinned `search_path` and is executable only by `lookup_api` (S3-DATABASE-02, -03 extended). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-02: INSERT on engagement_events is refused to the API role; only the database functions write there ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-02: the API role cannot read the tap keys or the reports at all; only super admins read reports ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-02, ALL-DATABASE-06, -07: recognition.count_recognition_attempts(uuid, uuid) is a definer function with its search path pinned, run only by the API role, returning what it documents.
- [x] **P4-DATABASE-03** `engagement_events` and `recognition_candidates` grow with listener traffic, so each is range-partitioned by month on its arrival time, like `recognition_events`: partitions for the current month and future months plus a default partition, future months kept topped up, old months detached whole, and partitions reachable only through the parent table and its row-level-security policies (ALL-SCALING-07). *Source:* conventions §Scaling rules "Tables that grow with listener traffic are partitioned by month on their arrival time"; plan §Standing requirements "The database partitions high-volume event tables by month" (line 21). *Verified by:* `database-scaling.test.ts` extended: both tables are range-partitioned by month with current, future and default partitions; the API role cannot reach a partition directly. Which column is each table's arrival time is gap 47 (the P4 design answers it: the recognition's receipt time for candidates, the tap's arrival for engagements). How long recognitions, candidates, taps and reports are kept, and so when a month is detached, is deferred to P8 with O7 and the data-protection review (user's answer, 11 October 2026, P4 design Q13, P4-D37): the detach runbook is in ADR-003, and nothing is detached in P4. *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-03: candidates and taps are range-partitioned by month on their arrival columns: the recognition’s receipt, and the tap’s own ; apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: the scheduler can top up future months, and running it twice creates nothing new ; apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: the API role cannot reach a partition directly, around the parent table and its policies.
- [x] **P4-DATABASE-04** The API role reads `recognition_events` only through column grants that leave out `listener_install_id`, `hinted_station_id`, `idempotency_key`, `client_captured_at` and `clock_skew_milliseconds` (the listener, their "My station", the phone's own key, and a fingerprint of the phone's clock); `lookup_admin_api` still reads every column. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §4.5, P4-D30 (gap 38); ALL-DATABASE-04, ALL-PRIVACY-01. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts`: as `lookup_api`, `SELECT` of each of the five columns is refused with "permission denied", one case per column, and so is `SELECT *`; `id`, `station_id`, `outcome`, `resolution` and `captured_at` stay readable. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-04: the API role cannot read hinted_station_id of a recognition, even in its own station ; apps/api/test/database-privileges.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-04: so SELECT * is refused to the API role; the columns a station may see stay readable, and super admins read every column.
- [x] **P4-DATABASE-05** `engagement_events` has no foreign key to `recognition_events` (the function that records a tap checks the recognition itself), so a month of recognitions can be detached while taps of a later month name it. *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §4.2, §4.6, contract amendment A8, P4-D11; ALL-SCALING-01. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts`: with a tap in engagement month N+1 that names a recognition of month N, the recognitions' month N is detached after the candidates' month N and the tap stays; `apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts`: no foreign key from `engagement_events` to `recognition_events`. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/database-catalogue.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-05: taps and reports hold no foreign key to recognitions, so an old month of recognitions can always be detached ; apps/api/test/database-scaling.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-05, ALL-SCALING-01 (A8): a month of recognitions detaches after its month of candidates, in the order of ADR-003, while a tap of the next month that names one of them stays.
- [x] **P4-DATABASE-06** A lookup of a recognition by its id reads at most two monthly partitions: the receipt time is bounded by two immutable helpers that read the id's own time (`received_at >= …` and `received_at <= …`, never a range test, which PostgreSQL 16 does not use to prune partitions). *Source:* P4 design (docs/design/phase-4-station-resolution-and-engagements.md) §8.4, §4.4, P4-D32; ALL-SCALING-07. *Verified by:* `apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts`: the plan (`EXPLAIN`) of the lookup touches at most two partitions. Added on 11 October 2026 (P4-D36). *Evidence:* apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-06: a lookup between those bounds reads at most two monthly partitions, as written and as a prepared statement, where a range test reads them all ; apps/api/test/station-resolution-database.test.ts :: P4-DATABASE-06: every function that finds a recognition by its id alone uses those bounds, never a range test.

---

## Possible gaps in the sources (S3 and P4)

These are places where the sources are silent, vague or in conflict. Nothing below has been turned into a requirement; each needs a decision before or during the phase. The slice gap it comes from is named in brackets.

### Phase homes and build order

1. **Phase homes are judgement calls.** Near-full acoustic duplicates, partial overlap to NEEDS_REVIEW and re-index tooling sit in P8; the local in-flight limit, the deferred-clip rules and both k6 tests sit in P4; the breaker's half-open test and ingest retries with backoff sit in S3; the candidate filters and the window rule sit in S3 while grace sits in P4. The super-admin overlap review (P5A) has nothing to review until P8. The user should confirm these homes. (recognition gap 1; blueprint gap 19; data-model gap 30; cross-cutting gap 7)
2. **The k6 load tests have no targets and no clear home.** No target request rate or 95th-percentile latency is given, O14 waits for a "Phase 3 load test" that no phase names as a deliverable, and it is not stated whether the 4-processor build machine can run it or whether it belongs with the P8 tests on Render. (recognition gaps 1, 11; blueprint gap 18; cross-cutting gap 21)
3. **Repeated keys and capture time in Step 3.** No source says what Step 3 does with a repeated idempotency key before the P4 claim exists (replay, 409 or something else; it must not be a database error). Nor does any source say what Step 3 stores as `captured_at` before clock correction exists: the built check already enforces the 24-hour and 2-minute bounds, so a raw time from a phone with a wrong clock would fail the insert. (recognition gap 2; blueprint gap 25)
4. **Device-integrity verdict.** Resolved in review round 1: reading and storing are both S3 (S3-INSTALLS-10 to -12). The verdict values and what, if anything, a failed verdict changes are still not stated. (data-model gap 31)

### Contract and names

5. **Wire names against the naming rule.** The plan's request, job, worker-result and response names (`clip_ms`, `offset_ms`, `audioB64`, `fpVersion`, `asset_key`, `deferred`, `w`, `h`) break the newer "no abbreviations, units in the name, booleans as questions" rule, and no source gives the final names. The worker files are `match.py` and `ingest.py` in the master plan, while Step 3 calls the job `recognize`; the image commands are `match` and `ingest`. (recognition gap 3; blueprint gap 15; data-model gaps 10, 12)
6. **No station identifier in the response.** The result and candidate shapes give a station's name, frequency and logo only, and G9 calls the tenant id a leak, yet the choice endpoint, the engagements endpoint and the station hint all need a station identifier from the app. Review round 1 added the requirement for one public reference (S3-RESPONSE-17); whether it is the station id or an opaque candidate token must still be chosen. (recognition gap 4; blueprint gap 13; data-model gap 21)
7. **`result.kind` is undefined.** Its values are never listed, and no source says how a default-card result is marked to the app, although the stored outcome is `MATCH_DEFAULT` and the public `status` has only three values. (recognition gap 5; data-model gap 29)
8. **Images in the response.** How header-image and logo addresses are served from a private bucket (a public delivery path, or signed addresses and their lifetime) is not stated; replays and saved History entries need them to keep working. (recognition gap 30)
9. **Codec list and versions.** The allowed `codec` values are not listed; the edge accepts `OggS` while the app records AAC only; the behaviour when a job's fingerprint version differs from the worker's is not stated. (recognition gap 31)
10. **Contract tooling.** A JSON Schema for a multipart recognize request is unusual and its use is not explained; nothing says how the Dart and Python sides stay in step with the schemas beyond shared fixtures. (data-model gap 36)

### Errors and status codes

11. **Missing error details.** No code names for 413 and 415; no status or code for a clip length outside 2 to 15 seconds, a missing or malformed field, or a station hint naming an unknown station; whether `Retry-After` accompanies `RECOGNITION_TIMEOUT` and 429 is not stated. (recognition gap 6; blueprint gap 24)
12. **What counts as `INVALID_AUDIO`.** Which failures map to 400 `INVALID_AUDIO` (decode failure, no audio stream, too short after decoding, silence) and which to 415 or a server error is not listed. (recognition gap 7)
13. **Replaying a stored `INVALID_AUDIO`.** It is terminal, and terminal outcomes are "replayed with 200", yet the first answer was a 400. (recognition gap 8)
14. **Worker result values.** The worker's `status` values are not listed (match, no match, ambiguous, expired, error codes), nor what the API answers and stores when the worker returns `EXPIRED` or a lookup-timeout code. (recognition gap 9)
15. **The client acknowledgement rule is incomplete.** It covers 200, 422, `INVALID_AUDIO`, 409, 429, 503 and network errors, but not 401 (re-register and retry?), 413, 415, 426, other 400s or 500; the server's choice of codes affects it. (recognition gap 23)
16. **Refused requests.** Whether requests refused before the claim (401, 413, 415, 429) are recorded anywhere beyond the logs is not stated. (recognition gap 33)

### Numbers

17. **Engine numbers before the pilot.** T, R, the canonical sample rate, the hash-count tolerance and the final hash type were left to Phase 0, whose field testing D10 moved to the pilot. The engine's current values (20 aligned landmarks, margin 2, 11,025 hertz) are starting points; no source records the interim values to ship with before P8. (recognition gap 10; data-model gap 34; cross-cutting gap 13)
18. **Numbers not given at all.** The queue-depth threshold; the local in-flight limit; the breaker's failure threshold, reset time and half-open trial count; the heartbeat interval and expiry; the `Retry-After` value for live 503s (the Busy wireframe shows "Trying again in 12 s"); the "margin" in "2.5 seconds plus margin"; the presigned GET lifetime; the install-token lifetime; the per-address ceiling; the deferred priority value; and the ingest retry count and delays. The live limits (6 per minute, 60 per day) and the deferred limit (10 per hour) are examples only. (recognition gap 11; blueprint gap 18)
19. **256 kilobytes.** Not stated whether this is 256,000 or 262,144 bytes, or whether it limits the audio part or the whole multipart body. (recognition gap 12)

### Time rules and windows

20. **Time-bound details.** The 24-hour and 2-minute bounds are introduced under "Deferred:", but the database check applies to every row; whether live requests get the same 422 answers, whether each bound includes its exact instant, and how a missing, malformed or absurd `sent_at` (for example earlier than `captured_at`) is treated are not stated. Upload time on a slow network is counted as clock skew. (recognition gap 13)
21. **Which rate allowance.** Not stated which allowance a request uses when the app says live but the server computes deferred (or the reverse), or whether replays, 409 answers and retries after a 503 use up the allowance; at 6 per minute, automatic retries could lock a listener out. (recognition gap 14)
22. **Window edge rules.** Not stated: whether window and period boundaries are inclusive; which ISO day a midnight-crossing window belongs to after midnight (the data-model slice's test assumes the start day); behaviour across daylight-saving changes for stations outside Zambia; and whether equal start and end times mean "all day" (the built table refuses them). (recognition gap 29; blueprint gap 23)

### Resolution

23. **Candidate and owner definitions.** Step 8 filters candidates by ACTIVE station and campaign, then re-checks status "at `received_at`". Status keeps no history, so if candidates are filtered by current status the later check can never fail, and a campaign paused after capture (O13) would not be a candidate at all. "Owner" is never defined: whether it needs an ACTIVE campaign, a READY ad and an ad that is not archived is not stated. (recognition gap 15)
24. **Suspended station against the default-card rule.** Step 8 says a station no longer active at receipt gets "the default card"; the Phase 4 check and the baseline say a suspended station's card is never returned. This checklist assumes no card at all for a station that is not ACTIVE. (recognition gap 16)
25. **No default card in the full cascade.** Not stated what happens when the hinted owner has no default card, when some chooser candidates have none, or when a campaign is at its limit or paused and the client has no default card. (recognition gap 17)
26. **Chooser order and overflow.** With more than four in-window stations, which four are shown and in what order is not stated; "most recently aired first" has no definition without air-play data (latest window end, latest recognition, or something else). (recognition gap 18)
27. **Resolution values.** Not every case maps to one of the five values (default card by hint, default card for a single owner, chooser before a choice); the built `recognition_events` has no `resolution` column yet. (recognition gap 19)
28. **What an engagement limit counts, and when.** Step 8 "reserves the cap atomically" at recognition time; O4 and the race test count taps. Not stated whether the counter moves at recognition, at the tap or both, whether chooser candidates each reserve, or whether taps on a default card count. A money budget (blueprint "budget cap") is silently dropped. (recognition gap 20; blueprint gap 8)
29. **Out-of-window policy values.** The data model gives campaigns an `out_of_window` policy, but O3 states one fixed behaviour and no source lists other values. (recognition gap 28; data-model gap 18)

### Endpoints

30. **Choice endpoint details.** Not stated: whether the choice must come from the same install; the response body; the status and code for a non-candidate station, an unknown recognition or a recognition that was not a chooser; whether a second, different choice is refused or replaces the first; any time limit; its rate limit. (recognition gap 21)
31. **Engagement endpoint details.** Not stated: the response and status when a tap "does not count" (the app's outbox must know whether to drop or retry); whether `action_id` is checked against the card; whether a tap is allowed on a chooser recognition before a choice; its rate limit. `engagement_events` has no install, campaign or card column, and (resolved in review round 1 from the conventions) candidates and engagements are partitioned by month like `recognition_events` (P4-DATABASE-03). (recognition gap 22; data-model gap 22)
32. **Install registration details.** Not stated: the request body; the token's lifetime, renewal and revocation; what a BLOCKED install gets; how the server learns the app version for 426 `APP_UPDATE_REQUIRED`; a route-specific limit for `/installs`; rotation of the pepper and the signing key. (recognition gap 24)

### Ingest and data model

33. **Ingest details.** Not stated: whether the SHA-256 is of the uploaded file or of the canonical audio (re-encodes differ); how two simultaneous ingests of the same new file are settled; the list of ingest error codes; what happens to an ad stuck in PROCESSING while the worker is down; whether the uploaded object or a canonical copy is kept. The ingest job is created by Step 2's `complete` endpoint but its contents are specified only in Step 3, so the two steps must agree on the job shape. (recognition gap 25)
34. **"ffprobe".** The plan's ingest step names ffprobe, but the built engine decodes with PyAV's bundled libraries and the worker image installs no ffprobe binary; S3-INGEST-04 is written as "inspect before decoding". (recognition gap 26)
35. **Foundation and plan differ.** Step 1's text says the Foundation migration creates `fp.mark_ingested`, `recog.resolve` and `recog.record_recognition`, but the built migration leaves them to later steps. The built `recognition_events` has no `resolution`, `client_captured_at` or skew column, has a `fallback_reason` column the plan does not mention, and keeps an `idempotency_key` column although uniqueness moved to a separate table. `recognition_candidates`, `engagement_events` and `campaign_counters` do not exist; `audio_assets` lacks `canonical_object_key` and `fp_status`; `campaigns` has no out-of-window policy column. No source lists the migrations needed. (recognition gap 27; data-model gaps 13, 14, 20)
36. **Heartbeats with several workers.** Not stated whether one recognize heartbeat is enough for admission, whether the ingest worker has its own heartbeat, or what the portal shows when it is missing. (recognition gap 32)
37. **Retention and scheduled jobs.** The idempotency-key retry horizon is not stated (a migration comment says two days); no retention is given for recognition events, candidates or engagements; and the Render topology has no cron service to run the key clean-up or partition jobs. (data-model gap 23; cross-cutting gap 6)
38. **Listener identity in station data.** The built grants let the API role read every column of `recognition_events`, including the install reference; no source lists which event fields a station may see, so the promise that stations never learn who a listener is rests on response shaping alone. (blueprint gap 28)

### Engine, tooling and process

39. **Engine origin.** D9 says no Dejavu file is copied, while the plan's "Verified facts" and "Repo layout" still say "vendor and modernise" with an MIT attribution; whether the attribution (S3-ENGINE-17) is still owed is unclear. (blueprint gap 16; data-model gap 8)
40. **Python tooling.** The plan says uv; the repository README installs with venv and pip. (data-model gap 11)
41. **Module name drift.** The slice walk-through says `bench.synth`; the repository has `bench/synthetic_audio.py`. (recognition gap 35; cross-cutting gap 17)
42. **Leak patterns.** The plan's pattern list lacks "Fatal error", and the built scanner (`apps/api/test/support/internal-detail-patterns.ts`) lacks both "Fatal error" and `Traceback`; S3-TESTS-09 and S3-TESTS-19 need both. (cross-cutting gap 11; status-built.md shortfalls)
43. **Tests that cannot fail yet.** The Postgres index tests skip silently unless `LOOKUP_FINGERPRINT_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set, and there is no continuous integration before P8, so "blocking" tests (the cross-language contract test, the leak suite, the accuracy gate) run only by hand at each gate. (status-built.md shortfalls; cross-cutting gap 8)
44. **Compose contents.** End-to-end tests need MinIO, the API and the worker in compose; today's compose file has only Postgres and Valkey, and the machine limits may stop the full stack running locally. (cross-cutting gap 18)
45. **Open decisions treated as decided.** O3, O4, O6 and O10 to O14 are recommendations under "Open decisions"; this checklist treats them as decided because the plan is approved, but no source records the user confirming each one. (recognition gap 34; cross-cutting gap 25)
46. **Status snapshot.** `status-built.md` was taken at commit `318fa2c`. Since then `main` has gained `4e9409f` (portal sign-in), `9c310e1` (station access, profile, clients, audit trail), `5dfd86a` (ads with direct uploads), `ce8a7a7` (cards, schedules, publishing, seed), `8c69b85` (portal shell and client error reporting) and `e291d01` (log privacy, process failures). `status-built.md` must be regenerated from `main` with a fresh test run, and the ticks here re-checked, before the S3 gate.
47. **Partition columns for candidates and engagements.** The conventions require tables that grow with listener traffic to be partitioned by month on their arrival time (P4-DATABASE-03), but the plan's data model gives `recognition_candidates` no time column and `engagement_events` only `occurred_at` (clamped, so not the arrival time). The arrival column of each, and how the cross-month uniqueness table of P4-ENGAGEMENTS-12 is cleaned up, are not stated.
48. **Super-admin read path into the fingerprint schema.** G3 makes the `fingerprints` schema readable only by the worker role, while O6 and plan §B let super admins see shared-audio links and play both assets of a partial overlap. Whether `lookup_admin_api` gets a narrow SECURITY DEFINER function (returning the finding, the two asset numbers and short-lived preview keys) or a `SELECT` grant on the overlap table only is not decided; ADR-002 should record the choice (conflict 14).
49. **Decode caps for ingest.** Baseline §4 requires time and size caps on all audio decoding; the plan gives values only for listener clips (1-second decode, 256 KB). The time cap, the input-size cap (uploads may be 20 MB) and the decoded-length cap for ad uploads are not stated (S3-INGEST-24).
